<a id="13-french-ophelie-project-handoff-and-next-plan-2026-09md"></a>

# File: `13_FRENCH_OPHELIE_PROJECT_HANDOFF_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09.md`

# French OPHELIE / Jacoutot Reproduction
## New-computer handoff, verified status, and next development plan

> Updated: 2026-09-16  
> Repository: `Xiangyu-Hu/SPHinXsys`  
> Working branch: `feature/EM-Biot-Savart`  
> Verified handoff commit reported by the latest status pack: `d0096015e`  
> Purpose: restore the full project context on a new computer and give Cursor a precise continuation plan.  
> Scientific claim: **auditable SPH–EM–thermal–flow prototype with Fig. 4-like periodic coupling**, not yet a quantitative reproduction of the Jacoutot papers.

---

## 1. Project objective

The project aims to reproduce, as far as practical in SPHinXsys, two French Jacoutot/OPHELIE studies of molten glass heated by direct induction in a cold crucible:

1. **Natural-convection paper** — Jacoutot et al., *Chemical Engineering and Processing* 47 (2008): approximately **50 kW absorbed in the glass**, approximately **282 kHz** in the current reference setup.
2. **Mechanically stirred paper** — Jacoutot et al., *Chemical Engineering Science* 63 (2008): approximately **60 kW absorbed in the glass**, **300 kHz**, and a **10 rpm** stirrer.

The roughly **400 kW generator rating is not the absorbed glass power** and must never be used as the Joule-heating target.

The target physics chain is:

```text
multiloop coil
  → Biot–Savart A_coil
  → complex edge-flux A–φ solve
  → E and J reconstruction
  → Q = 0.5 σ |E|²
  → calibrated / controlled electromagnetic input
  → temperature-dependent material properties
  → SPH heat transfer + Boussinesq flow
  → optional 10 rpm mechanical stirring
  → periodic T → σ(T) → EM → Q feedback
```

The original French coupling iterates a 3D thermo-hydrodynamic calculation with a 2D axisymmetric OPHELIE electromagnetic calculation. The current SPHinXsys implementation reproduces the **data flow**, but still performs a **3D SPH EM solve** after azimuthally averaging conductivity. This distinction must remain explicit.

---

## 2. Long-standing decisions that must be preserved

These decisions were made during the earlier development and should not be silently changed by a new Cursor session.

### 2.1 Reference cases

| Item | Natural reference | Stirred reference |
|---|---:|---:|
| Glass absorbed-power target | 50 kW | 60 kW |
| Frequency | 282 kHz current reference | 300 kHz |
| Stirrer | none | 10 rpm |
| Production spacing currently used | 15 mm | 5 mm |
| Current vertical axis | +z | +z |
| Periodic A_ind default | off | off |

- The two cases use different relax/reload data. **Never mix their `Reload.xml` files.**
- The current production meshes are `glass-z.stl` / paddle `_z`; earlier CAD coordinates were different, but the current simulation vertical direction is **+z**, not y.
- The free surface remains open. The natural case uses a short splash-control rim, not a lid. The stirring case keeps `rim_height = 0`.

### 2.2 Electromagnetic development stages

1. Production baseline: complex **edge-flux** φ formulation with `A_coil`.
2. Validate local E/J/Q and power reconstruction.
3. Add or enable `A_ind` only as a separately identifiable option.
4. Cold-crucible metal carriers and `A_metal` are later work.

`A_ind=on` or Picard convergence does **not** by itself demonstrate physical validation.

### 2.3 Power control

The reference initial current is calibrated using reconstructed Joule power:

```text
I_new = I_old * sqrt(P_target / P_recon)
```

`P_recon` / `probe.joule_power_w` is the calibration quantity, not graph-Laplacian energy.

Two control modes are supported:

- `FixedCoilCurrent`: calibrate at the initial state, then lock current; absorbed power changes with σ(T). This is the preferred physical baseline unless the paper explicitly imposes constant absorbed power.
- `FixedAbsorbedPower`: recalibrate on each EM update. This is a sensitivity/control mode and must be labeled accordingly.

### 2.4 Scope constraints

Do not claim the following as paper physics:

- `--balance-heat-loss`;
- silent Q renormalization;
- power closure alone without local-field validation;
- one-shot or frozen Q as a full thermo–EM coupling;
- A_ind Picard convergence as validation;
- a relaxed skin-depth gate as accurate skin-depth reproduction.

Do not mix the RH200 customer geometry into the French reference cases. Reusing a generic Euler/CIC numerical helper is acceptable; reusing RH200 geometry or physics is not.

---

## 3. Historical development before the latest coupling work

The project evolved through the following main steps:

1. A reduced French EM case was created around a cylindrical glass domain and a parameterized seven-loop coil.
2. Biot–Savart `A_coil`, φ, E/J reconstruction, Joule heat, and absorbed-power calibration were connected.
3. The initial real formulation was replaced/extended by the complex edge-flux formulation.
4. Conservation and bookkeeping checks were added, including reconstructed power and alternative Joule-power expressions.
5. One-way Joule-to-heat, natural-convection frozen-Q, and mechanical-stirring cases were created.
6. Experimental self-induction / `A_ind` Picard paths were added.
7. Analytical validation cases were introduced for a cylinder, thin plate, skin depth, and French r–z probes.
8. Before September 2026, the closest stirred case was still one-shot EM → fixed Euler Q → SPH stirring and therefore did not reproduce the paper's iterative coupling architecture.

Before the latest work, the honest maturity assessment was approximately:

- EM functionality: reasonably advanced, but local-field trust incomplete;
- alignment with the stirred paper: partial;
- paper-grade quantitative reproduction: early stage.

---

## 4. Latest work inspected for this handoff

This document was prepared after reviewing:

- `10_THERMO_EM_COUPLING_ROUND1.md`;
- `11_THERMO_EM_COUPLING_AFTER_CHATGPT_TASK.md`;
- `12_THERMO_EM_COUPLING_CHATGPT_DISCUSSION_2026-09.md`.

The latest summaries are internally consistent on the major implementation and run results. The important update is that the periodic coupling architecture has been **implemented and run**, rather than merely proposed.

---

## 5. What is now implemented

### 5.1 Unified French parameters

`electromagnetic_ophelie_french_literature_parameters.h` now distinguishes the natural and stirred regimes and records unresolved literature parameters rather than silently guessing them.

Known open parameter differences include:

- natural frequency 282 kHz versus a nominal 300 kHz;
- natural glass height 0.185 m versus stirred height 0.21 m;
- density 2750 versus 2800 kg/m³ in different sources/code paths;
- σ at 1473 K: 16 S/m in the CEP-anchored natural setup versus approximately 24.75 S/m in the stirred IV.10 law;
- the exact paper definitions/characteristic lengths for Np and Nu.

These should remain labeled `OPEN_PARAMETER` until resolved from the journal papers or an authoritative source.

### 5.2 Shared periodic coupling controller

`electromagnetic_ophelie_thermo_em_coupling.h` implements:

- physical-time-based `shouldUpdateEM`;
- T → σ(T);
- azimuthal averaging to σ(r,z);
- empty-bin handling;
- conductivity under-relaxation;
- periodic EM re-solve;
- Q refresh on a fixed Euler grid;
- power bookkeeping;
- coupling and spatial-statistics CSV output.

The periodic data flow is now:

```text
t = 0:
  EM at reference σ
  → calibrate coil current
  → lock current in fixed-current mode
  → construct Euler Q grid

each SPH advection interval:
  sample lab-frame Q
  → Joule heating
  → thermal diffusion when enabled
  → boundary losses
  → Boussinesq / stirring dynamics

each physical EM update interval:
  particle T
  → σ(T)
  → azimuthally averaged σ(r,z)
  → under-relaxed EM conductivity
  → EM re-solve
  → refreshed Q(r,z) and Euler Q grid
```

### 5.3 Q-map bookkeeping

Silent rescaling is no longer the default in the stirring case. Conservative remapping is opt-in through `--q-conservative-remap` and records power before the correction, scale factor, and power afterward.

Power labels distinguish:

- generator power;
- target glass absorbed power;
- reconstructed glass power;
- coil current.

### 5.4 Thermal path

The periodic path now enables SPH internal conduction by default unless `--no-thermal-diffusion` is used. At present, k, cp, and μ are still constants evaluated around 1473 K; the full temperature-dependent laws are not yet active in the production coupled runs.

An energy CSV is written, but the desired 2–5% long-time residual is **not yet a validated pass gate**.

### 5.5 Natural-convection hydro stabilization

The natural case previously lost particles because an initially non-hydrostatic WCSPH melt was placed in an open cup with insufficient dummy-wall/rim support.

The implemented fix keeps a physically open top and adds:

- dummy-wall thickness factor 4;
- a short rim extending `6 dp` above the free surface;
- hydrostatic density initialization;
- an approximately 0.15 s gravity-only settling stage before Joule heat and Boussinesq are enabled;
- a reset of the physical clock after settling;
- containment checks based on escaped fraction and `U_max ≤ c0`.

This removed particle escape through the tested 50 s natural run.

### 5.6 Stirring coupling

The same controller is attached to the 10 rpm stirred case. Periodic runs with locked current and A_ind off were completed for 1 s, one revolution, and five revolutions.

### 5.7 Diagnostics for remote work

Because VTP files are large and slow to copy over SSH, compact diagnostics were added:

- `U_rms`, `U_th`, `U_z`;
- `T_std`;
- `T_out`, `T_ctr`;
- `T_bot`, `T_top`;
- coupling, energy, and spatial-statistics CSVs.

The `T_std = 0` cancellation bug was fixed by accumulating variance in `double` instead of float32.

---

## 6. Runs that have actually been completed

Unless otherwise stated, the periodic runs used:

```text
--thermo-em-coupling=periodic
--em-control=fixed-current
--aind=off
thermal diffusion on
no --balance-heat-loss
```

### 6.1 Natural case: 50 s

Configuration:

- natural relax/reload;
- `dp = 15 mm`;
- EM update interval 2 s;
- target initial absorbed power 50 kW.

Endpoint:

```text
passed = 1
escaped = 0 / 10474
coupling_updates = 25
coil current = 168.46 A, locked
P_joule: 50.00 → 46.46 kW
σ_mean: 16.00 → 14.67 S/m
T_mean: 1473 → 1443 K
T_std: 0.09 → 35.2 K
T_min / T_max: 1340 / 1496 K
U_rms ≈ 0.022 m/s
U_buoy endpoint scale ≈ 0.053 m/s
total loss ≈ 111 kW versus Joule input ≈ 46 kW
```

Interpretation:

- The periodic coupling and containment work.
- This run does **not** show a trustworthy Jacoutot natural-convection cell.
- `U_rms` is already approximately 0.02 m/s near the beginning and stays almost flat while ΔT and the buoyancy scale increase. Residual WCSPH/acoustic motion dominates the expected slow viscous buoyancy signal.
- The 15 mm grid does not resolve the cold wall boundary layer well. Therefore `T_out > T_ctr` in this run is not evidence that the physical wall layer is hotter; the outer diagnostic region is dominated by the induction-heating zone on a coarse mesh.
- Extending the same run in time is not expected to reveal the missing convection cell.

### 6.2 Stirred case: 30 s / five revolutions

Configuration:

- stirring relax/reload;
- `dp = 5 mm`;
- EM update interval 2 s;
- 10 rpm;
- target initial absorbed power 60 kW.

Endpoint:

```text
passed = 1
physical time = 30 s
revolutions = 5
coupling_updates = 15
coil current = 132.68 A, locked
P_joule: 60.00 → 54.81 kW
σ_mean: 24.75 → 23.18 S/m
U_tip ≈ 0.209 m/s
U_rms ≈ 0.08–0.13 m/s
T_mean: 1473 → 1453 K
T_out < T_ctr
T_top < T_bot
total loss ≈ 138 kW versus Joule input ≈ 55 kW
Re_imp ≈ 19
mechanical paddle power ≈ 0.4 W
```

Interpretation:

- The stirred Gate B-light architecture is demonstrated.
- Fixed-current feedback is visible: cooling lowers σ and absorbed power.
- Bulk stirring exists; `U_rms` and `U_th` are the useful measures.
- `U_max` is affected by contact/reload spikes and must not be interpreted as the mixing velocity.
- `T_out < T_ctr` is plausible on the finer 5 mm mesh because the diagnostic outer region includes the resolved cold wall boundary layer.
- The model cools too quickly to justify a 1000–1200 s paper comparison without skull/thermal-closure physics.

---

## 7. Current scientific status

| Gate | Meaning | Status after the latest work |
|---|---|---|
| Gate A | Local EM operator trust | Open. Cylinder/plate smoke tests exist, but skin-depth `J_rel ≈ 0.87` and French external probes remain unresolved. |
| Gate B-light | Periodic T → σ(T) → EM → Q in SPH | Closed as a prototype for natural and stirred cases. |
| Gate B-full | Paper-style 3D thermo ↔ 2D axisymmetric OPHELIE | Open. Current EM solve remains 3D. |
| Gate C | Quantitative paper figures, skull, Np/Nu, long-time state | Open. Thermal closure and local EM validation are insufficient. |

The latest work materially advances the project: items 1–5 and the stirred coupling item from the previous roadmap are complete. Natural-convection “validation” is complete only in the sense that the architecture and hydro containment work; it is not a literature flow-field validation.

---

## 8. Main blockers, ranked

### 8.1 Thermal closure — primary blocker

Both current simulations lose roughly two to three times as much heat as they receive electromagnetically. Without the insulating frozen-glass skull/crust represented in the French process, the melt cools rapidly, σ decreases, Joule power falls in fixed-current mode, and long-time comparison becomes meaningless.

This is now the main development bottleneck. It is more urgent than another 50 s natural run or a 1200 s stirred run.

### 8.2 Temperature-dependent thermal/fluid properties

The periodic path still uses constant k, cp, and μ around 1473 K. The literature reports strong temperature dependence, particularly viscosity and electrical conductivity. Constant μ can materially alter natural convection and stirred-flow resistance.

### 8.3 Natural-convection signal versus WCSPH residual

At `dp = 15 mm`, the expected slow, highly viscous natural-convection velocity is buried beneath approximately 2 cm/s residual motion. A finer grid alone may not solve this unless initialization, acoustic damping, viscosity, boundary layers, and diagnostic averaging are treated together.

### 8.4 Local EM-field validation

Power bookkeeping is good, but the plate skin-depth result still needs a relaxed `J_rel < 0.90` gate and has approximately 0.87 error. The French 30-point r–z probe set still lacks an OPHELIE/COMSOL reference.

### 8.5 Missing paper observables

Paper-grade Np, Nu, torque/heat-transfer definitions, skull contour, and figure-to-figure comparisons are not yet complete.

---

## 9. Recommended primary next step

### Primary direction: physically interpretable thermal closure

The next main development should replace the current excessive direct wall/free-surface losses with a physically interpretable skull/thermal-resistance model. Do **not** merely tune `--balance-heat-loss` until loss equals the input power.

Implement this in two levels.

#### Level 1 — effective fixed skull resistance

Introduce an explicit, documented series thermal resistance at the cooled wall/bottom:

```text
R_total = delta_skull / k_skull + 1 / h_cool
q_wall = (T_melt - T_cool) / R_total
```

Requirements:

- `delta_skull`, `k_skull`, `h_cool`, and `T_cool` must be explicit inputs;
- use literature values where available;
- if thickness is uncertain, run a declared sensitivity range instead of fitting one hidden scale factor;
- keep side, bottom, and free-surface contributions separate;
- preserve the raw `--balance-heat-loss` knob only as an engineering legacy option, never as the French reproduction default;
- record instantaneous and cumulative heat losses through every boundary group;
- add a zero-dimensional energy-balance regression before long 3D runs.

This level is an effective boundary model, not a resolved skull shape. Label it accordingly.

#### Level 2 — explicit fixed solid skull shell

After Level 1 is stable, add a stationary solid/frozen-glass shell with conduction and no fluid motion. The shell can initially have a prescribed thickness and material properties. This provides a bridge toward a later temperature-evolving skull without immediately implementing full phase change.

Do not begin dynamic solidification until the fixed shell closes the heat budget and the interface flux is verified.

---

## 10. Material-property work to accompany thermal closure

Implement and verify the production use of the literature laws for:

- σ(T);
- μ(T), highest priority for natural and stirred flow;
- k(T);
- cp(T);
- optionally density/thermal expansion where supported by the chosen Boussinesq model.

Development requirements:

1. One authoritative helper should evaluate all French material properties.
2. Every law must state source, units, valid temperature range, and clamping behavior.
3. CSV output should record min/mean/max for σ, μ, k, and cp.
4. Add unit tests at lower bound, 1473 K, and upper bound.
5. Add a constant-property compatibility mode for regression.
6. Do not use a thesis value when it conflicts with a journal value without recording the choice in `OPEN_PARAMETER`.

Recommended sequencing:

```text
energy accounting audit
  → μ(T), k(T), cp(T) activation
  → effective fixed-skull resistance
  → short natural and stirred verification
  → explicit fixed skull shell
```

---

## 11. Natural-convection plan after thermal closure

Do not simply rerun the existing 15 mm case for longer. Use the following decision process:

### 11.1 First estimate the expected physical velocity

Using the actual μ(T), β, ΔT, geometry, and boundary conditions, estimate Ra, Pr, and a literature-scale velocity. Compare that velocity with the measured residual `U_rms` after the gravity-settling stage.

### 11.2 Add diagnostics that separate flow from noise

Prefer:

- time-averaged meridional velocity in r–z bins;
- signed streamfunction or circulation measure;
- axisymmetric mean `U_r` and `U_z` rather than speed only;
- kinetic energy before and after Boussinesq activation;
- a zero-buoyancy control using identical initialization;
- longer temporal averaging after acoustic transients.

`U_max` and instantaneous `U_rms` alone are inadequate for identifying a slow convection roll.

### 11.3 Decide whether to create a finer natural relax

Only start a new 5–7.5 mm natural relaxation campaign if:

- the expected physical velocity is not orders of magnitude below the residual floor;
- the temperature-dependent viscosity and thermal boundary model are active;
- the coarse-grid wall layer is demonstrably inadequate;
- the new run has predefined convergence observables.

If those criteria fail, honestly declare quantitative natural-cell reproduction out of the current midterm scope rather than spending GPU time on an uninformative fine mesh.

---

## 12. EM validation track to run in parallel

This is a parallel science track, not a reason to stop the thermal-closure implementation.

### 12.1 Skin-depth case

Investigate:

- total versus scattered-field comparison;
- complex harmonic sign convention;
- `A_real/A_imag` phase treatment;
- peak versus RMS conventions;
- open-boundary truncation;
- thickness-direction resolution;
- boundary gradient reconstruction;
- interior-only versus full-domain norms.

Report:

- full-domain J error;
- interior J error;
- magnitude error;
- phase error;
- one-dimensional through-thickness profiles.

### 12.2 French probe reference

Obtain an external 2D axisymmetric COMSOL/OPHELIE reference using exactly the same geometry, frequency, coil, σ, and amplitude convention. Compare at least:

- A or Aθ;
- E/J amplitude and phase;
- Q;
- integrated glass power;
- peak-Q position.

Suggested eventual targets, to be confirmed against discretization limits:

- integrated power error below approximately 2%;
- interior Q L2 error around 10–15% or better;
- peak position within one local particle spacing;
- principal probe amplitude errors around 10–20% or better.

Do not invent external reference values when none are available.

### 12.3 A_ind

After the baseline local-field comparison is credible:

- compare `A_ind=off` and `A_ind=on` at identical locked coil current;
- record changes in power, Q peak, radial penetration, phase, and solver convergence;
- only then enable periodic `A_ind=on` production campaigns.

---

## 13. Stirring and paper observables

The existing five-revolution result is sufficient evidence for a Gate B-light prototype. Do not extend to 1000–1200 s until the heat budget is physically closed.

After thermal closure, add/verify:

- paddle torque and mechanical power;
- the exact paper Power number definition;
- the exact paper Nusselt number definition;
- mean/max and component velocity profiles;
- temperature standard deviation and mixing metric;
- r–z or sectional Q, T, and velocity fields at paper-relevant times;
- comparison between unstirred and 10 rpm cases;
- fixed-current and fixed-power sensitivity runs, clearly distinguished.

Mechanical power currently appears tiny compared with EM heating. That may be physically plausible for slow viscous mixing, but torque and characteristic-diameter conventions must be audited before comparison with paper Np.

---

## 14. Explicitly deferred work

Unless a new project decision is made, keep the following out of the immediate development round:

- segmented copper cold-crucible carriers;
- `A_metal`;
- full dynamic phase-change skull;
- TEAM7 as a blocking French gate;
- RH200 demo polish;
- SPHinXsim/JSON interface refactoring;
- Python or web API work;
- 1000–1200 s production runs without physical thermal closure;
- presentation-only VTP campaigns over SSH.

These may become later phases, but they should not displace the current French-physics bottleneck.

---

## 15. Proposed next development rounds

### Round 3A — energy and material audit

Deliverables:

1. Trace every energy term and its sign/area/volume integration.
2. Verify cumulative versus instantaneous energy bookkeeping.
3. Activate and test μ(T), k(T), and cp(T).
4. Preserve constant-property regression mode.
5. Add a simple uniform-heating/conduction energy test.

Acceptance:

- no NaN/Inf;
- no hidden normalization;
- all boundary heat flows have explicit sign conventions;
- energy mismatch is characterized over long windows, not instantaneous acoustic steps;
- material values at reference temperatures match the chosen source.

### Round 3B — effective fixed-skull resistance

Deliverables:

1. Side/bottom skull-resistance boundary model.
2. Explicit parameters and literature citations/comments.
3. Sensitivity cases for skull thickness/conductivity.
4. Short natural and stirred periodic runs.

Acceptance:

- boundary-flux unit/regression tests pass;
- the model reduces excessive cooling without a hidden global scale;
- energy accounting closes to an agreed long-time tolerance;
- predicted behavior varies monotonically and physically with skull thickness and conductivity.

### Round 3C — natural-flow detectability decision

Deliverables:

1. Ra/Pr and expected-velocity estimate with μ(T).
2. zero-buoyancy control;
3. time-averaged r–z meridional-flow diagnostic;
4. written go/no-go decision for a finer natural mesh.

Acceptance:

- a convection roll is demonstrated above the control/noise floor, or the limitation is documented and quantitative natural-cell reproduction is deferred.

### Round 3D — stirred comparison after heat closure

Deliverables:

1. 5–20 revolution periodic coupled runs with physical thermal closure;
2. torque, mechanical power, Np candidate, Nu candidate;
3. temperature uniformity and field comparisons;
4. locked-current baseline plus optional fixed-power sensitivity.

Acceptance:

- no rapid unphysical bulk cooling;
- coupled power/temperature evolution is interpretable;
- metrics are defined consistently with the paper or explicitly labeled as SPH-specific alternatives.

### Parallel Round A — EM field trust

Deliverables:

1. skin-depth error decomposition;
2. external French r–z reference;
3. A_ind off/on comparison.

Acceptance:

- power and local-field comparisons are both documented;
- production choices are based on measured error, not only convergence.

## 16. New-computer restoration checklist

### 16.1 Source checkout

```bash
git clone git@github.com:Xiangyu-Hu/SPHinXsys.git
cd SPHinXsys
git fetch origin
git switch feature/EM-Biot-Savart
git pull --ff-only
git rev-parse --short HEAD
```

Expected handoff commit from the latest summary:

```text
d0096015e
```

If the branch has advanced, preserve the newer commit but compare its changes against this handoff point.

### 16.2 Build environment

Recreate the existing SPHinXsys build configuration used on the old machine. Confirm compiler, CMake options, dependency paths, GPU/SYCL backend, and release/debug mode before comparing results. Do not interpret numerical differences until the build modes are comparable.

### 16.3 Required generated data

Check whether the following reload directories exist after checkout:

```text
test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_glass_relax/bin/reload
test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_glass_relax/bin/reload
```

Generated reload files may not be committed. If missing, regenerate them with the matching geometry and spacing:

- natural: `dp = 0.015 m`;
- stirring: `dp = 0.005 m`.

Never use the natural reload for stirring or the stirring reload for natural convection.

### 16.4 Minimum compile targets

```bash
cmake --build . --target \
  test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q \
  test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em \
  -j$(nproc)
```

Use the repository's established Ninja/CMake invocation if the build directory was configured differently.

### 16.5 Minimum migration verification

Do not immediately repeat the 50 s or 30 s campaigns. First perform:

1. frozen-Q regression;
2. short natural periodic run with at least 2–3 real EM refreshes;
3. constant-σ periodic check;
4. short stirred periodic run;
5. compare coil current, initial power, particle counts, coupling updates, and absence of NaN/escaped particles against this document.

Only after the short baselines match should longer development runs begin.

### 16.6 Files worth transferring from the old computer

Prefer compact inputs and diagnostic outputs:

- relax/reload XML/data if regeneration is expensive;
- `french_thermo_em_coupling.csv`;
- `french_energy_budget.csv`;
- `french_spatial_stats.csv`;
- `french_sigma_rz.csv`;
- `french_q_rz.csv`;
- important run command logs;
- any external COMSOL/OPHELIE probe reference when created.

Do not prioritize copying large VTP sequences unless they contain a unique result that cannot be regenerated.

### 16.7 Copyright

Do not commit the Elsevier Jacoutot PDF to the public repository. Keep bibliographic information and derived parameter tables in the repo, but store the copyrighted paper privately.

```text
tests/.../test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_glass_relax/
tests/.../test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q/

```

Use `rg --files` and `rg` to resolve the exact common-prefix path after checkout; the summaries abbreviate some paths as `tests/...`.

---

## 18. Instructions for the new Cursor session

Before editing code:

1. Read this handoff document.
2. Read documents 09–12 listed above.
3. Inspect the current branch and compare HEAD with `d0096015e`.
4. Confirm that the unified controller and both periodic case connections still exist.
5. Confirm current defaults rather than relying on historical text.
6. Report any disagreement between code and documentation before changing behavior.

For the next implementation round, focus first on **Round 3A: energy and temperature-dependent material audit**. Do not begin `A_metal`, segmented crucible, dynamic phase change, or long 1000 s runs.

When finished, report:

- files changed;
- physical equations and sign conventions;
- material-law sources and units;
- compile/test commands and results;
- energy-budget results;
- regression results for constant-property/frozen-Q modes;
- remaining limitations;
- whether the code is ready for Round 3B fixed-skull resistance.

---

## 19. Recommended claim for reports and discussions

Use:

> We have implemented an auditable SPH-based Fig. 4-like thermo–electromagnetic coupling prototype for the French molten-glass cases. Temperature-dependent electrical conductivity is azimuthally averaged and periodically fed back to the electromagnetic solver, with reconstructed Joule heat remapped to the three-dimensional SPH flow. The loop has been demonstrated for natural-convection geometry and for five revolutions of a 10 rpm stirrer. Quantitative Jacoutot reproduction remains open because skull-mediated thermal closure, full temperature-dependent thermal/rheological properties, local electromagnetic-field validation, and paper-specific Np/Nu comparisons are not yet complete.

Do not claim full OPHELIE reproduction or quantitative agreement with the Jacoutot figures at the present stage.

---

## 20. Immediate next action

The first task on the new computer should be:

> Restore and verify the short periodic natural/stirring baselines at the handoff commit, then audit the complete energy equation and activate verified μ(T), k(T), and cp(T) laws in the common French material path. Only after that should an explicit effective fixed-skull thermal resistance be added.

This sequence preserves the working Gate B-light architecture and attacks the present physical bottleneck without expanding prematurely into the full segmented cold-crucible EM problem.

<a id="14-round3a-energy-and-material-auditmd"></a>

# File: `14_ROUND3A_ENERGY_AND_MATERIAL_AUDIT.md`

# Round 3A — Energy audit and μ(T)/k(T)/cp(T) activation (2026-09-16)

> Implements the first development round from [`13_FRENCH_OPHELIE_PROJECT_HANDOFF_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09.md`](13_FRENCH_OPHELIE_PROJECT_HANDOFF_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09.md) §15.  
> Branch: `feature/EM-Biot-Savart`  
> Claim level unchanged: auditable Gate B-light prototype, not Jacoutot quantitative reproduction.

---

## 1. Energy equation bookkeeping (signs)

Paper (CES 2008 Eq. 3, stirred; CEP natural analogous):

```text
ρ₀ ∂(cp T)/∂t + ρ₀ ∇·(cp T v) = ∇·(λ ∇T) + Q_th
```

SPH prototype (periodic path):

| Term | Code | Sign / notes |
|------|------|----------------|
| Stored thermal energy | Constant cp: `E = Σ ρ cp T V`. Variable cp: **sensible enthalpy** `H = Σ ρ V ∫_{T₀}^T cp` (Batch C / doc 26). Legacy `Σ ρ cp_i T_i V` is inconsistent with `T += Q dt/(ρ cp)` | Reference for `dE/dt` |
| Joule source | `Q` from EM / Euler grid sample | Positive heating; `T += Q dt /(ρ cp)` |
| Conduction | Pairwise Laplace with harmonic-mean **k** in the pair weights, then `T += dt (−1/(ρ cp)) L` | See §2 fix |
| Side / bottom / free losses | Robin + radiation via boundary shell | Positive = heat leaving the melt |
| Residual (diagnostic) | `dE/dt − (P_joule − wall − free − bottom)` | Not a pass gate yet |

Boundary heat-loss powers from `hostOphelieThermalFrenchNaturalHeatLossPowers` are **outward** fluxes (W). Free surface is split into convection + radiation in the monitor, summed into the free-surface CSV column.

---

## 2. Critical fix: diffusion no longer double-counts k

**Finding.** `OpheliePairwiseLaplaceCK` already multiplies pair weights by the harmonic mean of the particle conductivity field. `ApplyLaplaceDiffusionDtCK` / the combined one-way step previously also multiplied by `k/(ρ cp)`.

- MMS used `k = 1`, so the bug was invisible.
- French production `k = 4` would have made the effective diffusivity **16/(ρ cp)** instead of **4/(ρ cp)**.

**Fix.** Time update uses only `1/(ρ cp)` against the conductivity-weighted Laplace. Deprecated constructor overloads that still take `k` ignore that argument.

---

## 3. Material laws

### 3.1 Sources

| Property | Production source | Clamp / range |
|----------|-------------------|---------------|
| σ(T) | Unchanged: natural journal-anchored / stirred thesis IV.10 | Existing |
| μ(T), cp(T), k(T) | **CES 2008 Fig. 2 NUM visual digitization** (`OPEN_PARAMETER`) anchored at Table-1 @ 1473 K | Table clamps at [300, 1800] K |

Digitized tables live in `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_material_laws.h` via `installJacoutotCes2008Fig2ThermalRheology`. Replace if an authoritative closed-form NUM fit is obtained.

### 3.2 Modes

| CLI | Behavior |
|-----|----------|
| default / `--constant-thermal-rheology` | Evaluate μ, cp, k at `T₀` only (Round-1 regression) |
| `--temperature-dependent-properties` | Particle `k`/`cp` follow `T`; body `Viscosity` refreshed from volume-mean `μ(T)` |

`verifyFrenchMaterialLawsAtReferenceTemperatures` checks Table-1 anchors @ 1473 K and a cooling→heating μ trend.

### 3.3 Limitations (honest)

- Body-level `Viscosity` is **volume-mean**, not a full particle-variable viscous CK model.
- Paper energy form uses `∂(cp T)/∂t`; we still use `cp ∂T/∂t` (or particle `cp_i` in the heating kernel) without the `T ∂cp/∂t` term.
- Free-surface BC coefficients remain literature defaults; skull is Round 3B.

---

## 4. Files touched

- `src/shared/materials/viscosity.h` — `setReferenceViscosity`
- `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_material_laws.h` — Fig. 2 laws, rheology fields, host update, particle-cp heating
- `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_literature_parameters.h` — CLI + source logging
- `electromagnetic_ophelie_thermo_em_coupling.h` — energy CSV gains μ/cp/k stats
- `electromagnetic_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_one_way.h` — double-k fix
- `test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q.cpp`
- `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em.cpp`

---

## 5. Verification (when GPU is available)

```bash
# Constant-property regression (default)
./test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q ... --thermo-em-coupling=periodic --end-time=0.5

# Temperature-dependent thermal/rheology
./test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q ... --thermo-em-coupling=periodic \
  --temperature-dependent-properties --end-time=0.5

# Thermal diffusion MMS (k=1 path; should still pass)
./test_3d_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_mms
```

Expect: finite fields, `material_check passed=1`, energy CSV columns for μ/cp/k populated, no NaN.

---

## 6. Ready for Round 3B?

Code-side: **yes to start** effective fixed-skull resistance once short GPU baselines confirm the diffusion fix and material wiring.

Still deferred: dynamic skull, `A_metal`, 1000 s runs, Gate A skin-depth closure.

<a id="15-round3b-skull-resistance-level1md"></a>

# File: `15_ROUND3B_SKULL_RESISTANCE_LEVEL1.md`

# Round 3B Level 1 — Effective fixed skull resistance (2026-09-17)

> Follows [`13_FRENCH_OPHELIE_PROJECT_HANDOFF_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09.md`](13_FRENCH_OPHELIE_PROJECT_HANDOFF_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09.md) §9 / §15 Round 3B.  
> This is an **effective boundary model**, not a resolved skull shape.

---

## 1. Model

### 1a. Cooled side and bottom walls (Level-1)

```text
R_total = delta_skull / k_skull + 1 / h_cool
h_eff   = 1 / R_total
q_wall  = h_eff (T_melt − T_cool)
```

### 1b. Free-surface crust companion (Level-1b)

Conduction through crust thickness in series with surface convection + radiation.
Outer face temperature `T_s` solves:

```text
(T_melt − T_s) / (δ/k) = h_free (T_s − T_a) + εσ (T_s⁴ − T_ar⁴)
q_free = (T_melt − T_s) / (δ/k)
```

Convection-only free crust (no radiation in the surface balance) barely changes the budget;
radiation must be included at `T_s`.

| Parameter | Default | Notes |
|-----------|---------|-------|
| `delta` side/bottom | 5 mm | OPEN_PARAMETER; paper says mm–cm |
| `k_skull` / `k_crust` | 1 W/(m·K) | Low-T Fig. 2-like frozen glass |
| `h_cool` side/bottom | existing production `h_side`/`h_bottom` (300 / 35) | Coolant-side before skull |
| free-crust `delta` | 5 mm | OPEN_PARAMETER |

`--balance-heat-loss` remains legacy and is **rejected** when combined with `--skull-resistance`.

---

## 2. CLI

```text
--skull-resistance
--skull-delta=0.005          # both faces
--skull-delta-side=...
--skull-delta-bottom=...
--skull-k=1.0
--skull-h-cool-side=300
--skull-h-cool-bottom=35
--skull-t-cool=300

--free-crust-resistance
--free-crust-delta=0.005
--free-crust-k=1.0
```

---

## 3. Code

- `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_skull_resistance.h` — formula, 0D budget, regression, CLI
- `frenchFreeSurfaceHeatFlux` in `electromagnetic_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_one_way.h` — Newton `T_s`
- Wired into `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em` and `test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q`

Startup always runs `verifyFrenchSkullResistanceRegression` (monotonic `h_eff`, 0D loss reduction, free-crust reduction).

---

## 4. δ sensitivity (stirring short smoke @ T≈T0, P=60 kW)

| δ side/bottom | h_eff side | T0 total loss (SPH) | notes |
|---------------|------------|---------------------|-------|
| 0 (bare) | 300 | ~149 kW | free rad ~43 kW |
| 2 mm | 187.5 | ~113 kW | |
| 5 mm | 120 | ~91 kW | default Level-1 |
| 20 mm | 42.9 | ~65 kW | free surface still ~47 kW |

Free radiation on a bare free surface dominates the remaining imbalance after thick skulls.

**Stirring short smoke (δ_skull=δ_crust=5 mm, k=1, 0.5 s):**

| Path | free_conv + free_rad @ T0 | total loss @ T0 | vs P=60 kW |
|------|---------------------------|-----------------|------------|
| skull only | ~47.5 kW | ~91 kW | over-cooled |
| skull + free crust | ~15.6 kW | ~59.2 kW | near balance |

Level-1b free crust cools outer `T_s` (~1084 K at melt 1473 K) so radiation drops sharply.

---

## 5. Example (stirring, after build)

```bash
./test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em \
  --reload-dir=../../test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_glass_relax/bin/reload \
  --thermo-em-coupling=periodic --em-update-interval=0.5 \
  --em-control=fixed-current --aind=off \
  --skull-resistance --skull-delta=0.005 \
  --free-crust-resistance --free-crust-delta=0.005 \
  --end-time=1 --no-state-recording
```

Sensitivity (declared, not hidden fit): `--skull-delta=0.002`, `0.005`, `0.010`, `0.020`.

---

## 6. Explicit non-claims

- Not Jacoutot Fig. 7 skull geometry
- Not a resolved free-surface crust mesh
- Not dynamic solidification (Level 2 / later)
- 0D `loss/P` near 1 is not required for Level-1 acceptance; free radiation alone can remain large without crust

<a id="17-round3b-level2-fixed-skull-shell-planmd"></a>

# File: `17_ROUND3B_LEVEL2_FIXED_SKULL_SHELL_PLAN.md`

# Round 3B Level-2 — Fixed solid skull shell (plan + MVP status)

> Follows handoff [`13_...`](13_FRENCH_OPHELIE_PROJECT_HANDOFF_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09.md) §9 Level 2.  
> Prerequisite: Level-1/1b verified in [`15_...`](15_ROUND3B_SKULL_RESISTANCE_LEVEL1.md) + [`16_...`](16_ROUND3B_SKULL_CRUST_SHORT_VERIFICATION.md).

---

## 1. Goal

Replace Level-1 **effective** side/bottom series resistance with a **stationary solid** `WallBoundary` that conducts heat, contacts the melt, and is cooled on the outer face.

Not Jacoutot Fig. 7; not dynamic solidification.

---

## 2. Architecture (natural MVP)

| Piece | Implementation |
|-------|----------------|
| Shell body | Existing hydro `WallBoundary` |
| Melt ↔ shell | `DiffusionRelaxationCK` Inner+Contact Dirichlet (RH200 pattern) |
| Shell outer | Dirichlet `T_cool` on outer band |
| Free surface | Optional Level-1b `--free-crust-resistance` |
| Mutual exclusion | `--skull-shell` rejects `--skull-resistance` |
| Coarse-mesh `k_eff` | `k_eff = k_skull * wall_thickness / delta` so `R = delta/k_skull` |
| Wall T init | Linear melt→cool profile |

---

## 3. Contact heat-flux audit (2026-09-18)

Host sum of DiffusionRelaxation transfer fields:

```text
TemperatureTransferWithGlassBody   on melt  →  P_melt = Σ transfer·V
TemperatureTransferWithWallBoundary on wall →  P_wall = Σ transfer·V
melt_loss_W = −P_melt   (heat leaving melt)
wall_gain_W =  P_wall
```

Wired into mid-run residual (Contact replaces Robin side/bottom) and final summary.

Code: `hostFrenchSkullShellContactHeatPowers` in `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_skull_shell.h`.

---

## 4. Smoke results

### 4a. Natural 1 s (Contact audit first enable)

| Metric | Result |
|--------|--------|
| contact_melt_loss_W | ~27.5 kW |
| contact_wall_gain_W | ~27.5 kW |
| free (conv+rad) | ~12.7 kW |
| total audited | ~40.3 kW vs P ~50.0 kW |
| passed | 1 |

### 4b. Natural 10 s (user-run, 2026-09-18)

```text
contact audit: melt_loss_W=34996.8 wall_gain_W=34920.6
free_W=12755.8 total_W=47752.6 vs P=50413.3
escaped_frac=0 passed=1
T_mean≈1474.9 K  dT≈22.9 K
```

| Metric | 1 s | 10 s |
|--------|-----|------|
| contact melt / wall | 27.5 / 27.5 kW | **35.0 / 34.9 kW** |
| free | 12.7 kW | 12.8 kW |
| total / P | 40.3 / 50.0 (**0.80**) | **47.8 / 50.4 (0.95)** |
| P − total | ~9.8 kW | **~2.7 kW (5%)** |

Contact melt↔wall stays consistent; free surface nearly flat. Total loss approaches joule power over the 10 s window (slow residual heating, `T_mean` +1.9 K). Coarse `dp=0.015` Level-2 is **acceptable as a closure trend**, not yet Level-1-tight at T0.
---

## 5. CLI

```text
--skull-shell
--skull-shell-delta=0.005
--skull-shell-k=1.0
--skull-shell-t-cool=300
```

Stirring: CLI accepted but run still refused (natural MVP first).

---

## 6. Status / next

| Step | Status |
|------|--------|
| Contact path + outer cool | **done** |
| Contact heat-flux host audit | **done** |
| Natural 10 s energy window | **done** (total/P → 0.95) |
| Finer dp resolving δ=5 mm | deferred |
| Stirring WallBoundary thermal | deferred |
| Round 3C Ra/Pr detectability | **no-go** finer mesh (doc 19; buoy≈zero control) |

**Production thermal closure** for stirring: Level-1 `--skull-resistance` + `--free-crust-resistance` (doc 16/20). Round 3D 10 rev locked-current **passed** (`Np_sph`/`Nu_sph` logged). Stirring `--skull-shell` still not wired.
---

## 7. Explicit non-claims

- Not Fig. 7 geometry / phase change
- Not bit-identical to Level-1 loss split at `dp=0.015`
- Contact audit is instantaneous transfer×Vol; not a long-window cumulative flux integral

<a id="19-round3c-natural-flow-detectabilitymd"></a>

# File: `19_ROUND3C_NATURAL_FLOW_DETECTABILITY.md`

# Round 3C — Natural-flow detectability (after Level-2 thermal path)

> Date: 2026-09-18  
> Case: `test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q`  
> Depends on: docs 15–18 (skull / free-crust / Contact audit)
>
> **Supersession (2026-09-30, Doc77):** the historical “finer natural mesh **no-go**” below records only the 5 s dp=15 mm dual-run floor. It is **not** a proof that SPH cannot solve natural convection. Client priority is now natural; mesh refine is gated by Doc77 A/B/C foundation tests — do not repeat “natural no-go” as current policy.

## Goal

Decide whether a finer natural mesh is worth GPU time: is a buoyancy-driven meridional roll **detectable above** the WCSPH residual floor at `dp = 15 mm`?

## Deliverables (this round)

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| Ra/Pr + viscous/inertial U scales with μ and measured ΔT | **wired** (`[ophelie][natural-ra]`) |
| Zero-buoyancy control CLI | **wired** (`--zero-buoyancy` / `--no-boussinesq`; mid-loop Boussinesq gated) |
| Time-averaged r–z meridional `U_r`/`U_z` | **wired** (`output/french_meridional_ur_uz_mean.csv`, `[ophelie][meridional]`) |
| Written go/no-go for finer mesh | **done — no-go** (5 s dual run 2026-09-18) |

## 5 s dual-run result (skull-shell + free-crust, dp=15 mm)

| Metric | Boussinesq on | Zero buoyancy | Δ |
|--------|---------------|---------------|---|
| `U_merid_mean` | 0.015043 | 0.015041 | ~0.000002 |
| `circ_proxy` | −0.003832 | −0.003819 | ~0.000013 |
| `U_rms_inst_avg` | 0.02171 | 0.02174 | ~0 |
| end `dT` | 11.27 K | 11.26 K | ~0 |
| `passed` | 1 | 1 | — |

Logs: `build/nat_detect_buoy_5s.log`, `build/nat_detect_zero_5s.log`.

**Decision: no-go for a finer natural relax campaign.**

- Scale estimate alone said `U_visc/U_noise ~ 1.2` (optimistic), but the **control twin** shows meridional mean and circulation proxy are **indistinguishable** from residual WCSPH motion.
- Instantaneous `U_rms ~ 2.2 cm/s` from t≈0 in both runs; buoyancy does not grow a detectable roll in 5 s.
- Quantitative Jacoutot natural-cell reproduction is deferred at this midterm scope. Keep natural as architecture / Level-2 energy-closure smoke only.

## How to read the diagnostics

1. **`U_viscous ~ g β ΔT H² / ν`** — expected creeping buoyancy speed at high Pr.  
2. **`U_noise_hint` / `U_rms_inst_avg`** — residual floor (~2 cm/s historically).  
3. **`verdict=`** on `[natural-ra]`:
   - `buried_defer_finer_mesh` if `U_visc/U_noise < 0.2`
   - `marginal` if `0.2–1`
   - `signal_may_exceed_noise` if `≥ 1`
4. **Paired runs**: same flags except buoyancy on vs `--zero-buoyancy`. Compare:
   - `U_merid_mean` (speed of **time-averaged** meridional field)
   - `circ_proxy` (volume mean of `U_z · (2r/R − 1)`)
   - Instantaneous `U_rms` alone is **not** decisive.

## Dual-run commands (GPU machine)

```bash
source /opt/intel/oneapi/setvars.sh
cd /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build
ninja test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q

BIN=/home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q/bin
RELOAD=../../test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_glass_relax/bin/reload
COMMON="--reload-dir=$RELOAD --thermo-em-coupling=periodic --em-update-interval=0.5 \
  --aind=off --target-absorbed-power=50000 \
  --skull-shell --skull-shell-delta=0.005 \
  --free-crust-resistance --free-crust-delta=0.005 \
  --end-time=5 --meridional-average-start=0.5 --no-state-recording"

cd "$BIN"
./test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q $COMMON \
  2>&1 | tee /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_detect_buoy_5s.log
cp -f output/french_meridional_ur_uz_mean.csv /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_meridional_buoy_5s.csv
cp -f output/french_spatial_stats.csv /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_spatial_buoy_5s.csv

./test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q $COMMON --zero-buoyancy \
  2>&1 | tee /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_detect_zero_5s.log
cp -f output/french_meridional_ur_uz_mean.csv /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_meridional_zero_5s.csv
cp -f output/french_spatial_stats.csv /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_spatial_zero_5s.csv

# extract
grep -E 'natural-ra|meridional|natural-detect|passed=' \
  /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_detect_buoy_5s.log \
  /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/nat_detect_zero_5s.log
```

Optional longer window: `--end-time=10` (same flags). Prefer completing the 5 s pair first.

## Decision rule (go / no-go finer mesh)

**Go finer natural relax** only if **all** hold:

- `U_visc / U_noise ≳ 0.5` at measured end-of-run ΔT (or literature ΔT if substituted);
- buoy run `U_merid_mean` or `|circ_proxy|` clearly above the zero-buoyancy twin (roughly ≥ 2× or well outside sample noise);
- skull/free-crust thermal path already active (this Level-2 setup);
- optional: `--temperature-dependent-properties` if μ contrast across ΔT is large.

Otherwise document **defer quantitative natural-cell reproduction** and keep natural as architecture / energy-closure smoke only.

## Park note (2026-09-18)

Dual 5 s buoy / zero-buoyancy runs completed → **no-go**. Natural quantitative cell work is **parked**. Active track: stirring Round 3D ([`20_ROUND3D_STIRRED_COMPARISON_PLAN.md`](20_ROUND3D_STIRRED_COMPARISON_PLAN.md)).

## Explicit non-claims

- Not Jacoutot cell reproduction or paper Nu/Ra validation.
- Proxy ΔT = 20 K at start; end-of-run re-estimate uses measured `dT`.
- `circ_proxy` is an SPH diagnostic, not a published streamfunction.


<!-- ===== END 19_ROUND3C_NATURAL_FLOW_DETECTABILITY.md ===== -->

# File: `20_ROUND3D_STIRRED_COMPARISON_PLAN.md`

# Round 3D — Stirred comparison after heat closure

> Date: 2026-09-18  
> Case: `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em`  
> Thermal path: Level-1 `--skull-resistance` + `--free-crust-resistance` (doc 16)  
> Prerequisite park: natural Round 3C **no-go** (doc 19); see also track status below.

## Track status (natural parked)

| Track | Status |
|-------|--------|
| Natural Level-2 Contact / energy | done (doc 17–18); keep as smoke only |
| Natural Round 3C detectability | **no-go** finer mesh (doc 19) |
| Natural Jacoutot cell / Ra–Nu | **deferred** until noise floor addressed |
| Stirring Level-1 skull+crust 5 rev | done (doc 16) |
| Stirring `--skull-shell` | **not wired** — refuse; use Level-1 |
| Stirring Round 3D Np/Nu + fixed-power pair | **done** (10 rev both modes); paper L still open |

## Goal

With physically interpretable thermal closure (not `--balance-heat-loss`), run locked-current periodic thermo–EM stirring and report SPH-labeled Np / Nu candidates plus temperature uniformity, without claiming bit-identical Jacoutot figures yet.

## Deliverables

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| 5 rev Level-1 skull+crust baseline | done (doc 16) |
| Screen/CSV `P_mech`, `Np_sph`, `Nu_sph` candidates | wired this session |
| 10 rev (~60 s) locked-current run | **done** — `passed=1` (2026-09-18) |
| Optional fixed-power sensitivity | **done** — equivalent to locked-current on 10 rev |
| Paper-definition Np/Nu audit | open (label SPH candidates until paper L frozen) |

## 10 rev result (Level-1 skull+crust, fixed-current)

Log: `build/stir_skull_crust_10rev.log`. Wall ~0.42 h (~25 min).

| Metric | End (rev≈10) |
|--------|----------------|
| P_joule | 60.00 kW |
| total_heat_loss | 57.87 kW (loss/P ≈ 0.96) |
| T_mean / T_std | 1473.4 K / 21.1 K |
| T_min / T_max | 1362 / 1509 K |
| T_out / T_ctr | 1470 / 1466 K |
| U_rms / U_th | 0.073 / 0.047 m/s (bulk mix present; U_max spikes ignored) |
| Tz / P_mech | −0.51 Nm / **0.53 W** |
| Np_sph | ~4.0 (noisy; mid-run often 2–4) |
| Nu_sph | ~30 (fell slowly ~32→30) |
| coupling_updates | 120 |
| escaped / diverged | 0 / 0 |
| passed | **1** |

**Read:**

- Thermal closure holds: no runaway cool; loss tracks joule within ~4%.
- Mechanical power ≪ EM (~0.5 W vs 60 kW) — expected for creeping swirl; Np_sph is an SPH label, not yet paper Np.
- Nu_sph stable order ~30; definition uses wall side+bottom only + melt ΔT (OPEN).
- Compared to 5 rev baseline (doc 16): similar T_mean, larger T_std (~14→21), still interpretable.

**Next (after this pair):** Parallel Round A EM field trust — see `docs/ophelie/21_ROUND_A_EM_FIELD_TRUST.md`. Longer 20 rev not required for control-mode closure. Paper L for Np/Nu remains open.

## SPH metric definitions (explicitly not paper-frozen)

Uses impeller tip radius `r_tip` from rotor particles, `ω` from 10 rpm, `D = 2 r_tip`, `N = ω/(2π)` [rev/s].

```text
P_mech   = |T_z| · ω                         [W]
Np_sph   = P_mech / (ρ N³ D⁵)                 [-]
h_eff    = (Q_side + Q_bottom) / (A_wb · max(ΔT, ε))
Nu_sph   = h_eff · H / k                      [-]
```

- `A_wb` = side + bottom geometric areas from French zero-D budget.  
- `ΔT = T_max − T_min` on melt (OPEN; paper Nu may use other ΔT).  
- Free-surface loss is logged separately; not folded into `Nu_sph` wall candidate.  
- If paper characteristic diameter differs, retag — do not silently rescale.

## Acceptance (this round)

- no rapid unphysical bulk cooling with skull+crust;
- `P_joule` and total loss stay interpretable over ≥10 rev;
- `Np_sph`, `Nu_sph`, `T_std`, `T_out`/`T_ctr` written every screen/CSV cadence;
- `passed=1`, no divergence.

## Command — 10 revolutions

```bash
source /opt/intel/oneapi/setvars.sh
cd /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build
ninja test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em

BIN=/home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em/bin
cd "$BIN"
./test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em \
  --reload-dir=../../test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_glass_relax/bin/reload \
  --thermo-em-coupling=periodic --em-update-interval=0.5 \
  --em-control=fixed-current --aind=off \
  --skull-resistance --skull-delta=0.005 \
  --free-crust-resistance --free-crust-delta=0.005 \
  --end-time=60 --no-state-recording \
  2>&1 | tee /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/stir_skull_crust_10rev.log

grep -E 'Np_sph|Nu_sph|P_mech|passed=|revolutions' \
  /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/stir_skull_crust_10rev.log | tail -50
```

Shorter smoke (already validated class): `--end-time=30` (5 rev).

Same reload, skull+crust, interval, and horizon as the locked-current baseline. Only the control mode changes. Log: `build/stir_skull_crust_10rev_fixed_power.log`.

| Check | Locked-current | Fixed-power |
|-------|----------------|-------------|

# File: `21_ROUND_A_EM_FIELD_TRUST.md`

# Round A — EM field trust (French)

> Date: 2026-09-18  
> After: Round 3D closed (doc 20); natural Round 3C parked (doc 19)  
> Plan source: `docs/ophelie/13` § Parallel Round A

## Goal

Base production EM choices on **measured field / penetration error**, not only solver convergence or total power.

## Deliverables

| # | Item | Status |
|---|------|--------|
| 1 | Skin-depth error decomposition | **partial done** — δ + radial shells; weak-feedback Q∝r² restored in Batch E (`analytic_induction_simple`) |
| 2 | External French r–z reference | **blocked tools** — GetDP/Gmsh absent; schema scaffold in `docs/ophelie/reference/em_rz_getdp/` |
| 3 | A_ind off/on comparison | **coarse baseline done** — one-way vs Picard on dp=0.05; production-mesh Picard deferred |

## Inventory (what exists)

| Asset | Role |
|-------|------|
| `diagnostics/electromagnetic_ophelie_french_skin_depth_diagnostic.h` | δ, δ/R, outer-1δ power frac, plane-skin proxy, Q_outer/center |
| `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em` | logs `[ophelie][skin-depth]` after EM handoff |
| `test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic` | one-way A_ind/A_coil + skin report |
| `test_3d_ophelie_french_self_induction_picard` | Picard A_total = A_coil + A_ind |
| `french_q_rz.csv` / `french_sigma_rz.csv` | **simulated** axisymmetric maps (not external refs) |
| `test_3d_ophelie_analytic_induction_simple` | documented in doc 09 — **missing from this tree** |
| External French r–z probe CSV | **TBD** |

## Skin-depth diagnostic (SPH labels)

```text
δ = sqrt(2 / (ω μ0 σ))
regime: thin_skin (δ/R<0.1) | intermediate (<0.3) | deep_penetration
P_outer_1delta_frac = power with r ≥ R−min(δ,R)
plane_skin_proxy_frac = ∫_0^{shell} exp(−2x/δ) / ∫_0^R …   (plane approximation)
```

For Jacoutot glass (f ~ 280–300 kHz, σ ~ 16–25 S/m, R ~ 0.25 m) expect **δ ~ R** → `deep_penetration`. Thin-skin plane proxy is then weak; do not treat shell mismatch as a blocking operator bug without a proper cylinder reference.

## A_ind baseline (commands)

Production stirring/natural periodic coupling defaults to **`--aind=off`** (coil-only φ). Picard is experimental and O(N²) expensive on stirring reload (~3e5 particles).

### Coarse lattice smoke (recommended first)

```bash
source /opt/intel/oneapi/setvars.sh
cd /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build
ninja test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic test_3d_ophelie_french_self_induction_picard

AIN=./tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic/bin
PIC=./tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_french_self_induction_picard/bin

$AIN/test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic --dp=0.05 \
  --ophelie-current-form=edge-flux --ophelie-edge-flux-complex=1 \
  2>&1 | tee /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/roundA_aind_oneway_dp05.log

$PIC/test_3d_ophelie_french_self_induction_picard --dp=0.05 \
  --ophelie-current-form=edge-flux --ophelie-edge-flux-complex=1 \
  --self-induction --self-induction-max-iter=15 \
  2>&1 | tee /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/roundA_aind_picard_dp05.log
```

Or: `bash /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/run_roundA_em_trust_smokes.sh` (Picard section uses max_iter=15 after this update).

Record: `A_ind_over_A_coil`, `B_ind_over_B_coil`, `P_joule_W`, `picard_converged`, skin `delta/R` + `P_outer_1delta_frac`.

### Production-geometry skin (stirring, aind=off)

EM handoff only needs a few acoustic steps after Q map:

```bash
BIN=/home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em/bin
cd "$BIN"
./test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em \
  --reload-dir=../../test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_glass_relax/bin/reload \
  --thermo-em-coupling=periodic --em-update-interval=0.5 \
  --em-control=fixed-current --aind=off \
  --skull-resistance --skull-delta=0.005 \
  --free-crust-resistance --free-crust-delta=0.005 \
  --end-time=0.05 --no-state-recording \
  2>&1 | tee /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/roundA_stir_skin_aind_off.log

grep -E 'skin-depth|EM handoff|Q_outer' /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/roundA_stir_skin_aind_off.log
```

Full stirring `--aind=on` Picard on production mesh is **deferred** (cost); use reduced lattice/picard for feedback comparison.

## Acceptance (this round)

- Document δ/R regime for French glass and radial power shells on production stirring EM.
- Document one-way vs Picard A_ind ratios on a reproducible coarse mesh.
- Do **not** claim external French r–z match until a reference CSV is available.
- Keep production default `--aind=off` unless measured error justifies Picard.

## Results (2026-09-18)

| Case | Key metrics | Notes |
|------|-------------|-------|
| Analytic δ (stirring σ=24.7, 300 kHz) | δ=0.185 m, δ/R=0.74, δ/dp=37, **deep_penetration** | plane-skin outer-1δ proxy ≈0.93 (weak when δ~R) |
| Analytic δ (natural σ=16, 282 kHz) | δ=0.237 m, δ/R=0.95 | same regime |
| Stirring production skin (`roundA_stir_skin_aind_off.log`) | δ/R=0.739, P_outer_r>0.75R_frac=0.724, **Q_outer/center=33.3**, P=60.0 kW, aind=off | regime deep_penetration |
| A_ind one-way dp=0.05 | A_ind/A_coil=**0.415**, B_ind/B_coil=**0.355**, P=7.40 W (uncalibrated lattice), skin δ/R=0.71, Q_o/c=27.5, **passed=1** | **Superseded by Batch B** — old path skipped real feedback (`max_J_real=0`); see doc 25 |
| Picard dp=0.05 (max_iter=15) | converged in **7** iters, J_rel=0.040, A_ind/A_coil=**0.405**, B_ind/B_coil=**0.347**, P=6.54 W, **passed=1** | **Superseded by Batch B** — old `J_rel` was relaxed step; corrected: 9 iters, fixed_point≈0.044, P≈6.31 W |

### Read

1. French glass at literature f/σ is **deep penetration** (δ ~ R). Thin-skin plate gates are the wrong primary metric; keep radial shell / Q_outer/center as SPH labels until an external r–z ref exists.
2. One-way A_ind is already ~40% of A_coil on coarse lattice — not negligible. Picard converges and trims P by ~12% at fixed I, but A_ind/A_coil barely moves. Production stays **`--aind=off`** until a calibrated fixed-power pair on finer mesh justifies otherwise.
3. External French r–z reference remains the open blocker for deliverable 2.
4. Do not run full stirring `--aind=on` on production reload for Round A (cost); reduced lattice is the documented baseline.

## Explicit non-claims

- Not Jacoutot Fig. local J/E quantitative match  
- Not TEAM7 Bind/Bcoil closure transferred to French glass  
- Not restoring missing `analytic_induction_simple` in this first slice  
- Natural finer-mesh campaign remains parked

# File: `22_ROUND_A_EXTERNAL_RZ_GPT_DISCUSSION_2026-09-18.md`

5. Give **one primary next step** (2–4 weeks) and explicit deferrals.

Questions in §7.

## 1. Where the project stands (short)

| Track | Status |
|-------|--------|
| Natural Level-2 Contact / energy | done; smoke only |
| Natural Round 3C flow detectability | **no-go** (buoy ≈ zero-buoyancy control); finer mesh **parked** |

## 2. What “external French r–z reference” means

### 2.1 What we already write (simulation output — not a reference)

Under thermo–EM coupling the code can emit **axisymmetric averages of our own fields**:

| File | Content |
|------|---------|
| `french_q_rz.csv` | simulated Joule density Q(r,z) |
| `french_sigma_rz.csv` | simulated σ(r,z) after T→σ map |

These are useful for plotting and for comparing **our** `--aind=off` vs `on`, but they are **not** an independent truth.

### 2.2 What is missing

An **independent** dataset for the **French glass / Jacoutot-like** geometry and excitation, e.g.:

- Q(r,z) or |J|(r,z) or B on an r–z grid, or  
- a fixed probe polyline (docs historically mentioned a **30-point French probe CSV**; **not present** in this tree),  

from COMSOL, OPHELIE, a thesis supplement, or carefully digitized Jacoutot figures — with documented f, σ, coil layout, and current/power convention.

### 2.3 What does **not** count

| Asset | Why it does not close French Round A |
|-------|--------------------------------------|
| `reference_data/team7/*.csv` | Different geometry, metal plate, 50 Hz-class TEAM7; not French melt |
| Matching `P_joule` to 50/60 kW | Integral constraint only; wrong radial shape can still hit power |
| φ residual / edge-flux self-consistency | Solver health, not literature field shape |
| Analytic skin depth δ alone | Regime label; not a full-field error vs Jacoutot |

---

## 3. Why it matters (impact)

### 3.1 One-line thesis

**Total absorbed power can be correct while the spatial Joule map is wrong.**  
Without an external French r–z (or probe) reference, Round A cannot **accept or reject** the local heating pattern — only report SPH-labeled diagnostics.

### 3.2 What stays open without it

| Topic | Effect |
|-------|--------|
| Skin-depth / radial shells | We can report δ/R and P fractions; we **cannot** quote “% error vs literature” |
| Production `--aind` | Coarse Picard shows ~12% power change at fixed I; without a map we **keep `aind=off`** by policy, not by measured field error |
| Stirred Nu_sph / T uniformity | Thermally closed runs can look fine with a **wrong Q shape** |
| Natural convection (parked) | Buoyancy cells depend on heating distribution; field-shape error would poison any later Ra–Nu claim |
| Paper / client narrative | Allowed: power book, thermal closure, SPH metrics. **Not allowed:** “matched Jacoutot EM field figures” |

### 3.3 What is **not** blocked

- Continue Level-1 stirring engineering runs (fixed-current, skull+crust).  
- Continue SPH metric logging (Np_sph, Nu_sph) as **candidates**.  
- Keep natural parked; no need for finer natural mesh for this decision.  
- Coarse A_ind diagnostics and documentation.

---

### 4.1 Skin / penetration (French glass)

| Case | δ | δ/R | Regime | Notes |
|------|---|-----|--------|-------|
| Stirring σ≈24.7 S/m, 300 kHz, R≈0.25 m | 0.185 m | **0.74** | **deep_penetration** | production reload |
| Natural σ=16, 282 kHz | 0.237 m | 0.95 | deep_penetration | analytic only |
| Stirring short EM (`aind=off`) | — | 0.739 | deep | Q_outer/center (**r>0.75R vs r<0.25R means**) ≈ **33.3**; P=60 kW |

Implication we want checked: **thin-skin plate-style gates are the wrong primary metric** for this melt; deep penetration is expected.

### 4.2 A_ind one-way vs Picard (coarse lattice dp=0.05, edge-flux complex)

| Mode | A_ind/A_coil | B_ind/B_coil | P_joule (uncalibrated lattice) | Result |
|------|--------------|--------------|--------------------------------|--------|
| One-way diagnostic | **0.415** | 0.355 | 7.40 W | passed=1 |
| Picard (7 iters, converged) | **0.405** | 0.347 | 6.54 W (−11.5% vs one-way) | passed=1 |

Production stirring mesh Picard (`~3e5` particles, O(N²) Biot) is **deferred** on cost. Default remains `--aind=off`.

### 4.3 Code / docs added this session

- `diagnostics/electromagnetic_ophelie_french_skin_depth_diagnostic.h`  
- Wired into `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em` and `test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic`  
- Plan + numbers: `docs/ophelie/21_ROUND_A_EM_FIELD_TRUST.md`  
- Missing from tree (still): `test_3d_ophelie_analytic_induction_simple/`, French external probe CSV

---

## 5. Candidate reference strategies (for you to rank)

| Option | Effort | Closes what | Risk |
|--------|--------|-------------|------|
| **A.** Obtain/digitize Jacoutot or COMSOL r–z / probe for French melt | Medium–high (data access) | Deliverable 2 directly | Digitization error; figure not same σ/f |
| **B.** Rebuild conducting-cylinder / analytic induction case (`analytic_induction_simple` was documented, missing here) | Medium (code) | Operator skin error vs analytic; **not** Jacoutot figure match | Wrong geometry vs paper furnace |
| **C.** Use TEAM7 probes as proxy | Low | Almost nothing for French | Wrong physics regime |
| **D.** Stay SPH-self-consistent longer; treat Round A.2 as deferred | Low | Honest midterm scope | Leaves A_ind and Q-shape claims soft |
| **E.** Fixed-power calibrated Picard vs coil-only on finer French reduced mesh, still without external map | Medium | Sensitivity only | Still no absolute field truth |

Our current bias: **A if data exists; else B for operator trust + D for paper claims**; avoid C; do not flip production `aind` on E alone.

# File: `23_FRENCH_EM_INTERNAL_FIXES_AND_DEVELOPMENT_PLAN_2026-09-19.md`

# French OPHELIE reproduction: internal corrections and development plan

Date: 2026-09-19  
Audience: Cursor taking over development on `feature/EM-Biot-Savart`  
Scope: correctness repairs, verification, independent EM reference, and subsequent French literature reproduction.  
Document language: English, following the project's English-only documentation convention.

## 0. Read this first

The next development round is **internal consistency repair**, followed by independent electromagnetic validation. Do not treat the missing OPHELIE/COMSOL CSV as the only blocker.

The recent implementation provides a useful periodic thermo–EM prototype and explicit skull/crust resistance options. However, source inspection found gaps in transferred-power measurement, energy accounting, one-way complex feedback, Picard stopping/finalization, and acceptance checks. Fix these before relying on long-run thermal balance or enabling self-induction as the production default.

This task brief is based on static inspection of the user-supplied `roundA_recent_code_chatgpt_2026-09-18` code pack, its three log excerpts, and docs 13–22. The reviewer did **not** compile or execute the full solver. Some included files call implementations absent from the pack. Therefore:

- **CONFIRMED** means directly visible in the inspected source or log.
- **RISK / VERIFY** means a plausible defect whose full call path or kernel implementation must be checked in the complete repository.
- **MODEL LIMITATION** means an approximation or insufficient validation, not necessarily a programming error.
- Before modifying anything, compare the actual current checkout with the reviewed snapshot. Do not reapply fixes already present.

This document supersedes older roadmap statements where they conflict with the findings below. Historical run results remain historical evidence; do not erase or silently relabel them as results of corrected code.

## 1. Retain the established project scope

### 1.1 Reference regimes

| Item | Natural reference | Stirred reference |
|---|---|---|
| Initial glass absorbed power | 50 kW | 60 kW |
| Current reference frequency | 282 kHz | 300 kHz |
| Current production spacing | 15 mm | 5 mm |
| Vertical axis for current meshes | +z | +z |
| Stirrer | None | 10 rpm |
| Periodic baseline | Fixed current, A_ind off | Fixed current, A_ind off |

These are current configuration values, not a fresh verification of every journal parameter. Preserve unresolved geometry/property differences as `OPEN_PARAMETER`. Generator power around 400 kW is not the glass absorbed-power target. Natural and stirred reloads must not be mixed.

### 1.2 Preserve working functionality

- Complex edge-flux EM and initial `P_recon`-based current calibration.
- Shared physical-time periodic coupling controller.
- Temperature-to-conductivity mapping and azimuthal averaging.
- Fixed-current and fixed-absorbed-power modes as distinct modes.
- Laboratory-fixed Eulerian heat-source sampling by moving fluid particles.
- Frozen-Q/one-shot regression modes.
- Open free surface; do not add a lid to suppress hydro errors.
- Explicit skull/crust parameters and explicit conservative-remap logging.

`A_ind=off` remains a reduced regression baseline, not a validated assertion that self-induction is negligible. Do not change the default solely because a coarse test has A_ind/A_coil around 0.4.

## 2. What the latest work actually supports

| Topic | Supported conclusion | Unsupported extension |
|---|---|---|
| Periodic coupling | Both cases have an implemented, exercised feedback path | Every power transfer and physical field is validated |
| Stirring with skull/crust | A 10-revolution run with near-stable mean T was reported | Dynamic skull or paper-grade thermal closure |

| Coarse Picard | An iteration stopped under its configured relaxed criterion | Accurate self-consistent production EM |
| Material interfaces | Temperature-dependent fields and a mean-viscosity update exist | Full local variable-viscosity physics is active in production |
| passed=1 | Existing smoke-style conditions were met | Strict solver, transfer, energy, and run-completion gates all passed |

## 3. Mandatory checkout audit before edits

1. Record branch, full commit SHA, dirty-worktree status, compiler/backend, and precision (`Real` size).
2. Read applicable repository instructions and current docs 14–22.
3. Locate the functions listed below using `rg`; do not rely on historical line numbers.

5. Inspect missing dependencies in the full checkout, especially:
   - `electromagnetic_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_one_way.h`;
   - complex edge-flux component construction, solve, and reconstruction;
   - `hostEdgeFluxReconPower` and the EM-to-thermal Q synchronization;
   - CK viscosity and force kernels, material setter, and device kernel lifetime;
   - Boussinesq force and `ForcePrior` update semantics;
   - Euler grid/device interpolation and particle sorting registration;
   - skull-shell contact diffusion and cooling implementation.
6. Produce a concise table: finding ID, still present/already fixed/not reproducible, evidence, proposed action.
7. Continue with the repairs below. Do not stop after producing a plan when implementation is feasible.

Primary source prefix:

```text
tests/extra_source_and_tests/extra_src/shared/electromagnetic_ophelie/
```
