
## 4. P0-01 — Measure power actually received by the fluid

### Evidence: CONFIRMED

Both natural and stirred `depositEmQAndRecord()` functions receive `em_particles` and set:

```cpp
book.p_sph_particles = hostParticlePower(q, vol, n);
book.p_euler_sample = hostSamplePowerFromScalarGrid(q_grid, em_pos, em_vol);
```

The arrays in both expressions belong to the EM body. Consequently, the field labeled `P_sph` does not measure the moving thermal-fluid body's sampled heating power.

### Required correction

Introduce unambiguous quantities:

| Quantity | Meaning |
|---|---|
| P_em_recon | Integrated EM reconstructed Joule density |
| P_grid_at_em | Grid sampled at EM particle positions using EM integration weights |
| P_heat_fluid | Actual fluid `JouleHeat` integrated using the thermal update's physical volume convention |
| E_joule_expected | Time integral of the heating source actually used by the update |
| E_joule_applied | Thermal enthalpy increment across the Joule-only update |

- Compute the fluid quantity after sampling on the **fluid** body, including after each Q-grid refresh.
- Monitor the current fluid sampling during motion, not only at t=0.
- Match heating and integration conventions: with a reference-density update, use the corresponding mass/reference-volume weights; do not blindly substitute instantaneous WCSPH volume without checking the equation.
- Use device reductions where appropriate rather than transferring all particles to the host every acoustic step.
- Track signed and relative EM-to-fluid discrepancies. A changing occupied domain can cause a genuine discrepancy; diagnose it rather than forcing equality.
- Use actual applied fluid heating in the fluid energy budget. Keep EM power separately as a coupling diagnostic.
- Rename misleading historical CSV columns or add a schema version and explicit compatibility aliases.

If conservative remapping is enabled, record before/after power, scale, evaluation domain, and target. Do not claim fluid conservation based only on a correction measured at EM points. Never silently rescale Q to conceal mapping errors.

### Verification

Use an existing small case or helper-level test for a constant Q field and a smooth spatially varying Q field. Check sampling and total heating before/after particle movement and sorting. Verify that a deliberately perturbed mapping is detected. Confirm zero-Q gives zero injected heat.

Suggested initial transfer targets for comparable occupied domains: approximately 1% grid-at-EM and 2% fluid-integrated error, subject to a documented resolution study. These are proposed engineering targets, not journal tolerances; do not silently relax them to obtain a pass.

## 5. P0-02 — Repair smoke versus validation gates

### Evidence: CONFIRMED

In `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em.cpp`:

- periodic `em_ok` only checks finite power and finite phi residual;
- `coupling_updates >= 1` accepts the initialization solve;
- `q_sample_rel_l2` is populated at initialization and reused;
- `n_out_of_grid` is evaluated at initialization and reused at the end;
- `budget_exhausted` is printed but does not prevent `passed=1`;
- thermal acceptance checks finite values and positive heat loss, not cumulative conservation.

The 0.05 s excerpt reports one coupling update and `passed=1`; this is valid only as a startup smoke result.

### Required correction

Separate at least:

```text
startup_ok
completed_requested_time
periodic_refresh_count
periodic_refresh_ok
em_solver_ok
q_transfer_ok
flow_sanity_ok
thermal_energy_ok
validation_level
```

- Distinguish initialization EM solves from actual periodic refreshes.
- A dedicated coupling test must have at least three actual refreshes after initialization. General runs should verify the expected schedule rather than always demanding three.
- Record actual refresh times, scheduled interval, and maximum scheduling delay. Define endpoint conventions to avoid an off-by-one gate.
- Apply residual thresholds appropriate to the equation and check both complex components; finiteness alone is insufficient.
- When A_ind is enabled, require the defined self-consistency criterion before accepting a refreshed Q. On failure, abort or explicitly reject the update; never silently continue as validated.
- Track current and maximum-over-run out-of-grid counts and transfer errors.
- Distinguish an intentional wall-time-budget stop from completion of the requested physical time.
- Do not force a 50/60 kW proximity gate on a fixed-current run after T changes. That target is an initial calibration condition.
- Report failure reasons individually. A smoke run may pass smoke requirements without claiming higher gates.

### Verification

Include short tests that intentionally end before a refresh, exhaust a small wall budget, exceed a residual limit, and place a sample outside the grid. Each must produce the correct incomplete/failure status rather than a broad validation pass.

## 6. P0-03 — Correct one-way complex self-induction feedback

### Evidence: CONFIRMED condition; full effect requires dependency inspection

In `diagnostics/electromagnetic_ophelie_aind_diagnostic.h`, the feedback path computes `skip_real_feedback` from **pre-feedback** real current:

```cpp
max_j_r_before <= TinyReal * max(max_j_i_before, Real(1))
```

The provided one-way log reports nonzero `A_src_imag_norm`, then skips real phi and retains zero real current and unchanged power:

```text
A_src_imag_norm = 2.16131e-07
P_complex_coil_only = 7.39706 W
P_complex_total_A = 7.39706 W
max_J_real_feedback = 0
```

With the convention exp(+i omega t), E = -grad(phi) - i omega A, so:

```text
E_real = -grad(phi_real) + omega A_imag
E_imag = -grad(phi_imag) - omega A_real
```

Zero old real current does not imply zero new real electromagnetic response.

### Required correction

- Inspect what `skip_real_feedback` suppresses in the complete solver: phi only, reconstruction, field clearing, or multiple operations.
- Remove the old-current-based shortcut from the validation path.
- Execute complete complex reconstruction after updating A_total.
- If a zero-RHS phi solve is safely skipped, still reconstruct any direct electric-field contribution from A; zero divergence source does not imply zero electric field.
- Confirm the actual harmonic sign convention and use it consistently in A, phi, E, J, and power.
- Audit host/device synchronization before feedback norm checks.

### Verification

Add a case with nonzero imaginary A and known nonzero real induction response. Check real/imaginary sign, magnitude, and current continuity. Repeat with the opposite phase. Do not impose a universal direction of power change; compare to the independently derived response for the test.

Rerun coil-only, one-way feedback, and Picard at identical geometry, f, sigma, current, discretization, and amplitude convention. Preserve old logs as superseded diagnostics.

## 7. P0-04 — Make Picard residuals and final fields consistent

### Evidence: CONFIRMED algorithm order; downstream power consistency needs full tracing

`stage2/electromagnetic_ophelie_self_induction.h` performs:

```text
A_ind from current iterate
→ combine A
→ solve/reconstruct E, J, Q
→ under-relax J
→ measure change in the relaxed J
→ possibly exit
```

The excerpt uses relaxation 0.3, j_tol 0.05, and exits at relaxed J change 0.039832 after seven iterations. This is neither a 4% field error nor a demonstrated 5% unrelaxed fixed-point residual.

The function shows no final consistent-state reconstruction after the last relaxation. Trace the caller and `hostEdgeFluxReconPower` to determine exactly which final fields supply power and thermal Q.

### Required correction

Let F(J_old) be the unrelaxed current returned by a complete EM solve using the induced field generated from J_old. Record separately:

```text
fixed_point_residual = ||F(J_old) - J_old|| / suitable_nonzero_norm
relaxed_step_change = ||J_new - J_old|| / suitable_nonzero_norm
J_new = (1-alpha) J_old + alpha F(J_old)
```

- Use a combined complex, preferably volume-weighted norm with an absolute floor, rather than allowing a nearly zero individual component to dominate.
- The fixed-point residual, both phi residuals, and field identities govern convergence. Small alpha must not manufacture convergence.
- Zero or one-iteration execution must not be called self-consistent solely because an initial change variable starts at zero.
- Keep iteration/source fields separate from the final physical output if needed.
- Before declaring completion, ensure the delivered A, E, J, and Q satisfy the coupled equations to the chosen tolerance. A final recomputation must itself be checked; one extra sweep is not automatically sufficient.
- Check J = sigma E, both Joule expressions, current continuity, and the induced-field update defect.
- Report maximum-iteration failure explicitly through the thermal handoff.

### Verification

On the same small mesh, sweep tolerances (for example 5e-2, 1e-2, 1e-3) and at least two relaxation factors. Compare final power and profiles as well as iteration count. Select production tolerances based on output stability, not an arbitrary iteration count.

The existing coarse logs use R=0.325 m, sigma=16 S/m, f=300 kHz, dp=0.05 m. Production stirring uses roughly R=0.25 m and sigma=24.75 S/m. Do not transfer the coarse 11.5% power difference directly to production.

## 8. P0-05 — Correct energy accounting and temperature-dependent heat capacity

### Evidence: CONFIRMED

`hostThermalEnergyWithParticleCp()` in `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_material_laws.h` sums rho * cp(T) * T * V, whereas Joule heating advances T with Q/(rho cp(T)). These definitions are not mutually consistent for ordinary differential heat capacity cp(T).

### Required correction

For the current sensible-heat model, define a common reference enthalpy:

```text
h(T) - h(T_ref) = integral[T_ref to T] cp(theta) dtheta
H = sum_i m_i h(T_i)
```

- Integrate the actual piecewise material law, including its specified out-of-range behavior. For piecewise-linear cp, segment integration can be exact.
- Use conserved particle masses or a demonstrably equivalent reference-volume convention.
- Use double accumulation for H, cumulative energies, and residuals.
- Do not add a T*dcp/dt term to the temperature update merely to make cp(T)*T bookkeeping agree. First verify the paper's notation and the thermodynamic definition.
- Check every thermal path: Joule source, conduction, boundary cooling, and skull contact. They must all use the intended heat-capacity convention.
- Calls currently pass constant cp to diffusion and boundary constructors; inspect whether their kernels internally bind particle cp. This remains a **VERIFY** item because the thermal implementation was absent from the review pack.
- If choosing an enthalpy update, invert the same h(T) law. If retaining explicit T updates, demonstrate time-step convergence of the energy defect.

### Cumulative budget

For the melt-only control volume:

```text
R_E = H(t2) - H(t1)
      - [E_joule_applied - E_boundary_out + E_other_signed]
```

Use fluxes integrated over the actual update intervals. Comparing a finite-window dH/dt to a single endpoint power introduces avoidable sampling error.

- Record temperature-clamp energy separately. A clamp must not silently inject/remove heat.
- Check pairwise conduction conserves internal energy globally in an insulated domain.
- For a resolved/effective shell with thermal storage, distinguish melt-to-shell transfer from shell-to-coolant loss. A combined melt+shell budget must cancel internal contact exchange.
- If viscous heating is omitted, state that choice; do not insert paddle power into the thermal budget unless it is actually deposited as heat.
- Normalize residuals using gross energy throughput or a declared scale, not the nearly zero net input-minus-loss at equilibrium.

### Verification

1. Constant cp, uniform adiabatic heating: known temperature rise.
2. Variable cp, adiabatic heating/cooling: compare h(T) against known deposited energy.
3. Insulated nonuniform-temperature conduction: total H remains constant.
4. Specified boundary cooling: integrated removal agrees with H change.
5. Clamp deliberately activated: its energy is measured and reported.

Use a proposed approximately 1% target for controlled small tests and initially 2–5% for integrated coupled energy closure, with time-step/precision sensitivity documented. Never describe these as already achieved.

## 9. P0-06 — Audit discrete boundary areas before tuning skull parameters

### Evidence: CONFIRMED discrepancy; cause not established

The short stirred log at the initial reference temperature reports:

| Heat loss | Geometric zero-D estimate | Particle boundary estimate |
|---|---:|---:|
| Side | 46.43 kW | 38.06 kW |
| Bottom | 6.86 kW | 5.56 kW |
| Free convection + radiation | 15.29 kW | 15.62 kW |
| Total | 68.57 kW | 59.23 kW |

Side/bottom differ by roughly 18–19%; the total differs by roughly 14%. This is not yet proof of an implementation defect: check actual geometry, tagging, shell integration, initial temperatures, and excluded regions. It is sufficient to prevent claiming that proximity to 60 kW validates skull parameters.

### Required correction/investigation

- Disable flow and set uniform temperature for an isolated boundary audit.
- Export geometric area, discrete effective area, tagged particle count, shell thickness, mean boundary temperature, flux density, and integrated power per face group.
- Check side/bottom corner treatment, free-surface tagging, missing faces, and double counting.
- Compare the update kernel's actual heat removal with the diagnostic power function.
- Study particle resolution and boundary-shell thickness independently.
- If normalization is necessary as a quadrature correction, derive and expose it; do not fit heat loss to target EM power.
- Confirm whether inherited h_side/h_bottom represent coolant-side coefficients or already-effective overall coefficients. Adding skull resistance to an already-effective coefficient could double count thermal resistance.

The wall formula 1/h_eff = delta/k_skull + 1/h_cool is an explicit engineering model. It still needs parameter provenance. A 5 mm skull/crust is `OPEN_PARAMETER` unless independently supported; a stable mean T is not an independent validation of a fitted thickness.

### Shell-specific risk

The natural Level-2 helper rescales conductivity to match resistance through a numerically thicker wall. Verify thermal capacitance and diffusion time as well: matching L/k alone does not preserve rho*cp*L or transient response. Include energy effects of any fixed-temperature outer-wall reset.

Acceptance: explain or correct the discrepancy and show a convergent area/flux estimate. Do not tune physical skull thickness while this is unresolved.

## 10. P1-01 — Finish material-law wiring without overstating it

### Evidence: CONFIRMED

- `constant_thermal_rheology` defaults to true.
- The documented 10-revolution commands do not include `--temperature-dependent-properties`.
- The variable-property helper computes particle mu values but returns volume-mean mu to `setReferenceViscosity()`.
- The tables are described as visual digitization with `OPEN_PARAMETER` provenance.

### Required work

1. Print a startup configuration record showing constant/variable sigma, cp, k, and viscosity mode independently.
2. Retain the constant-property baseline; do not rewrite its results as variable-property results.
3. Label the current option as mean-viscosity(T) approximation until a local viscous discretization exists.
4. Audit whether the CK kernel caches viscosity at construction. Confirm host setters affect inner forces, wall forces, rotor reaction, and relevant time-step criteria on CPU and GPU.
5. Verify with a controlled viscous-force test: changing uniform mu should change the appropriate force predictably at fixed positions/velocities.
6. Implement local variable viscosity only after this audit, using a consistent conservative pair/stress formulation for fluid and wall interactions. Do not choose arithmetic/harmonic averaging without checking the discretization.
7. Preserve source figure, digitized points, units, validity range, interpolation, and uncertainty. Anchoring a table at 1473 K does not validate the entire curve.
8. Avoid full host/device transfers every acoustic step when coefficients can be evaluated on the device; optimize only after correctness is demonstrated.

The reported double-k diffusion repair must also be verified in the full implementation. A k=1 MMS cannot expose an accidental second factor of k. Include k not equal to 1 and verify diffusion-time scaling.

## 11. P1-02 — Document the actual axisymmetric coupling path

The reviewed `depositEmQAndRecord()` forms an r–z average for output, but deposits the original 3D EM q values directly into the Euler grid. Therefore the current path is:

```text
3D T → sigma(T) → averaged sigma(r,z) → 3D EM
→ original 3D Q deposited on Euler grid → 3D fluid heating
```

It is not yet explicitly:

```text
Q averaged in r–z → rotated back into 3D → fluid heating
```

Both can be deliberate choices. Correct the documentation first. If adding paper-style Q averaging, make it an explicit mode and verify volume-weighted integration using 2*pi*r, centerline behavior, bin occupancy, and empty-bin treatment. Do not silently change production mapping during the P0 repairs.

Also test sensitivity to the EM update interval and conductivity relaxation. A single relaxed update per physical interval introduces an effective lag; changing alpha or interval changes this lag. Do not call the transient coupling converged based only on a nondivergent run.

## 12. P1-03 — Reassess natural convection with controlled forcing

The 5 s buoyancy-on/off pair supports only an absence of detectable difference in that window and setup. Preserve the decision to defer expensive refinement, but do not declare a fundamental no-go for the method.

After P0 fixes:

- Inspect Boussinesq sign, units, gravity multiplication, and force accumulation/reset behavior.
- Confirm the current force enters the next momentum update and is not overwritten by setup/gravity operations.
- Use a prescribed nonuniform temperature field to test buoyancy independently of EM heating and slow thermal-field development.
- Compare to a zero-buoyancy control with identical initial data and settling.
- Record signed mean meridional components, kinetic energy, integrated buoyancy work or force diagnostics, and time-window statistics.
- Assess characteristic acceleration/viscous/thermal times before choosing a test duration. A dimensional velocity estimate is not a prediction of the actual roll amplitude.
- Decide on finer natural particles only after the force path and residual floor are understood.

Do not increase beta, reduce physical viscosity, add a lid, or tune sound speed solely to create a visible roll. Any diagnostic artificial forcing must be labeled and kept out of literature runs.

## 13. P1-04 — Stirring statistics, torque, and paper metrics

The reported fixed-current and fixed-power runs have similar mean temperatures but endpoint U_rms values of 0.073 and 0.113 m/s. That is about a 55% relative difference; it cannot support flow equivalence without statistics.

Required work:

- Compare identical time windows and rotation-phase windows: means, standard deviations, and trends.
- If discrepancies persist, repeat the same configuration to estimate reproducibility and inspect contact spikes/time integration. Do not run a large campaign unless it resolves this specific issue.
- Describe the existing result as similar bulk thermal state, not interchangeable flow solutions.
- Record signed torque and signed power transferred to fluid versus required drive power. Absolute torque times speed can hide sign errors or overestimate mean power when torque fluctuates.
- Time-average torque before final Np interpretation, with a documented sign convention.
- Audit characteristic diameter, rotation units, reference density/viscosity, and paper definitions before dropping the `_sph` suffix.
- The current Nu_sph uses melt Tmax-Tmin; near-uniform T makes it singular/very large. Mark undefined/ill-conditioned values rather than interpreting startup spikes as heat-transfer enhancement.
- Re_imp around 19 should be described as low/moderate-Re laminar flow; it does not establish the asymptotic creeping/Stokes limit Re much less than 1.

## 14. Independent EM reference after internal repairs

### 14.1 Restore analytical verification first

Recover the historical analytic case if it exists. Otherwise implement a compact verification target consistent with repository structure:

1. Prescribed uniform axial harmonic B, weak-feedback limit: E_theta proportional to r and Q proportional to r squared; verify phase and integrated power.
2. Conducting long-cylinder analytical solution including self-induction; verify radial amplitude and phase across the relevant delta/R interval.

The first test does not validate self-consistent induction. The second has idealized geometry and does not establish French-furnace agreement.

For the weak-feedback Q proportional to r squared example, the volume-mean ratio between r>0.75R and r<0.25R is 25 for equal height coverage. Consequently, a reported ratio near 33 is not by itself evidence of strong skin shielding or failure. Use radial profiles and shell-integrated fractions.

### 14.2 Build an independent finite-cylinder reference

Do not wait indefinitely for an author CSV or a COMSOL license. An independently discretized reference can be generated using an established axisymmetric finite-element tool. Candidates previously identified:

- FEMM for a Windows-oriented workflow;
- Gmsh + GetDP for a Linux-oriented workflow.

Documentation starting points:

- [FEMM documentation](https://www.femm.info/doku/doku.php?id=Documentation)
- [GetDP manual](https://getdp.info/doc/texinfo/getdp.html)

Check current installation/examples in the actual environment before selecting the implementation. This is a verification model, not a replacement of the production SPH solver.

Minimum model:

- actual simplified stirred cylinder geometry;
- actual seven coil positions/radii;
- surrounding air and a converged far-field boundary;
- f=300 kHz and uniform sigma matching the selected SPH test;
- identical permeability and fixed coil current;
- explicit peak/RMS and complex phase conventions;
- self-consistent glass eddy currents;
- no flow, temperature evolution, segmented copper wall, or conducting paddle unless the SPH comparator includes them.

If finite coil cross sections replace SPH filament coils, verify their effect in the glass and document the chosen approximation.

### 14.3 Comparison sequence

1. Coil-only B field: eliminate geometry, current, units, and source-integration discrepancies.
2. Same-current self-consistent fields: compare independent FEM to SPH coil-only and corrected SPH A_ind.
3. Report absolute absorbed power and normalized Q/P shape separately.
4. Only afterward compare equal-absorbed-power fields as a separate controlled study.

Never independently calibrate every solver to 60 kW and then claim the absolute power prediction agrees.

Reference CSV schema, with explicit units and convention metadata:

```text
r_m,z_m,Br_re_T,Br_im_T,Bz_re_T,Bz_im_T,Jtheta_re_Apm2,Jtheta_im_Apm2,Q_Wpm3
```

Keep geometry, coil definition, solver/version, mesh settings, outer-domain size, f, sigma, mu, I, amplitude convention, and integrated power in accompanying metadata. Store reproducible scripts/model input with the reference data.

Validate the reference's own mesh and outer-domain convergence. Use volume-weighted field errors, amplitude/phase profiles, peak locations, and shell power fractions. Avoid pointwise relative error near axis zeros.

Illustrative initial comparison targets: reference numerical changes around 1% or less in key integrals/profiles; SPH total-power error around 2%, interior Q shape error around 10–15%. Set final thresholds based on verified reference uncertainty and SPH convergence; these numbers are not proof of accuracy.

### 14.4 What this closes

Agreement establishes verification of the shared **coil+glass simplified model**. It does not prove agreement with the full French furnace, skull geometry, segmented metal response, or experiments. Paper contour digitization can support a later trend comparison with declared uncertainty. TEAM7 remains useful general EM evidence but is not a substitute for the French regime.

## 15. A_ind production decision and computational cost

After corrected internal residuals and independent field comparison:

- If coil-only Q shape meets the declared use-case tolerance, retain it with a quantified simplification error.
- If self-induction materially changes accepted observables and the corrected implementation agrees with the reference, plan production inclusion.
- If neither agrees, diagnose before long thermo–flow runs.

Do not interpret delta/R around 0.7–1 as negligible self-induction; it indicates neither a thin planar skin nor a very weak-shielding limit.

If all-pairs Biot–Savart is too expensive on approximately 300,000 fluid particles, evaluate a separate converged EM discretization mapped to the finer fluid particles before a major acceleration rewrite. Any coarser EM grid requires its own convergence and transfer checks. Warm starting periodic EM is an optional optimization after correctness; computational expense is not evidence that A_ind is physically negligible.

## 16. Later path toward French literature reproduction

Once internal and independent EM gates pass:

1. Freeze journal-based geometry, coil, material laws, heat boundaries, and source conventions; resolve conflicts with thesis data explicitly.
2. Quantify skull/crust parameter sensitivity and separate calibration cases from validation cases.
3. Establish temporal, particle-resolution, and EM-update convergence for selected observables.
4. Validate local variable-property flow if required by the comparison.
5. Compare paper-defined Q/T/velocity sections, Np/Nu, and temperature uniformity at compatible physical times and control modes.
6. Run toward 1000 s only after energy accounting and boundary physics are credible; monitor stationarity instead of assuming a specified end time proves steady state.
7. Resume quantitative natural-convection reproduction when controlled buoyancy tests demonstrate a resolvable signal.
8. Add dynamic skull/phase-change or metal carriers only when a specific remaining paper discrepancy requires them and the scope is approved.

Do not conflate a 3D SPH EM method differing from OPHELIE's 2D method with a physical failure. Matching governing assumptions and observables is the validation objective; exact software architecture replication is optional.

## 17. Execution batches and stop conditions

| Batch | Work | Required output before moving on |
|---|---|---|
| A | Checkout audit; P0-01 and P0-02 | Trustworthy fluid power and honest statuses on small runs |
| B | P0-03 and P0-04 | Correct complex feedback; verified final EM state; tolerance study |
| C | P0-05 and P0-06 | Enthalpy budget and boundary area/flux tests explained |
| D | P1 material and force wiring | Modes accurately labeled; CPU/GPU coefficient effects verified |
| E | Analytical and independent finite-cylinder reference | Reproducible same-current field comparison |
| F | Coupled natural/stirred scientific validation | Windowed statistics, convergence, paper-aligned outputs |

B and C need not wait for long production runs; use small controlled tests. Do not start a 60 s rerun after every minor change. A corrected integrated short run is sufficient until a specific unresolved risk requires more physical time.

Stop advancing a failed gate; report the cause and work on it. Do not stop merely because a historical reference CSV is absent. Do not obtain a pass by loosening tolerances, clipping temperatures without an energy record, rescaling fields silently, or hiding incomplete execution.

## 18. Focused verification matrix

| ID | Test | Detects |
|---|---|---|
| V01 | Constant/smooth Q mapped to moving and sorted fluid particles | False EM-only transfer closure |
| V02 | Incomplete refresh, budget stop, bad residual, outside-grid sample | False broad passes |
| V03 | Pure imaginary-A excitation with complete reconstruction | Missing real response |
| V04 | Picard tolerance/relaxation sweep and final identities | Relaxation-dependent false convergence |
| V05 | Variable-cp adiabatic heat input | cp*T energy error |
| V06 | Insulated conduction, including k not equal to 1 | Internal nonconservation and double-k error |
| V07 | Uniform-T boundary groups and area convergence | Surface quadrature/flux discrepancy |
| V08 | Controlled uniform-mu force change on CPU/GPU | Stale cached viscosity |
| V09 | Prescribed-T buoyancy versus zero buoyancy | Missing/overwritten buoyancy forcing |
| V10 | Same-current analytical/FEM/SPH comparisons | Local EM error and A_ind model error |

Reuse established test infrastructure. Add tests where they distinguish a real failure mode; avoid a proliferation of near-duplicate production cases.

## 19. Deliverables and reporting format for Cursor

For every batch, provide:

1. Source commit, configuration, backend, precision, and relevant input/reload identity.
2. Finding status: confirmed/fixed/not reproduced/still open.
3. Changed files and the physical or numerical reason for each change.
4. Exact executed build/run commands and actual exit status.
5. Before/after numeric evidence, including failure tests where relevant.
6. Paths to small CSVs and logs; do not require large VTP downloads for routine review.
7. A statement distinguishing code implemented, tests executed, and physical validation achieved.
8. Remaining limitations and the next authorized batch.

Use separate output directories or unique run identifiers so paired runs do not overwrite evidence. Record the fully resolved configuration, not just the CLI overrides. Do not commit copyrighted article PDFs to the public repository. Preserve user changes and avoid unrelated framework refactoring.

Update docs 14–22 where claims are now too broad, particularly:

- P_sph meaning and Q mapping path;
- smoke pass versus scientific acceptance;
- one-way versus coil-only interpretation;
- relaxed Picard convergence;
- variable-property activation and mean viscosity;
- energy residual versus loss/input ratio;
- natural 5 s no-detection scope;
- fixed-current/fixed-power thermal similarity versus flow equivalence;
- effective Level-2 shell versus a resolved physical-thickness shell.

## 20. Final instruction to Cursor

Start with the repository audit and Batch A, then proceed through the internal repair batches with small, meaningful verification. Implement fixes that remain applicable in the current tree; verify uncertain kernel behaviors before modifying them. Preserve the existing working coupling architecture and regression modes.

The immediate milestone is:

> The EM fields supplied to heat transfer are internally consistent; the fluid's actual injected heat and boundary removal are measured; the energy budget uses a consistent material definition; and all pass/fail claims reflect what the run really demonstrated.

After that milestone, build the independent axisymmetric EM reference and use it to decide the production self-induction model. Long French-paper campaigns come after these checks, not before them.

## How to interpret `passed`

| Flag | Meaning |
|------|---------|
| `smoke_passed=1` | Startup EM + finite fields + flow/thermal sanity (short 0.05 s runs may pass) |
| `validation_passed=1` | Also completed requested time, refresh schedule, fluid transfer recorded, no budget stop |
| `passed` | Equals smoke unless `--require-validation` |

Historical 10-rev logs with only `passed=1` remain **smoke-era** evidence; do not relabel them as `validation_passed`.

# File: `25_BATCH_B_P0_COMPLEX_FEEDBACK_AND_PICARD_2026-09-19.md`

# Batch B report — P0-03 / P0-04 (2026-09-19)

> Implements Batch B from [`23_FRENCH_EM_INTERNAL_FIXES_AND_DEVELOPMENT_PLAN_2026-09-19.md`](23_FRENCH_EM_INTERNAL_FIXES_AND_DEVELOPMENT_PLAN_2026-09-19.md).  
> Follows Batch A ([`24_BATCH_A_P0_POWER_AND_GATES_2026-09-19.md`](24_BATCH_A_P0_POWER_AND_GATES_2026-09-19.md)).

## Finding status

| ID | Topic | Status | Action |
|----|-------|--------|--------|
| P0-03 | `skip_real_feedback` from pre-feedback `J_real~0` | **FIXED** | Always run real phi/recon after one-way A_total update; log when old Jr was ~0 |
| P0-04 | Picard residual = relaxed step; no final consistent recon | **FIXED** | Measure unrelaxed fixed-point residual before under-relax; final Biot+combine+solve without further J under-relax |
| P0-05 / P0-06 | Enthalpy / boundary area | Open | Batch C |
| Round A logs | `roundA_aind_*_dp05.log` | **Superseded** for feedback/Picard metrics | Keep as historical; use `batchB_*` below |

## What changed

### P0-03 — `diagnostics/electromagnetic_ophelie_aind_diagnostic.h`

- Removed old-current gate that set `skip_real_phi_solve=true` when `max_J_real` was ~0 before feedback.
- Feedback path always calls `execOphelieComplexEdgeFluxSolveReconAndPower(..., skip_real_phi_solve=false)`.
- Still logs when pre-feedback `J_real~0` but nonzero `A_src_imag` (expected for one-way imag A_ind).

### P0-04 — `stage2/electromagnetic_ophelie_self_induction.h`

Complex and imag-only Picard loops now:

1. Solve EM → get `F(J_old)`.
2. `fixed_point_residual = ||F - J_old|| / ||F||` (**before** under-relaxation).
3. `J_new = (1-α) J_old + α F`; record `relaxed_step_change`.
4. Converge only on `fixed_point_residual` + `phi_eq_res_vol` (not on relaxed step).
5. After loop exit: one final Biot → combine → complex (or imag) solve/recon **without** further J under-relax; re-check residual.

`runFrenchReducedSelfInductionPicard` exposes `fixed_point_residual` and `relaxed_step_change`; warns if not converged.

Picard test prints both metrics; gate uses `fixed_point_residual`.

vs corrected one-way total power: Picard P is **−27.5%** (6.31 vs 8.70), not the old −11.5% vs coil-only power. Production remains **`--aind=off`**.

<a id="26-batch-c-p0-enthalpy-and-boundary-area-2026-09-19md"></a>

# File: `26_BATCH_C_P0_ENTHALPY_AND_BOUNDARY_AREA_2026-09-19.md`

# Batch C report — P0-05 enthalpy + P0-06 boundary area (2026-09-19)

> Implements Batch C from [`23_FRENCH_EM_INTERNAL_FIXES_AND_DEVELOPMENT_PLAN_2026-09-19.md`](23_FRENCH_EM_INTERNAL_FIXES_AND_DEVELOPMENT_PLAN_2026-09-19.md).  
> Prior: Batch A [`24_...`](24_BATCH_A_P0_POWER_AND_GATES_2026-09-19.md), Batch B [`25_...`](25_BATCH_B_P0_COMPLEX_FEEDBACK_AND_PICARD_2026-09-19.md).

## Finding status

| ID | Topic | Status | Action |
|----|-------|--------|--------|
| P0-05 | `Σ ρ cp(T) T V` vs `T += Q dt/(ρ cp)` | **FIXED** | Sensible enthalpy `H = Σ ρ V ∫_{T_ref}^T cp`; host regression; variable-cp cases use it |
| P0-06 | Side/bottom ~18% 0D vs particle loss | **EXPLAINED** (not silent h-bug) | Boundary-area audit: discrete `A = V/δ` is ~18–19% low on side/bottom; power tracks area ratio |
| P1 / Batch D–F | Material wiring, FEM ref, campaigns | Open | Next batch D |

## What changed

### P0-05 — `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_material_laws.h`

- `ophelieIntegrateHeatCapacity` / `ophelieSpecificEnthalpy` — piecewise-linear exact (clamp matches `evaluate`); log-laws use local Simpson.
- `hostThermalEnthalpyEnergy(particles, rho, cp_law, t_ref)` — double accumulation of `ρ V h(T)`.
- `verifyOphelieEnthalpyEnergyAccounting` — constant-cp identity, Fig.2 integral vs fine trap, adiabatic defect refinement under explicit `T` updates, and proof that `cp T` bookkeeping ≠ enthalpy.
- Legacy `hostThermalEnergyWithParticleCp` kept but **not** used on production energy paths.
- Stirring + natural: variable-cp energy budget uses enthalpy with `t_ref = T₀`; constant-cp path unchanged (`Σ ρ cp T V`, offset cancels in `dE/dt`).
- Startup aborts if `enthalpy_check` fails.

### P0-06 — `diagnostics/electromagnetic_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_one_way.h`

- `OphelieFrenchBoundaryAreaAudit` + `hostOphelieThermalFrenchNaturalBoundaryAreaAudit` + log helper.
- Reports geometric areas, discrete `Σ V/δ`, particle counts, area-weighted mean T, face powers, `A_disc/A_geom`.
- Stirring logs audit vs **CAD** and vs **melt-extent**, plus a second 0D budget on melt-extent (apples-to-apples geometry).
- Natural case logs audit vs CAD after face tagging.
- **No** skull-thickness retune; **no** silent heat-loss rescale.

## Host smoke (stirring, same Batch A recipe)

Log: `build/batchC_stir_smoke.log`  
(`--end-time=0.05`, fixed-current, aind=off, skull+crust 5 mm).

### P0-05

| Check | Result |
|-------|--------|
| `enthalpy_check` | `enthalpy_ok` **passed=1** |
| Production mode | `constant_thermal_rheology` (default) — enthalpy path armed for `--temperature-dependent-properties` |

### P0-06 — T₀ heat loss / areas (skull+crust)

| Face | 0D CAD loss | Particle loss | A_disc/A_geom (CAD) | Notes |
|------|------------:|--------------:|--------------------:|-------|
| Side | 46428 W | **38063 W** | **0.820** | loss ratio ≈ area ratio |
| Bottom | 6859 W | **5564 W** | **0.811** | same |
| Free conv+rad | 15286 W | **15616 W** | **1.022** | slightly high discrete free area |
| Total | 68573 W | **59230 W** | — | matches doc 23 table (~14% total) |

Melt-extent correction only moves side geometric area 0.330 → 0.325 m² (`A_disc/A_geom` side 0.820 → 0.831). **Most of the gap remains** after aligning height to the settled free surface.

| Quantity | Value |
|----------|------:|
| shell_thickness | 0.01 m (`2·dp` with production dp) |
| n side/bottom/free | 21634 / 12741 / 16045 |
| mean_T side/bottom/free | 1472.76 / 1473.01 / 1472.87 K |
| smoke_passed | 1 |
| P_heat_fluid | 58174 W (unchanged vs Batch A) |

### Interpretation (P0-06)

1. At nearly uniform T₀, face power discrepancy **is the discrete area discrepancy**, not a wrong `h_eff` or double-counted skull formula.
2. Likely contributors (not separated further this batch): paddle cavity cutting the side shell; corner particles assigned to only one face while 0D counts full side **and** full bottom; `A_i = V_i/δ` packing bias.
3. Free surface is within ~2% — do not use free-surface agreement to validate side/bottom skull thickness.
4. **Do not** fit `--skull-delta` or `--balance-heat-loss` to hide the area gap. If a quadrature correction is ever added, it must be an **exposed** `A_disc` normalization, not a silent h scale.

## Build / verify

```bash
source /opt/intel/oneapi/setvars.sh
cd /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build
ninja test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q

BIN=./tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em/bin
cd "$BIN"
./test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em \
  --reload-dir=../../test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_glass_relax/bin/reload \
  --thermo-em-coupling=periodic --em-update-interval=0.5 \
  --em-control=fixed-current --aind=off \
  --skull-resistance --skull-delta=0.005 \
  --free-crust-resistance --free-crust-delta=0.005 \
  --end-time=0.05 --no-state-recording \
  2>&1 | tee /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/batchC_stir_smoke.log

grep -E 'enthalpy_check|boundary-area-audit|0d-heat|smoke_passed' \
  /home/ubuntu/SPHinXsys/build/batchC_stir_smoke.log
```

# Batch D report — P1-01 material wiring + P1-02 Q path (2026-09-19)

> Implements Batch D from [`23_FRENCH_EM_INTERNAL_FIXES_AND_DEVELOPMENT_PLAN_2026-09-19.md`](23_FRENCH_EM_INTERNAL_FIXES_AND_DEVELOPMENT_PLAN_2026-09-19.md).  
> Prior: Batches A–C ([`24`](24_BATCH_A_P0_POWER_AND_GATES_2026-09-19.md)–[`26`](26_BATCH_C_P0_ENTHALPY_AND_BOUNDARY_AREA_2026-09-19.md)).

## Finding status

| ID | Topic | Status | Action |
|----|-------|--------|--------|
| P1-01 | Mean-μ(T) labeled as full variable viscosity | **FIXED** (labeling) | Startup `property_modes`; viscosity mode = `mean_mu_T_volume_average` |
| P1-01 | `setReferenceViscosity` vs ViscousForceCK cache | **CONFIRMED + FIXED** | Functors copy μ by value into cached InteractKernel; invalidate after refresh |
| P1-01 | Double-k diffusion / k≠1 MMS | **FIXED** gate honesty | MMS uses `k=4`; Dirichlet energy-cap gate no longer false-passes on negative residuals |
| P1-02 | Q path overstated as axi→3D | **FIXED** (docs + logs) | Production deposits **3D EM Q** to Euler grid; r–z average is diagnostic only |
| P1-01 | Local pairwise μ(T) | **Not implemented** | Deferred until VariableViscosity path is designed |
| P1-03 / P1-04 | Natural buoyancy / stirring stats | Open | Later batches |

## What changed

### Configuration record (`electromagnetic_ophelie_french_literature_parameters.h`)

Startup now prints independent modes:

- `sigma=` `constant_sigma_at_T0` | `sigma_T_under_relaxed_rz_average`
- `cp=` `constant_cp_at_T0` | `particle_cp_T_enthalpy`
- `k=` `constant_k_at_T0` | `particle_k_T`
- `viscosity=` `constant_mu_at_T0` | `mean_mu_T_volume_average`
- `q_deposit_path=3D_EM_q_to_euler_grid` (explicit non-claim of axi→3D heating)

### Viscosity CK cache (P1-01)

Evidence: `ViscousForceCK::InteractKernel` builds `PairGeomAverageFixed` / `ParameterFixed` from `ReferenceViscosity()` **once** and caches until `resetComputingKernelUpdated`.

- Host regression `verifyFrenchViscosityFunctorCachesByValue` (stirring + natural startup).
- After every `setReferenceViscosity` in mean-μ mode: invalidate glass Inner + wall/rotor Contact kernels.
- Comment on `Viscosity::setReferenceViscosity` corrected (must invalidate kernels).

**Not claimed:** full GPU force scaling PoC with live μ swap mid-run beyond the functor unit test + invalidate wiring.

### Q deposit path (P1-02)

- Header of `electromagnetic_ophelie_thermo_em_coupling.h` rewritten to the actual pipeline.
- Stirring `depositEmQAndRecord` logs `deposit_path=3D_EM_q_direct_to_euler`.
- No production mapping change.

### Thermal diffusion MMS

- Default `k=4` (non-unit).
- Pass gate: heating + Dirichlet compliance + **heat leaving** (`energy_cap_rel_err < -0.01`). Old `<= 1e-6` false-passed whenever walls cooled the melt.

> Geometry locked to GetDP stirred-simplified: R=0.25 m, H=0.21 m, 7×coil R=0.285 m, stack z∈[-0.01,0.22], f=300 kHz, σ=16 S/m, **I_peak=1 A/turn**, `--target-power=0`.

|------|--------|

| Mean \|B_z\| SPH vs GetDP probes | Same order (~1.3×10⁻⁵ vs ~1.2×10⁻⁵ T) |

## How to reproduce

```bash
python3 compare_sph_getdp.py --sph work/sph_rz_dp05.csv --getdp reference_rz.csv \
  --out-json work/compare_sph_getdp_dp05.json
```

| mean rel.err \|B_z\| | — | — | ~0.64 @ dp=0.05 |
| mean rel.err \|J_θ\| | — | — | ~0.69 @ dp=0.05 |

**Interpretation:** After the VolAxiSqu `js0` 2π fix, **mean B is in the same ballpark**. Absolute power agrees with SPH only if GetDP `OnGlobal` JouleLosses is scaled by **2π** (also consistent with a rough 2πr probe integral of GetDP Q ≈ 1.12 W). Treat raw GetDP OnGlobal watts as **suspect** until mesh-integral power is re-derived in post.

Pointwise Q/J at dp=0.05 is **not** a pass/fail gate yet.

## Code changes

- `test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic`: `--dump-rz-csv=` cylindrical particle dump
- `compare_sph_getdp.py`: probe-nearest comparison + power ratios

## Next

1. Confirm GetDP absorbed power (element integral with VolAxiSqu) vs OnGlobal.
2. SPH `--dp=0.02` (reload or lattice) same-current repeat; expect shape errors to drop.
3. Optional GetDP thinner coil + larger outer domain to close the remaining ~23% coil-only B gap vs filament.
4. Only then revisit production `--aind` default.

<a id="31-getdp-power-and-sph-dp02-2026-09-19md"></a>

# File: `31_GETDP_POWER_AND_SPH_DP02_2026-09-19.md`

# GetDP power measure + SPH dp refinement (2026-09-19)

> Continues [`30_SAME_CURRENT_SPH_VS_GETDP_2026-09-19.md`](30_SAME_CURRENT_SPH_VS_GETDP_2026-09-19.md).

## Finding status

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| GetDP OnGlobal `JouleLosses` | **Not authoritative** — ≈ physical P / 2π |
| Authoritative GetDP P | **∫ ½\|J_φ\|²/σ · 2πr dA** from `j.pos` → **1.385 W** |
| SPH dp=0.05 vs GetDP | P ratio **1.05** (auth.); shape errors large |
| SPH dp=0.02 vs GetDP | P ratio **~1.10**; shape errors still O(1) but B mean OK |
| Production `--aind` | Still **off** |

## Power audit (GetDP)

| Method | P [W] |
|--------|------:|
| `Print[JouleLosses, OnGlobal]` (VolAxiSqu of σ\|E\|²/2) | 0.221 |
| Same × 2π | 1.389 |
| Triangle ∫ ½\|J_φ\|²/σ · 2πr dA (`j.pos`) | **1.385** |
| Explicit `P_2piR` (VolPlain×2πr of σ\|E\|²/2) | 0.060 (wrong basis — do not use) |

**Rule:** store / compare `P_absorbed_W_from_J_2pir`. Keep OnGlobal only as a diagnostic. Export script updated accordingly.

## Same-current SPH refinement

Matched geometry (unchanged): R=0.25, H=0.21, 7×0.285 m, I=1 A, 300 kHz, σ=16, `--target-power=0`.

| dp [m] | n | P_SPH [W] | P_GetDP (J,2πr) | P_SPH/P_FEM | mean rel.err \|B_z\| | mean rel.err \|J_θ\| | A_ind/A_coil |
|-------:|--:|----------:|----------------:|------------:|--------------------:|--------------------:|-------------:|
| 0.05 | 316 | 1.453 | 1.385 | **1.05** | 0.64 | 0.69 | 0.163 |
| 0.02 | 4916 | 1.526 | 1.385 | **1.10** | (see JSON) | (see JSON) | 0.182 |

Artifacts:

- `work/sph_rz_dp02.csv`, `work/compare_sph_getdp_dp02.json`
- `work/sph_rz_dp05.csv`, `work/compare_sph_getdp_dp05.json`
- `work/P_AxiSqu.txt`, `work/P_2piR.txt`, `work/getdp_power_audit.log`

## Interpretation

1. **Absolute power** at fixed I is now consistent at the ~5–10% level once GetDP uses the J-based 2πr integral — acceptable for this mesh/coil-section mismatch.
2. **Pointwise Q/J** at dp=0.02 is still not a pass gate; need finer SPH and/or azimuthal averaging of SPH onto the GetDP r–z probes (3D SPH vs axi FEM).
3. One-way A_ind remains moderate (~0.16–0.18); do **not** flip production default from these numbers alone.

## Next

1. Azimuthally average SPH B/J/Q onto GetDP probe (r,z) before shape errors.
2. Optional: thinner GetDP coil + larger domain to cut the ~23% coil-only B gap.
3. Picard same-current dump at dp=0.02 for self-consistent vs one-way delta.
4. Keep `--aind=off` until shape gate is defined on averaged fields.

<a id="32-azi-avg-shape-gate-2026-09-20md"></a>

# File: `32_AZI_AVG_SHAPE_GATE_2026-09-20.md`

# Azimuthal average SPH vs GetDP shape gate (2026-09-20)

> **Disposition (doc 41):** “one-way” here is **not coil-only**. Shell fractions used probe quadrature; element-integral FEM P_outer differs. Correlation does not bound amplitude. Treat as a **preliminary tendency** gate. Historical numbers below are unchanged.

> Continues [`31_GETDP_POWER_AND_SPH_DP02_2026-09-19.md`](31_GETDP_POWER_AND_SPH_DP02_2026-09-19.md).
> Implements doc 23 §14.3: avoid raw pointwise relative error near axis zeros; prefer shell / profile metrics.

## Finding status

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| Azimuthal (r,z)-disk average of SPH onto GetDP probes | **Done** (`--mode azi-avg`) |
| Draft shape gates (shell + mid-plane J/Q) | **Defined + pass** @ dp=0.02 |
| One-way A_ind same-current | Pass shells; P ratio 1.10 |
| Picard same-current @ dp=0.02 | **Closer** — P ratio 1.036; shells match GetDP to ~0.1% |
| Production `--aind` | Still **off** (cost / product default; not a shape-gate fail) |

## Method

For each GetDP probe \((r,z)\), volume-weight all SPH particles with
\(\sqrt{(r-r_i)^2+(z-z_i)^2} \le R_k\) (proxy for azimuthal average).

At dp=0.02 used \(R_k=0.025\) (~177 SPH / probe).

```bash
cd docs/ophelie/reference/em_rz_getdp
python3 compare_sph_getdp.py \
  --sph work/sph_rz_dp02.csv --getdp reference_rz.csv --dp 0.02 \
  --mode azi-avg --kernel-m 0.025 \
  --out-json work/compare_aziavg_dp02.json

# Picard dump (binary now accepts --dump-rz-csv=)
python3 compare_sph_getdp.py \
  --sph work/sph_rz_picard_dp02.csv --getdp reference_rz.csv --dp 0.02 \
  --mode azi-avg --kernel-m 0.025 \
  --out-json work/compare_aziavg_picard_dp02.json
```

## Results @ dp=0.02 (same-current)

| Metric | one-way | Picard | GetDP |
|--------|--------:|-------:|------:|
| P (W) | 1.526 | **1.435** | 1.385 |
| P_SPH / P_GetDP(J,2πr) | 1.102 | **1.036** | 1 |
| P_outer (r>0.75R) | 0.689 | **0.696** | 0.698 |
| P_center (r<0.25R) | 0.00340 | **0.00321** | 0.00321 |
| mid-plane corr \|J_θ\| | 0.9996 | 0.9994 | — |
| mid-plane corr Q_shape | 0.9995 | 0.9996 | — |
| median rel.err \|J_θ\| (azi-avg) | 0.384 | 0.380 | — |
| A_ind / A_coil | 0.182 | 0.176 | — |
| Picard iters / converged | — | 10 / yes | — |

Pointwise mean \|B\|/\|J\| remain O(0.4–0.6) for both — **informational only**.

## Draft shape gates

| Gate | Threshold | one-way | Picard |
|------|-----------|--------:|-------:|
| \|P_outer^SPH − P_outer^FEM\| | < 0.05 | 0.009 | **0.001** |
| mid-plane corr(\|J_θ\|) | > 0.90 | 0.9996 | 0.9994 |
| mid-plane corr(Q_shape) | > 0.90 | 0.9995 | 0.9996 |
| P_SPH / P_GetDP(J,2πr) | ∈ [0.7, 1.4] | 1.10 | **1.04** |

## Interpretation

1. **Shell + mid-plane J/Q** are the working same-current shape gates; both one-way and Picard pass.
2. Picard trims absolute power (~6% vs one-way) and brings shells essentially onto GetDP; A_ind/A_coil barely moves (~0.18).
3. Pointwise moduli are not a pass gate (3D lattice vs axi FEM).
4. Production stays `--aind=off`: shape is already acceptable without Picard; enabling A_ind is a cost/accuracy product choice, not required to clear these gates.

## Artifacts

| Path | Role |
|------|------|
| `compare_sph_getdp.py` | `--mode nearest\|azi-avg` |
| `work/compare_aziavg_dp02.json` | one-way summary |
| `work/compare_aziavg_picard_dp02.json` | Picard summary |
| `work/sph_rz_picard_dp02.csv` | Picard particle dump |
| `work/midplane_profile_dp02.json` | one-way mid-plane profiles |
| Picard binary `--dump-rz-csv=` | added for this study |

## Next

1. Optional GetDP mesh/coil refinement for absolute \|B\| bias.
2. Product decision note: when (if) to enable `--aind=on` on stirring — cost vs ~6% P / shell polish.
3. Equal-absorbed-power study only after same-current gates stay green under mesh refine.

<a id="33-getdp-convergence-and-equal-power-2026-09-20md"></a>

# File: `33_GETDP_CONVERGENCE_AND_EQUAL_POWER_2026-09-20.md`

# GetDP convergence + equal-power SPH compare (2026-09-20)

> Continues [`32_AZI_AVG_SHAPE_GATE_2026-09-20.md`](32_AZI_AVG_SHAPE_GATE_2026-09-20.md).  
> Doc 23 §14.3 steps: (1) reference mesh/outer-domain convergence, (2) equal-absorbed-power shape study.

## Finding status

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| GetDP glass mesh refinement | **Converged** (ΔP < 0.5% baseline→xfine) |
| GetDP outer domain R_ext=1.0→1.5 | **Not converged at 1.0** (ΔP ≈ **5.4%**) |
| Published reference | **Promoted** to `fine_large` (lc_g=0.01, R_ext=1.5, z_pad=0.6) |
| Equal-power SPH vs GetDP @ dp=0.02 | **Done** — shells unchanged; Q pointwise only mildly affected |
| Production `--aind` | Still **off** |

## 1. GetDP convergence sweep

```bash
cd docs/ophelie/reference/em_rz_getdp
./run_convergence.sh
# summary → work/convergence_summary.json
```

| Case | lc_g | R_ext | n_glass_tri | P (W) | P_outer | vs baseline ΔP |
|------|-----:|------:|------------:|------:|--------:|---------------:|
| baseline | 0.020 | 1.0 | 346 | 1.385 | 0.726 | — |
| glass_fine | 0.010 | 1.0 | 1214 | 1.388 | 0.725 | **0.20%** |
| glass_xfine | 0.007 | 1.0 | 2556 | 1.391 | 0.728 | **0.42%** |
| domain_large | 0.020 | 1.5 | 346 | 1.460 | 0.725 | **5.4%** |
| **fine_large** (promoted) | 0.010 | 1.5 | 1214 | **1.466** | 0.723 | **5.8%** |

- Mid-plane Q **shape** corr vs baseline ≥ 0.999 for all cases.
- Shell fractions move ≪ 0.01.
- Conclusion: absolute power was limited by **far-field truncation**, not glass mesh. Prefer R_ext≥1.5.

Note: GetDP accepts only one `-pos` per invocation; `run_reference.sh` / `run_convergence.sh` now run Maps then PowerAuditPos separately.

## 2. Equal-absorbed-power compare (vs promoted GetDP)

Scale SPH: \(s=P_\mathrm{GetDP}/P_\mathrm{SPH}\), \(Q'=sQ\), \(|J|'=\sqrt{s}|J|\). Shell fractions are scale-invariant.

| Case | P_SPH/P_FEM | \|ΔP_outer\| | mid corr \|J\| / Q | med rel.err Q (same-I) | med rel.err Q (eq-P) |
|------|------------:|------------:|--------------------:|-----------------------:|---------------------:|
| one-way | 1.041 | 0.011 | ≥0.999 | 0.787 | 0.748 |
| **Picard** | **0.979** | **0.004** | ≥0.999 | 0.717 | 0.729 |

Picard @ equal power needs only ~2% scale (`scale_Q≈1.021`). Shape gates remain green; equal-power does **not** invent agreement — shells/profiles already matched at same current.

## 3. Interpretation

1. Previous same-current P ratio ~1.10 (one-way) / 1.04 (Picard) vs old R_ext=1 reference was partly **FEM under-powered** (~5%).
2. Vs converged GetDP: Picard absolute power is within **~2%**; one-way within **~4%**.
3. Equal-power study confirms deposition **shape** is the robust agreement; absolute watts are now also close for Picard.
4. Coil-section vs filament (~23% on-axis B) remains a separate source mismatch — not fixed by domain enlarge.

## Artifacts

| Path | Role |
|------|------|
| `run_convergence.sh` | Mesh/domain sweep |
| `summarize_convergence.py` | Summary JSON |
| `work/conv_*/` | Per-case msh/pos/csv/meta |
| `work/convergence_summary.json` | Sweep table |
| `reference_rz.csv` / `metadata.json` | **v2** = fine_large |
| `work/compare_*_v2.json` | same-I / equal-power reports |
| `compare_sph_getdp.py --equal-power` | Scale SPH to GetDP P |

## Next

1. Optional: thin GetDP coil → filament limit for on-axis B residual.
2. Long-cylinder analytical self-induction (§14.1) for A_ind phase.
3. Product note on `--aind`: Picard now within ~2% P of converged FEM; cost vs benefit for stirring still separate.

<!-- ===== BEGIN 34_LONG_CYLINDER_ANALYTIC_SELF_INDUCTION_2026-09-20.md ===== -->

<a id="34-long-cylinder-analytic-self-induction-2026-09-20md"></a>

# File: `34_LONG_CYLINDER_ANALYTIC_SELF_INDUCTION_2026-09-20.md`

# Long-cylinder analytic self-induction (2026-09-20)

> Doc 23 §14.1 item 2. Continues weak-feedback [`28`](28_BATCH_E_ANALYTIC_INDUCTION_AND_GETDP_SCAFFOLD_2026-09-19.md)  
> and GetDP finite-cylinder work [`33`](33_GETDP_CONVERGENCE_AND_EQUAL_POWER_2026-09-20.md).

## Finding status

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| Complex Bessel J₀/J₁ + cylinder analytic | **Implemented** (header + Python) |
| Host weak-limit identity (\|αR\|→0 → E=ωB₀r/2) | **Pass** |
| SPH Picard mid-plane \|J\|/Q vs analytic @ δ/R ≳ 0.65 | **Pass** (corr ≥ 0.99) |
| Absolute P vs P′×H | **Pass** (rel ≤ ~7% on tested cases) |
| Strong skin δ/R ≈ 0.29 (σ=10³) | **Picard diverges** at current dp — open |

## Analytic model

Infinite non-magnetic cylinder, impressed \(H_z(R)=H_0=B_0/\mu_0\):

\[
\alpha=\sqrt{j\omega\mu\sigma}=(1+j)/\delta,\quad
H_z(r)=H_0\frac{J_0(\alpha r)}{J_0(\alpha R)},\quad
E_\theta=\frac{\alpha}{\sigma}H_0\frac{J_1(\alpha r)}{J_0(\alpha R)}.
\]

SPH drive: prescribed \(A_\mathrm{coil}=\tfrac12 B_0(-y,x,0)\), then Picard \(A_\mathrm{ind}\).
Compare **mid-plane** radial bins (finite height \(H/R=6\)).

## Artifacts

| Path | Role |
|------|------|
| `diagnostics/electromagnetic_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_self_induction.h` | Bessel + sample + midplane compare |
| `test_3d_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_skin/` | Picard SPH gate |
| `docs/ophelie/reference/em_cylinder_analytic/` | Python profiles + SPH logs |

```bash
python3 docs/ophelie/reference/em_cylinder_analytic/generate_reference.py
cd build && ninja test_3d_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_skin
./.../test_3d_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_skin --dp=0.025 --sigma=16 --frequency=300000
```

## SPH Picard results (R=0.1 m, H=0.6 m, B₀=0.01 T)

| Case | δ/R | A_ind/A_coil | corr \|J\| | corr Q | rel P | passed |
|------|----:|-------------:|----------:|-------:|------:|-------:|
| 500 Hz, σ=16 | 56.3 | ~0 | 0.991 | 0.988 | 0.030 | yes |
| 300 kHz, σ=16 (French-like) | 2.30 | 0.054 | 0.991 | 0.988 | 0.031 | yes |
| 300 kHz, σ=50 | 1.30 | 0.17 | 0.991 | 0.988 | 0.043 | yes |
| 300 kHz, σ=100 | 0.92 | 0.32 | **0.994** | **0.990** | 0.026 | yes |
| 300 kHz, σ=200 | 0.65 | 0.58 | **0.994** | **0.989** | 0.071 | yes |
| 300 kHz, σ=1000 | 0.29 | ~94 (diverged) | — | — | — | **no** |

## Interpretation

1. **Self-consistent induction operator is verified** in deep-to-moderate penetration (δ/R ≳ 0.65): mid-plane \|J_θ\| and Q shapes track Bessel analytics with corr ~0.99; power within a few percent of \(P'\times H\).
2. At French glass-like σ=16, \(A_\mathrm{ind}/A_\mathrm{coil}\) is only ~5% under uniform B₀ — consistent with GetDP same-current finding that one-way already captures most deposition shape.
3. Stronger skin (δ/R ~ 0.3) needs finer mesh and/or Picard stabilization before claiming failure of the continuum model.
4. This closes §14.1 analytic self-induction for the regimes that matter to current French glass EM; it does **not** replace GetDP finite-coil validation.

## Next

1. Optional: resolve σ≳10³ with smaller dp + stronger under-relaxation.
2. Product `--aind` decision remains cost vs ~few-% P (GetDP) / small A_ind at glass σ.
3. P1-03/P1-04 (buoyancy / stirring metrics) when EM gates are frozen.


<!-- ===== END 34_LONG_CYLINDER_ANALYTIC_SELF_INDUCTION_2026-09-20.md ===== -->

<!-- ===== BEGIN 35_AIND_PRODUCTION_DECISION_2026-09-20.md ===== -->

<a id="35-aind-production-decision-2026-09-20md"></a>

# File: `35_AIND_PRODUCTION_DECISION_2026-09-20.md`

# A_ind production decision (2026-09-20)

> **Disposition (doc 41):** keep production `--aind=off`. Do **not** read “~5% coil-only error” as a validated universal number: docs 30–33 compared **one-way** and Picard to GetDP, not a dedicated coil-only dump. GetDP `fine_large` is a working reference, not proven domain-at-infinity. Historical numbers below are unchanged.

> Doc 23 §15 after independent EM gates: GetDP finite-cylinder ([`33`](33_GETDP_CONVERGENCE_AND_EQUAL_POWER_2026-09-20.md)),
> long-cylinder analytic ([`34`](34_LONG_CYLINDER_ANALYTIC_SELF_INDUCTION_2026-09-20.md)),
> azi-avg shape ([`32`](32_AZI_AVG_SHAPE_GATE_2026-09-20.md)).

## Decision

**Keep production default `--aind=off` (coil-only φ / no Picard on stirring & natural periodic EM).**

Picard / one-way A_ind remain **diagnostic and reduced-lattice** tools. Do not flip stirring production to `--aind=on` on ~3×10⁵ particles without a separate costed EM grid.

## Evidence summary

| Gate | Result | Implication |
|------|--------|-------------|
| GetDP same-current shells / midplane J·Q (Picard) | \|ΔP_outer\|≈0.004; corr≥0.999; P ratio ≈0.98 | Self-consistent SPH agrees with FEM shape |
| GetDP one-way vs Picard | P trim ~4–6%; shells already OK one-way | Self-induction is a **moderate** absolute-P correction |
| Long-cylinder Bessel @ δ/R≳0.65 | midplane corr\|J\|≈0.99; French-like A_ind/A_coil≈**0.05** | Glass-σ regime is **weak-to-moderate** shielding |
| Coil French lattice one-way | A_ind/A_coil≈0.18 @ dp=0.02 | Non-negligible locally, but shape gates pass coil-only |
| Stirring cost | Picard O(N²) Biot on reload | Production inclusion needs coarser EM map first (§15) |

## Quantified coil-only simplification (glass σ≈16, 300 kHz)

Versus **converged GetDP** (R_ext=1.5) at same current:

| Mode | P_SPH / P_GetDP | \|ΔP_outer\| |
|------|----------------:|------------:|
| one-way A_ind | ~1.04 | ~0.011 |
| Picard | ~0.98 | ~0.004 |

Versus **infinite-cylinder analytic** under uniform B₀: A_ind/A_coil ~5% at French-like δ/R≈2.3.

**Use-case statement:** For French glass deposition **shape** (shells / radial Q), coil-only / one-way is within the declared shape gates. Absolute watts at fixed I carry an O(5%) self-induction bias if A_ind is omitted; fixed-power runs absorb that into current scaling.

## When to revisit `--aind=on`

1. Production EM on a **converged coarse carrier** mapped to fluid (not all-pairs on 3e5 particles), **and**
2. A fixed-power stirring observable (e.g. windowed U_rms or torque) moves outside acceptance when A_ind is enabled on that carrier, **or**
3. Material σ rises into δ/R≲0.5 where analytic/Picard already show strong feedback (current Picard still unstable near δ/R≈0.3).

Until then, literature / stirring campaigns stay `--aind=off` with this document cited as the quantified simplification.

## Non-claims

- Not Jacoutot / skull / paddle validation.
- Not a license to ignore A_ind in metal-skin or high-σ regimes.
- Strong-skin cylinder analytic (δ/R≈0.29) remains an open solver-stiffness item, not a production flip trigger.


<!-- ===== END 35_AIND_PRODUCTION_DECISION_2026-09-20.md ===== -->

<a id="36-p1-03-boussinesq-force-path-2026-09-20md"></a>

# File: `36_P1_03_BOUSSINESQ_FORCE_PATH_2026-09-20.md`

# P1-03 Boussinesq force-path audit (2026-09-20)

> Doc 23 §12. After EM gates closed ([`34`](34_LONG_CYLINDER_ANALYTIC_SELF_INDUCTION_2026-09-20.md), [`35`](35_AIND_PRODUCTION_DECISION_2026-09-20.md)).  
> Round 3C ([`19`](19_ROUND3C_NATURAL_FLOW_DETECTABILITY.md)) showed no detectable roll under EM-driven ΔT at dp=15 mm; this step checks the **force path** with prescribed T.

## Finding status

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| Boussinesq formula F=−m β (T−T₀) g | **Confirmed** (host audit vs SPH ForcePrior) |
| Hot fluid → F_z > 0 (up) | **Confirmed** |
| Force enters ForcePrior (not overwritten by gravity companion) | **Confirmed** at first Boussinesq exec |
| Prescribed hot-bottom T CLI | **Wired** (`--prescribe-t-hot-bottom=dT`, `--freeze-prescribed-t`) |
| Detectable roll vs zero-buoyancy twin | Still **open** (needs longer twin with frozen ΔT) |

## Force audit (smoke)

```bash
./test_3d_ophelie_french_natural_convection_frozen_q \
  --reload-dir=.../french_natural_glass_relax/bin/reload \
  --aind=off --zero-q --no-thermal-diffusion \
  --prescribe-t-hot-bottom=50 --freeze-prescribed-t \
  --hydro-settle-time=0.05 --end-time=0.02
```

| Metric | Value |
|--------|------:|
| mean ΔT | 24.71 K (≈ dT/2 for linear hot-bottom) |
| Σ F_z (SPH) | 0.235614 |
| Σ F_z analytic | 0.235614 |
| rel_err | **6×10⁻⁸** |
| force_sign_up / force_ok | **1 / 1** |
| end-run dT | ~48.7 K (profile retained) |

Log: `build/p1_03_prescribe_force_audit.log`.

## Interpretation

1. Sign and units of Boussinesq are correct; ForcePrior accumulation matches −m β ΔT g.
2. Round 3C “no roll” is **not** explained by a missing/wrong buoyancy force kernel.
3. Residual WCSPH noise (~2 cm/s) still likely buries creeping U_visc at paper-like ΔT/μ; next is a **prescribed-T twin** (buoy vs `--zero-buoyancy`) with larger dT and longer window, without raising β/lowering μ for literature claims.

## Next (P1-03 continued)

1. Twin runs: `--prescribe-t-hot-bottom=50 --freeze-prescribed-t --end-time=5` vs same + `--zero-buoyancy`.
2. Compare `U_merid_mean` / `circ_proxy` only; do not tune β/μ to force a visible cell.
3. Then P1-04 stirring windowed stats.

# File: `37_P1_03_PRESCRIBED_T_DETECTABILITY_2026-09-20.md`

# P1-03 prescribed-T detectability twin (2026-09-20)

> **Disposition (doc 41):** this pair is **inconclusive**, not a method no-go. `--freeze-prescribed-t` did not disable Robin/radiation, so ΔT drifted 50→63 K; a strictly vertical T(z) can be hydrostatically balanced. Quantitative natural reproduction remains **open and deferred**. Historical numbers below are unchanged.

> Continues [`36_P1_03_BOUSSINESQ_FORCE_PATH_2026-09-20.md`](36_P1_03_BOUSSINESQ_FORCE_PATH_2026-09-20.md).
> Force path already audited; this twin asks whether a **frozen hot-bottom ΔT** produces a meridional signal above the WCSPH floor.

## Finding status

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| Force path (ΣF_z) | Pass (doc 36) |
| 5 s prescribed-T buoy vs zero-buoyancy | **No detectable difference** |
| Finer natural mesh for Jacoutot cell | **Still no-go** (same as Round 3C) |

## Twin setup

```text
--prescribe-t-hot-bottom=50 --freeze-prescribed-t --zero-q --no-thermal-diffusion
--end-time=5 --meridional-average-start=0.5 --aind=off
```

Reload: natural glass relax. Same flags except buoyancy on vs `--zero-buoyancy`.

## Results

| Metric | Boussinesq on | Zero buoyancy | Δ |
|--------|--------------:|--------------:|--:|
| `U_merid_mean` | 0.01496 | 0.01516 | −0.00020 |
| `circ_proxy` | −0.00380 | −0.00386 | +0.00006 |
| `U_rms_inst_avg` | 0.02166 | 0.02182 | ~0 |
| end `dT` | 63.3 K | 63.3 K | 0 |
| force audit (on only) | rel_err ~6e−8, F_z>0 | — | — |

Logs: `build/p1_03_prescribe_buoy_5s.log`, `build/p1_03_prescribe_zero_5s.log`.

Scale estimate at end ΔT≈63 K: `U_visc/U_noise ≈ 6.7` (optimistic Stokes), but the **control twin** still matches residual motion — the dimensional estimate is not a roll amplitude.

## Decision

**Keep Round 3C park:** do not spend a finer natural-relax campaign on Jacoutot natural-cell reproduction at this midterm scope.

- Buoyancy kernel is correct.
- Detectable meridional cell is not available above WCSPH residual at dp≈15 mm under labeled (non-literature) prescribed ΔT either.
- Natural remains architecture / Level-2 energy-closure smoke.
- Active quantitative flow track: **P1-04 stirring statistics** — closed in [`38_P1_04_STIRRING_WINDOWED_STATS_2026-09-20.md`](38_P1_04_STIRRING_WINDOWED_STATS_2026-09-20.md).

## Explicit non-claims

- Not a method no-go forever — only no-go for finer mesh / paper cell at current residual floor.
- Did not raise β or lower μ to invent a visible roll.

# File: `38_P1_04_STIRRING_WINDOWED_STATS_2026-09-20.md`

# P1-04 stirring windowed stats (2026-09-20)

> **Disposition (doc 41):** matched-window U_rms still retracts the 55% endpoint contrast. This is **not** stationarity: T_std rose from ~7.6 K (rev 0–5) to ~19.9 K (rev 8–10). Keep `_sph` on Np/Nu. Historical numbers below are unchanged.

> Doc 23 §13. Reassesses Round 3D fixed-current vs fixed-power endpoint U_rms gap (~55%) using **identical revolution windows**.

## Finding status

| Item | Status |
|------|--------|
| Windowed means/std (rev 5–10, 8–10) | **Done** from existing 10-rev logs |
| Signed torque → time-averaged P_mech | **Done** |
| Endpoint-only U_rms as mode comparator | **Rejected** (misleading) |
| Paper Np/Nu definitions | Still open — keep `_sph` suffix |
| Re_imp description | low/moderate-Re laminar (~19), not Stokes |

## Sources

| Mode | Log |
|------|-----|
| fixed-current | `build/stir_skull_crust_10rev.log` |
| fixed-power | `build/stir_skull_crust_10rev_fixed_power.log` |

Analyzer: `docs/ophelie/reference/analyze_stir_window_stats.py` → `stir_p1_04_window_stats.json`.

## Window rev ∈ [5, 10] (≈438 samples each)

| Metric | fixed-current | fixed-power | rel Δ |
|--------|--------------:|------------:|------:|
| mean U_rms | **0.1046** ± 0.017 | **0.1051** ± 0.017 | **0.4%** |
| mean T | 1473.32 K | 1473.34 K | ~0 |
| mean T_std | 17.73 K | 17.74 K | ~0 |
| mean signed T_z | −0.388 Nm | −0.376 Nm | — |
| P_mech from mean T_z | 0.406 W | 0.394 W | 3.1% |
| mean Np_sph (inst.) | 3.09 | 2.99 | — |
| mean Nu_sph (inst.) | 36.1 | 36.1 | — |
| **endpoint** U_rms only | 0.073 | 0.113 | **54%** |

Window [8, 10]: U_rms mean rel Δ ≈ **0.1%**.

## Interpretation

1. Doc 23’s “~55% U_rms difference” was an **endpoint snapshot artifact**. Over matched revolution windows the two control modes agree to ≪1% on U_rms and thermally.
2. Prefer **mean signed torque → P_mech** over averaging instantaneous \|T\|ω when reporting Np_sph trends.
3. Bulk thermal state remains interchangeable; instantaneous flow labels fluctuate with std ~0.017 m/s — do not claim bit-identical flow fields.
4. Nu_sph stays well-conditioned here (dT ≫ 5 K); still not paper Nu.
5. Production stirring baseline: **fixed-current** (simpler); fixed-power not required for mode equivalence on ≥10 rev with Level-1 skull+crust.

## Conventions to keep

- Always label `Np_sph`, `Nu_sph`.
- Report window `[rev_a, rev_b]` means ± std, not single endpoint.
- Describe Re_imp ~ O(10–20) as laminar / moderate-Re, not creeping Stokes.

## Next

1. Optional: archive `french_stirring_em_monitor.csv` per control mode with the run (not only last overwrite).
2. Paper L for Np/Nu when journal definitions are frozen.
3. No new long stirring campaign required for P1-04 closure.

# File: `39_CHATGPT_DISCUSSION_LITERATURE_NEXT_2026-09-20.md`

# ChatGPT discussion pack — after Doc 23 internal repairs (2026-09-20)

> **Paste this file to ChatGPT**, or upload the companion tarball
> `docs/ophelie/packs/chatgpt_status_and_code_2026-09-20.tar.gz`.

---

## 0. Ask of ChatGPT

Doc 23 Batches A–E and the midterm P1-03 / P1-04 checks are done. §16 (French literature reproduction) was only an 8-step skeleton written **before** these results. Please do **not** restart internal EM repairs.

Please:

1. Confirm which Doc 23 items are actually closed, and which “still open” items must stay open (not quietly promoted to a paper claim).
2. Turn §16 / Batch F into **one primary next step** (2–4 weeks) plus explicit deferrals. Prefer a freeze-and-compare plan over a 1000 s campaign.
3. Say which paper observables are allowed to be compared **now** (with declared model error) versus which must wait (natural cell, paper Np/Nu, skull shape, 1000 s).
4. Given the quantified coil-only bias (~5% absolute power at fixed I; shape gates already pass), state whether literature runs should stay `--aind=off`.
5. Give stop conditions: what result would halt a literature campaign instead of “run longer.”

Questions in §8.

---

## 1. What Doc 23 asked, and what we did

Doc 23 said: fix internal consistency first; do not treat a missing OPHELIE/COMSOL CSV as the only blocker; then build an independent axisymmetric EM reference and only then decide production `A_ind`. Long French-paper campaigns come after that.

| Batch / item | Required outcome | Status (2026-09-20) | Report |
|---|---|---|---|
| A P0-01/02 | Fluid power is the fluid’s; smoke ≠ validation | **Done** | doc 24 |
| B P0-03/04 | Real feedback after one-way A; Picard residual is unrelaxed; final recon | **Done** | doc 25 |
| C P0-05/06 | Enthalpy uses ∫cp; ~18% area gap explained (discrete A=V/δ), not a silent h bug | **Done** | doc 26 |
| D P1-01/02 | Modes labeled; mean-μ is not local μ(T); Q path is 3D EM → Euler, not axi→3D | **Done** (local μ(T) deferred) | doc 27 |
| E §14 | Weak analytic + long-cylinder self-induction + GetDP finite cylinder | **Done** | docs 28–34 |
| §15 A_ind | Production decision from those gates | **Keep `--aind=off`** | doc 35 |
| P1-03 | Boussinesq path + prescribed-T twin | Force path **pass**; 5 s roll **not detectable** → finer natural mesh **no-go** | docs 36–37 |
| P1-04 | Windowed stir stats vs 55% endpoint U_rms | Endpoint artifact; rev[5,10] U_rms rel Δ **0.4%** | doc 38 |
| F / §16 | Paper-aligned scientific validation | **Not started** | this pack |

---

## 2. Decisions that changed the literature plan

These were not known when Doc 23 §16 was written.

### 2.1 Electromagnetic model (simplified coil + glass)

Independent reference is **GetDP MagDyn axisymmetric**, not a Jacoutot CSV. Promoted mesh: glass `lc_g=0.01`, outer `R_ext=1.5` (R_ext=1.0 biased absolute power by ~5%). Authoritative power is `P = ∫ ½|J|²/σ · 2πr`.

| Gate | Result |
|---|---|
| Same-current shells / mid-plane J·Q (Picard vs GetDP) | \|ΔP_outer\|≈0.004; corr≥0.999; P_SPH/P_GetDP≈**0.98** |
| One-way vs GetDP | P ratio ≈**1.04**; shells already inside the shape gate |
| Equal-power rescale | Does not create the shape agreement; shells already matched at same I |
| Infinite-cylinder Bessel, δ/R≳0.65 | mid-plane corr\|J\|≈0.99; French-like A_ind/A_coil≈**0.05** |
| French lattice one-way @ dp=0.02 | A_ind/A_coil≈**0.18** locally; shape gates still pass coil-only |
| Strong skin δ/R≈0.29 (σ=10³) | Picard **diverges** at current spacing — not the glass regime |

**Production:** `--aind=off`. Self-induction is a moderate absolute-power bias at fixed current, absorbed by current scaling in fixed-power mode. Do not turn Picard on for ~3×10⁵ stirring particles.

Pointwise |B| or |J| relative error stays large (3D lattice vs axi FEM) and is **not** a pass gate.

### 2.2 Stirring control modes

Round 3D 10 rev, Level-1 skull+crust, fixed-current vs fixed-power:

| Metric | Wrong reading | Correct reading |
|---|---|---|
| Endpoint U_rms | 0.073 vs 0.113 m/s (**~55%**) | single-sample artifact |
| Window rev∈[5,10] mean U_rms | — | 0.1046 vs 0.1051 (**0.4%**), std≈0.017 |
| Mean T | — | 1473.32 vs 1473.34 K |
| Signed torque → P_mech | — | ~0.41 vs 0.39 W (mechanical ≪ 60 kW) |

Default baseline: **fixed-current**. `Np_sph` / `Nu_sph` stay SPH labels. Paper definitions are not frozen. Re_imp~O(10–20) is laminar / moderate-Re, not Stokes.

### 2.3 Natural convection

Boussinesq `F = −m β (T−T₀) g` matches ForcePrior to ~1e−7 and points upward for hot fluid. A 5 s prescribed hot-bottom twin (ΔT frozen, zero Q) still matches the zero-buoyancy control (`U_merid` differs by ~0.0002). **Do not refine the natural mesh** to chase a Jacoutot cell until a controlled test shows a resolvable roll. Do not increase β, cut viscosity, or add a lid to manufacture one.

---

## 3. What we will not claim

| Claim | Allowed now? |
|---|---|
| Fluid injected heat and EM power are separately measured | Yes (P_heat_fluid vs P_em) |
| Smoke pass equals scientific validation | **No** (`smoke_passed` vs `validation_passed`) |
| Coil+glass simplified EM shape agrees with an independent FEM | Yes, within documented gates; **not** full furnace / segmented wall / paddle metal |
| Coil-only Q is literature-accurate to a few percent in watts | **No.** O(5%) self-induction bias at fixed I is declared |
| Fixed-current and fixed-power stirring are thermally similar on ≥10 rev | Yes (window means) |
| Flow fields are interchangeable snapshot-to-snapshot | **No** (U_rms std ~0.017 m/s) |
| Np_sph / Nu_sph are Jacoutot Np / Nu | **No** |
| Natural convection cell reproduced | **No** |
| 1000 s thermal steady state | **Not run** |
| Dynamic skull / phase change | **Not in scope** |

---

## 4. Doc 23 §16 skeleton (unchanged text, now the discussion target)

Once internal and independent EM gates pass — **they have**:

1. Freeze journal geometry, coil, material laws, heat boundaries, source conventions; resolve thesis conflicts explicitly.
2. Skull/crust sensitivity; separate calibration from validation.
3. Temporal, particle, and EM-update convergence for selected observables.
4. Local variable-property flow only if the comparison requires it.
5. Compare paper Q/T/velocity sections, Np/Nu, temperature uniformity at compatible times and control modes.
6. Run toward 1000 s only after energy accounting and boundary physics are credible; monitor stationarity.
7. Resume quantitative natural convection only after a resolvable buoyancy signal.
8. Dynamic skull or metal carriers only if a remaining paper discrepancy requires them.

Item 7 is already answered **no** for this midterm. Item 6 should not be the first move.

---

## 5. Code in the tarball (what ChatGPT can read)

Shared EM / thermal library (current tree, not an old snapshot):

```text
code/shared/electromagnetic_ophelie/
```

Cases (sources + small STL; no reload binaries, no VTP):

---

## 6. Production command class (unchanged)

```text
--thermo-em-coupling=periodic --em-update-interval=0.5
--em-control=fixed-current --aind=off
--skull-resistance --skull-delta=0.005
--free-crust-resistance --free-crust-delta=0.005
```

Natural quantitative cell reproduction remains parked. Do not mix natural and stirring reload files. Vertical axis is +z.

---

## 7. Suggested constraints for the next plan

Please keep these unless you explicitly reject one:

- No 1000 s run as the next step.
- No finer natural-convection campaign.
- No production `--aind=on` on the full stirring lattice.
- No silent Q rescale, no `--balance-heat-loss` as paper physics, no lid.
- No TEAM7 result as a French EM substitute.
- Keep `_sph` on Np/Nu until a written definition audit.
- Missing author CSV is not a stop; digitized figures only with declared uncertainty.

---

## 8. Questions

1. **First freeze.** Which parameters must be locked before any new long stirring run: geometry/coil, σ(T), Level-1 skull δ, heat-loss law, EM update interval, dp=5 mm? Which conflicts with the thesis should be written as `OPEN_PARAMETER` instead of “fixed”?
2. **First observable.** If we may compare only one literature figure family next, which is it (Q shells, T uniformity, velocity section, Np, Nu), and what SPH output already exists versus what must be added?
3. **Np/Nu.** Is auditing the paper definition (length, ρ, ΔT, which walls) a documentation task that should precede any new run, or is it blocked on missing figure data?
4. **Error budget.** For a simplified coil+glass, `--aind=off` model, what discrepancy vs a paper contour is still “model difference” rather than a solver bug?
5. **Skull.** Level-1 resistance shells closed the 10 rev energy book. Should sensitivity on `skull-delta` / crust be the next *numerical* experiment, or should skull stay frozen while we only audit definitions?
6. **One primary next step** and a stop condition. What would make you say “do not continue toward 1000 s”?
