<a id="40-post-doc23-code-review-and-next-plan-2026-09-21md"></a>

# File: `40_POST_DOC23_CODE_REVIEW_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09-21.md`

# Post-Doc-23 code review and next development plan

Date: 2026-09-21  
Audience: Cursor and the project owner  
Reviewed snapshot: `chatgpt_status_and_code_2026-09-20`  
Predecessor: Doc 23; implementation reports: Docs 24–39

## 1. Decision and scope

The project has made real progress: complex feedback is repaired, Picard stopping is substantially improved, a sensible-enthalpy helper exists, fluid-source power is separately reported, and an independent GetDP workflow now exists. Preserve these changes.

However, the statement “Batches A–E are closed; internal gates have passed” is too broad. Several changes close instrumentation or labeling tasks, while the corresponding physical or numerical acceptance remains open. In particular, the current snapshot still contains thermal-update inconsistencies and incomplete acceptance gates. Some independent-EM conclusions use incompatible comparison measures or confuse one-way induction with coil-only operation.

**Primary next objective:** produce one reproducible, quantitatively auditable stirring heat-transfer baseline, with a frozen literature parameter/observable contract and an honest first comparison to the French paper. Budget approximately 2–4 weeks as an ordering guide, not a runtime guarantee. Use short verification cases first; extend physical time only after the required gates pass.

Keep production `--aind=off` for now as a computational/modeling choice. Do not describe its fixed-current error as a validated universal 5%. Do not launch a 1000 s campaign, a finer natural-convection production mesh, segmented-metal EM, or dynamic skull development in this cycle.

## 2. Review coverage and evidence limits

Reviewed README, reports 24–38 and the discussion summary, both production case sources, shared thermal/material/buoyancy/self-induction code, GetDP formulation/export/comparison/convergence scripts, metadata, comparison JSON, analytic-cylinder implementation, and stirring statistics code/results. Compared the findings with Doc 23 requirements.

Performed independent numerical checks on supplied JSON/CSV and a circular-loop cross-section calculation. No SPHinXsys GPU binaries were rebuilt or executed. The package lacks the full framework, production reloads, most run logs, and raw SPH/GetDP fields needed to regenerate every published comparison. Therefore:

- “Confirmed in code” means supported by the supplied implementation.
- “Reported result” means a number in Cursor's supplied evidence, not a newly repeated solver run.
- “Recomputed” means a calculation repeated directly from the supplied data or stated geometry.
- Missing evidence is not automatically proof of an incorrect solver.
- The original journal/thesis figures were not supplied in this snapshot. Do not invent figure numbers, paper definitions, or digitized values from this review.

Paths below are relative to the snapshot root. In the actual checkout, locate the corresponding repository files rather than creating a second implementation under `code/`.

## 3. Revised closure table

| Doc 23 topic | What can be credited | What remains open |
|---|---|---|
| P0-01 fluid-source accounting | `P_heat_fluid` now measures the fluid body | Actual cumulative applied heat; moving-particle transfer history; energy residual still uses EM power |
| P0-02 acceptance gates | Stirring separates smoke/validation and checks completion/refreshes | Natural retains old gates; stirring energy gate is only a sanity check; short periodic runs can validate with zero refreshes |
| P0-03 real feedback | Old pre-feedback-current shortcut removed | Preserve regression; no need to redesign this fix |
| P0-04 Picard | Unrelaxed residual and final reconstruction added | Joint volume-weighted complex norm and explicit final induction defect; propagate failure to callers |
| P0-05 enthalpy | Integral of cp replaces cp(T)T in variable-property diagnostics | Diffusion and boundary cooling still divide by constant cp; cumulative coupled conservation not demonstrated |
| P0-06 boundary area | Discrete area deficit identified and quantified | Deficit remains in production cooling; geometric cause and convergent correction not completed |
| P1-01 materials | Modes labeled; mean viscosity declared; cache invalidation wired | Actual GPU force/time-step response not proven; local viscosity deferred; variable-cp path is inconsistent |
| P1-02 Q path | Actual 3D EM → Euler path documented | No need to implement axi rotate-back merely to match an earlier description |
| Independent EM | GetDP pipeline and useful reduced tests exist | Domain convergence, source normalization, same-support spatial errors, direct coil-only comparison, phase validation |
| P1-03 natural buoyancy | Buoyancy component agrees with its formula | Actual total-force path audit; genuinely controlled prescribed-T test; circulation detectability remains unresolved |
| P1-04 stirring statistics | Window means remove the misleading endpoint-only contrast | Time weighting, phase/turn statistics, stationary-window evidence and paper definitions |

## 4. Findings that must affect the next cycle

### R01 — Actual heat is still absent from the energy balance [P0, confirmed]

Evidence: `code/cases/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em.cpp`, periodic monitor block. It calls:

```cpp
residual = frenchEnergyResidual(dE_dt, p_joule, side_w,
                               free_conv_w + free_rad_w, bottom_w);
appendFrenchEnergyBudgetCsv(..., dE_dt, p_joule, ...);
```

Here `p_joule` is reconstructed EM power. The new `P_heat_fluid` does not drive this balance. The natural case also passes `p_joule` to its energy CSV.

The documented startup values are approximately 60,000 W on EM particles and 58,174 W on fluid particles, a difference of 1,826 W. This is a known transfer/domain difference, not heat that entered the fluid. An endpoint power is also not the time average over the interval used for `dE_dt`.

In stirring, `sample_joule_heat.exec()` is called on ordinary advection updates, but `recordFrenchFluidHeatPower` is called at initialization and EM refreshes, not after every ordinary resampling. Thus `last_power.p_heat_fluid` can become stale while the melt moves through a fixed Euler source. Out-of-grid maxima are also sampled at EM refreshes rather than necessarily every fluid sampling event.

Required changes:

1. Keep `P_em`, `P_grid_at_em`, `P_heat_fluid_sampled`, and actual applied thermal energy distinct.
2. Accumulate heat from the actual source operator over the actual substeps. For constant properties, reconcile Q·V·dt with the update using the same mass/density convention; for variable properties, measure the enthalpy increment or advance enthalpy directly.
3. Accumulate side, bottom, free-surface and other external heat exchanges at the time they are applied. Record clamp, imposed-temperature, and reset energy separately.
4. Evaluate, for a melt-only system:

   `R_E = H(t1)-H(t0) - E_Q_applied + E_boundary_out - E_other_in`.

   For a melt+wall-shell system, include wall storage and external wall loss; internal contact exchanges must cancel.
5. Use double precision for reductions and cumulative energy. Verify whether `rho0 * VolumetricMeasure` remains a conserved mass in the actual framework; prefer particle `Mass` for H when appropriate, and make the source conversion consistent with it.
6. Track transfer errors and out-of-grid counts during fluid sampling, with historical maxima. Do not silently normalize Q to hide a cavity or geometry mismatch. A source/receiver domain mismatch must be separated from interpolation loss.

Acceptance: a short adiabatic Q-only case, an insulated diffusion case, and a boundary-cooling case close independently under refinement; then a moving/sorted-particle coupled short run closes on the cumulative balance. Choose and freeze tolerances before the production comparison. A provisional coupled energy target of 1–2% is an engineering target, not a literature-derived tolerance.

### R02 — Variable-cp thermal evolution is inconsistent [P0 when enabled, confirmed]

Evidence:

- Stirring uses `ApplyJouleHeatWithParticleCpCK` when variable properties are enabled.
- `ApplyLaplaceDiffusionDtCK` stores `diff_factor_ = -1/(rho*cp)` from a scalar constructor argument.
- `ApplyOphelieThermalFrenchNaturalBcCK` stores `inv_rho_cp_delta_ = 1/(rho*cp*delta)` from a scalar constructor argument.
- Both are instantiated with reference `cp` even in variable-property mode.
- The energy diagnostic uses `hostThermalEnthalpyEnergy(..., laws.cp, ...)`.

The enthalpy definition is improved, but the solver still applies three different thermal contributions with inconsistent heat capacities. This is not repaired by improving the energy diagnostic alone. It does not invalidate the default constant-cp mode, but it blocks a full variable-cp claim.

Required change: use a common thermodynamic update contract for Joule heating, diffusion, boundary cooling and relevant wall/contact terms. Either read the same local cp at the appropriate stage, with a quantified explicit-step error, or advance specific enthalpy and invert h(T). Keep `ThermalDeltaT` synchronized. Do not introduce a spurious extra `T*dcp/dT` term.

Acceptance: variable-cp insulated two-region heat exchange conserves total H; variable-cp cooling and heating agree with scalar reference integrations and converge with dt. Until then, keep the literature baseline explicitly constant cp/k/mu and disable or clearly reject unsupported combinations.

### R03 — The wall-area error is explained, not solved [P0 before quantitative temperature comparison]

Doc 26 reports `A_disc/A_geom = 0.820` on the side and `0.811` on the bottom. Losses at near-uniform initial T are approximately:

| Face | Geometric estimate | Particle estimate |
|---|---:|---:|
| Side | 46.428 kW | 38.063 kW |
| Bottom | 6.859 kW | 5.564 kW |
| Free surface | 15.286 kW | 15.616 kW |
| Total | 68.573 kW | 59.230 kW |

Changing from CAD height to settled melt extent only modestly improves the side ratio. A roughly 9.34 kW total discrepancy is larger than the discussed few-percent induction correction. Agreement of old total heat loss with a 60 kW source may contain compensating discretization errors; it is not independent validation of 5 mm skull/crust resistance.

The face-tag code assigns each particle to only one closest face. A corner shell cannot independently represent both side and bottom flux through a single exclusive label. Other contributions, including packing and actual wetted geometry, remain to be separated.

Required change:

- Define actual wetted surfaces and any rotor/cavity exclusions.
- Use separate per-face area weights, allowing multiple surface contributions near corners where justified, or another documented geometric surface quadrature.
- A geometry-derived normalization is permissible if explicit, observable and verified for nonuniform flux; do not tune h or skull thickness to reproduce the old thermal balance.
- Test each face first at uniform T and then at a known nonuniform T distribution. Report dp and boundary-shell-width sensitivity, including retagging/motion.
- Use the same weights in temperature updates and loss accounting.

Acceptance: face area/flux errors meet an agreed small tolerance and decrease or stabilize appropriately with refinement. A provisional 1–2% area target is reasonable for a regular cylinder. If the current SPH boundary representation cannot achieve it, carry a quantified uncertainty and avoid a tighter temperature claim.

### R04 — Validation gates still permit false closure [P0, confirmed]

Stirring `thermal_energy_ok` is currently equivalent to finite mean/max T and positive total heat loss. It does not test energy conservation. `validation_passed` inherits that weak condition. Short periodic runs can pass with zero required refreshes. `q_sample_rel_l2` is the startup quantity; transfer and residual histories are not fully gated.

Natural still uses the earlier end-of-run pattern:

```cpp
em_ok = ... (periodic_coupling ? isfinite(phi_eq) : power_rel_err < ...);
coupling_ok = !periodic_coupling ||
              (update_count >= 1 && isfinite(last_phi_eq_res));
buoyancy_ok = true;
passed = em_ok && flow_ok && thermal_ok && buoyancy_ok && coupling_ok;
```

The natural end block has no equivalent `FrenchCouplingRunGates`/`validation_passed` contract. Batch A therefore did not close acceptance for both cases.

Required change: share an explicit gate implementation between cases. Separate startup smoke, thermal/source validation, periodic-coupling validation, and physical-observable validation. A periodic-coupling test must require at least three completed refreshes, finite and bounded residual histories, completion of requested time, valid transfer history, and cumulative energy closure. A shorter test is “not exercised,” not “validated.” Propagate Picard failure if enabled. Do not require positive heat loss for an intentionally insulated validation case.

### R05 — One-way feedback is not coil-only [P0 for EM claims, confirmed]

> **Disposition (doc 41 / 43):** three-mode azi-avg @ dp=0.02, σ=16 now exists. Coil-only P_SPH/P_GetDP = **1.009**; one-way 1.041; Picard 0.979. Do not quote Docs 30–35 one-way numbers as coil-only. Production σ≈24.75 and R06–R08 remain open.

Docs 30–33 use one-way A_ind feedback dumps and Picard dumps. Doc 30's command explicitly has `--ophelie-aind-one-way-feedback=1`; its reported power rises from coil-only 1.415 W to feedback 1.453 W. Doc 35 then uses the one-way/Picard comparison as evidence for a coil-only simplification.

These are three distinct models:

1. Coil-only: solve using A_coil; no induced A feedback.
2. One-way feedback: form A_ind from a previous current and solve again once.
3. Picard: iterate induced A/current toward self-consistency.

The approximately 1.04 FEM power ratio belongs to mode 2, not demonstrated mode 1. This is an evidence-labeling error, not a reason to turn production Picard on.

Required change: export all three modes at identical geometry, current, material, support and resolution. Include the actual production conductivity and the relevant sigma(T) range; the reference sigma=16 S/m is not automatically the stirred default near 24.75 S/m. Only then quantify coil-only model error. Equal-power rescaling removes a scalar power mismatch, not spatial deposition or thermal-feedback differences.

### R06 — Reference-domain convergence has not been established [P0 for absolute EM claims]

> **Disposition (doc 41 / 43):** further enlarge beyond `fine_large` moves P by +0.13% (radial), +1.59% (axial), +1.97% (both); gate <2% pass. Keep published working ref; residual is axial-dominated ≲2%. Not domain-at-infinity.

The supplied sweep increases outer radius from 1.0 to 1.5 m and z padding from 0.4 to 0.6 m, changing power by about 5.4%. `domain_large` and `fine_large` have the same outer dimensions and different meshes. Their small difference supports mesh refinement at that boundary, not boundary-at-infinity convergence.

The metadata calls the reference `populated_v2_converged_domain`, while its embedded convergence summary still has `all_cases_pass: false`. Neither the name nor a promotion step proves convergence.

Required change: retain the current case as a working reference, but label external-domain uncertainty open. Add at least one further enlargement and, if needed, another, controlling near-field/air resolution. Separate radial and axial truncation effects sufficiently to know which boundary matters. Stop enlarging when successive changes in target power and spatial metrics meet the predeclared tolerance; do not mandate an arbitrary huge domain.

### R07 — Spatial agreement is overstated by inconsistent measures [P0 before validating Q shape]

> **Disposition (doc 41 / 43):** probe vs element ΔP_outer ≈0.027 confirmed; midplane corr Q≈0.90 / shape L2≈0.20; full-glass masked L2(Q)≈0.9. “Near-perfect shells” retracted. Metrics in `compare_sph_getdp.py` → `common_support_L2`.

Recomputed from `metadata.json` and `evidence_json/compare_aziavg_picard_v2.json`:

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---:|
| SPH outer-shell power fraction | 0.696273697 |
| FEM fraction used in comparison script (probe quadrature) | 0.699906219 |
| FEM fraction from supplied element-based J integral | 0.723121829 |
| Published comparison difference | 0.003632522 |
| Difference using element-based FEM fraction | **0.026848132** |
| SPH represented volume / nominal cylinder volume | **0.953789804** |
| Approximate supplied probe-grid power integral | **1.35923 W** |
| Supplied FEM element-integral power | **1.46551 W** |

The corrected outer-shell difference is still below the old loose 0.05 threshold. It does not invalidate every shape result, but the claim of near-perfect shell matching is unsupported. The probe grid covers a centroid-derived inset domain rather than the full cylinder; nearest-centroid sampling introduces another approximation. SPH also represents about 4.6% less nominal cylinder volume at dp=0.02. Absolute totals can agree partly through support differences.

The same Picard comparison reports median relative errors of approximately 39% in |J_theta| and 72% in Q after azimuthal averaging. Near-axis zeros can inflate relative errors, but they do not justify dismissing the entire spatial discrepancy without localization. Correlation near 0.999 cannot detect multiplicative amplitude error: Q and 1.5Q have correlation 1.

Required changes:

1. Define one common physical comparison domain and physical bin volumes. Report represented-volume errors explicitly.
2. Use FEM element integration or controlled quadrature for shell totals; compare it with probe integration as a separate export check.
3. Compare cell/bin averages to cell/bin averages. Apply compatible spatial averaging to both methods; an SPH (r,z) disk average is not the same observable as an unsmoothed FEM point sample.
4. Add volume-weighted relative L2 errors for absolute Q, normalized Q and complex J, plus axial deposition and radial profiles at several heights. Mask or separately report near-zero-reference regions with an explicit rule.
5. Report correlation as supplementary, not sufficient. No arbitrary Q rescale in a same-current comparison.
6. Integrate all published metrics into a reproducible script. The supplied comparison script does not itself generate the added `midplane_corr_*` JSON fields; archive their producer as well.

### R08 — The 23% coil-field explanation is not credible as written [P0 reference audit]

> **Disposition (doc 41 / 43):** hard-coded metadata removed. Fresh Flag_coil_only \|Bz\| ~20% vs filament; 5 mm section ~0.001%; domain enlarge +1.2%. Do **not** retune js0. Residual open as formulation/extraction — not a Stage-3 power-gate blocker.

`export_reference_csv.py` hard-codes the same axis-B comparison into metadata, including promoted cases. It is not a freshly computed coil-only measurement for each case.

Independent check: average the exact circular-loop axis field over a uniformly driven 5 mm × 5 mm cross-section, at the stated seven loop centers, radius 0.285 m, current 1 A/turn, and observation point z=0.105 m:

```text
Filament:       1.43288729397e-5 T
Finite square:  1.43287145684e-5 T
Relative shift: -1.10526e-5  (about -0.0011%)
```

This calculation isolates finite cross-section geometry in free space; it does not include FEM truncation or discretization. It shows that “5 mm finite section” cannot by itself explain a 23% center-axis deficit for the stated source.

Audit coil-current normalization, axis-coordinate interpretation, field extraction, and domain/mesh error. Derive the source and power factors from the actual GetDP basis/Jacobian convention; do not change them merely until SPH totals agree. The GetDP manual explicitly distinguishes `VolAxi` and `VolAxiSqu`, reinforcing the need for a formulation-specific derivation: [GetDP Jacobian definitions](https://getdp.info/doc/texinfo/getdp.html#Types-for-Jacobian).

The supplied J-based power calculation is useful, but averages nodal J before squaring and uses centroid quadrature. Confirm its integration accuracy rather than treating it as exact. Preserve raw `.pos` data and demonstrate agreement of two independently derived physical-power integrals as resolution increases.

### R09 — Analytic-cylinder phase convention is reversed [P1, confirmed]

The analytic header uses `alpha=(1+i)/delta` with ordinary Bessel J functions and predicts weak-limit `E_theta=+i*omega*B0*r/2`. The supplied EM implementation uses `E_imag=-omega*A_real` and GetDP metadata declares `exp(+i*omega*t)`.

Derivation under that convention, for a nonmagnetic axisymmetric cylinder:

```text
curl(E) = -i*omega*B
J_theta = -dH_z/dr
H_z'' + H_z'/r - i*omega*mu*sigma*H_z = 0
alpha^2 = -i*omega*mu*sigma
```

With ordinary J0/J1, use `alpha=(1-i)/delta` (or an explicitly equivalent modified-Bessel formulation), yielding weak-limit `E_theta=-i*omega*B0*r/2` for the stated orientation.

The current analytic result is complex-conjugated relative to that convention. Magnitudes and Q are unchanged, so existing magnitude-only tests cannot catch it. This finding does not prove the production SPH induction operator has the wrong sign; it identifies a reference/phase-validation defect.

Required change: fix the analytic convention consistently in C++, Python, metadata and docs; test real/imaginary components and phase relative to the imposed field. State the finite-H versus infinite-cylinder boundary difference. R=0.1 m at sigma=16 has delta/R≈2.3, not the production R≈0.25 m regime. The sigma=100/200 tests probe more relevant penetration ratios but still need the corrected phase check and credible resolution evidence.

### R10 — Natural detectability experiment does not establish the claimed conclusion [P1 diagnostic]

Three separate limitations are visible:

1. `--freeze-prescribed-t` disables Q/diffusion but `apply_natural_bc.exec(dt)` still runs. The profile is prescribed only once. The reported imposed 50 K contrast becomes 63.3 K. Thus temperature is not frozen.
2. `hostAuditBoussinesqForcePrior` defaults to reading the field `BoussinesqBuoyancyForceCK`, not total `ForcePrior`. It verifies the component formula but does not independently prove downstream accumulation and use in the acoustic step. Do not claim an overwrite bug; test the actual path.
3. A strictly spatially fixed T(z) creates a vertical buoyancy field f(z)e_z with zero curl, which can be balanced hydrostatically. A heated-bottom instability test requires its own perturbation/thermal evolution and stability conditions; it is not automatically a deterministic circulation-forcing test. If temperatures are instead carried with particles, it is a different experiment and must be specified accordingly.

Required small diagnostic:

- Choose and state Eulerian prescribed T(r,z) versus material-particle T. For an Eulerian force-path test, use a smooth bounded radial temperature variation so buoyancy has a nonzero rotational component, and reimpose it at current positions before evaluating the force.
- Disable all thermal evolution in that test, including boundary cooling, wall contact and unintended resets; assert the prescribed-field error throughout.
- Compare buoyancy-on/off with identical geometry, particle state and sampling. Audit changes in total `ForcePrior` after gravity/viscosity/buoyancy updates and before the acoustic solver consumes it, including sorting.
- Measure signed circulation and meridional velocity fields, not only speed norms. Select duration based on force/viscous response and observed noise, not an unexplained universal 5 s.

Keep the expensive natural mesh refinement deferred. Replace “controlled test failed; natural is no-go” with “current controlled test is inconclusive; quantitative natural reproduction remains open.”

### R11 — Stirring statistics improve the interpretation but do not establish stationarity [P1]

Credit Doc 38: matched-window means near 0.1046 and 0.1051 m/s do not support the previous endpoint-only 55% flow difference. Retract an endpoint-based mode-equivalence conclusion in either direction.

Remaining limitations:

- `analyze_stir_window_stats.py` computes arithmetic sample means. Screen output is step-based; dt/physical sample spacing can vary. Parse physical time or revolutions and use quadrature weights.
- Avoid counting a duplicate endpoint summary as another independent sample.
- Report per-revolution means/variation and phase-conditioned fields where useful. Approximately 438 correlated samples are not 438 independent realizations; reported std is not a confidence interval.
- Resolve torque orientation and use signed rotor power, e.g. `-omega*mean(T_on_rotor)`, according to the actual force convention. Applying abs after averaging is useful for magnitude comparison but should not conceal a wrong sign.
- Compute Np from the same signed-mean power definition if that is the claimed observable; the existing `Np_instantaneous` mean is a different statistic.

The supplied fixed-current data show mean T_std≈7.63 K in rev[0,5], 17.73 K in rev[5,10], and 19.90 K in rev[8,10]. The temperature distribution is still evolving even though bulk mean T changes little. Do not label this a stationary thermal state. The two old modes are similar over the observed windows; equivalence at later time is untested.

### R12 — Secondary verification gaps [P1/P2, do not expand into a rewrite]

- Picard now measures unrelaxed updates, but `hostRelativeComplexEdgeJChange` takes the max of separately normalized unweighted real/imaginary norms. Implement the requested joint volume-weighted complex norm if using nonuniform carriers or rigorous convergence claims. A nearly zero component should not dominate a relative norm.
- Final A is built from the last iterate and final J is then reconstructed. This is a substantial improvement, but the remaining induction mismatch is finite. Report a final defect for A_ind[J_final] and constitutive/power consistency; do not call finite-tolerance fields exactly self-consistent. Keep the cost confined to reduced diagnostics while production induction is off.
- The k=4 thermal “MMS” reported in Doc 27 mainly checks heating/cooling direction and Dirichlet behavior. A negative energy-cap residual does not prove correct conductivity scaling. Add a manufactured/exact diffusion profile or decay-rate check with k=1 and k=4, using a small controlled test.
- Viscosity-kernel invalidation is visibly wired, but the framework implementations are absent. Verify actual force and relevant time-step response to a known viscosity swap once on the target device. Defer full local pairwise mu(T) until required by the chosen paper comparison.
- Natural Level-2 thick-wall thermal resistance does not by itself establish the physical thin-skull transient heat capacity. Keep that approximation explicit.

## 5. Execution plan: one primary track, bounded work

### Stage 0 — Freeze evidence and literature definitions (start immediately)

Deliver `41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION.md` and a machine-readable run manifest.

- Record commit, dirty diff, executable/build/device, commands, reload hashes, geometry, material modes, current/power convention, thermal boundaries and output paths.
- Correct closure statements in Docs 24–39 without deleting their historical numerical evidence.
- Extract journal/thesis values and definitions into a table with page/equation/figure provenance. Where sources conflict, keep `OPEN_PARAMETER`; distinguish a simulation's operational frozen value from a validated literature value.
- Freeze melt/rotor/wall dimensions, coil geometry, axis +z, natural-versus-stirred scenario, f, current convention, absorbed-power definition, T0, sigma(T), cp/k/mu modes, beta, skull/crust laws, boundary temperatures, rotation, reference lengths and normalization.
- Audit Np/Nu definitions now. Missing digitized curves do not block definitions, but missing source text does. Do not infer Nu's temperature difference or Np's characteristic diameter from current helper names.

No new long run is needed for this stage.

### Stage 1 — Close source/thermal accounting and geometry (first implementation priority)

Implement R01–R04 on both cases. Focus on constant-property stirring as the minimal literature baseline; fix variable-cp support or explicitly prevent unsupported modes from validating.

Deliver `42_THERMAL_SOURCE_BOUNDARY_VERIFICATION.md` plus small reproducible test results:

| Check | Required evidence |
|---|---|
| Q-only heating | Applied energy equals H increment with moving/sorted particles where applicable |
| Insulated diffusion | H conservation and meaningful k dependence |
| Boundary cooling | Same area weights/fluxes drive update and integrated loss |
| Boundary geometry | Side/bottom/free areas and nonuniform-flux errors; corner treatment |
| Coupled source refresh | At least three actual refreshes; all history gates; completed requested horizon |
| Invalid run behavior | Incomplete run, failed solver, invalid mapping, nonfinite field and energy failure do not validate |

Use a proposed tolerance table written before testing, with observed refinement behavior. If a test fails, diagnose the specific mismatch rather than loosening gates to preserve a previous pass.

### Stage 2 — Repair the independent EM evidence, not the production architecture

Address R05–R09 on the reduced carrier/reference problem. Deliver `43_EM_REFERENCE_REQUALIFICATION.md`.

Sequence:

1. Recompute coil-only axis/profile fields and source normalization; remove hard-coded measurements.
2. Extend the external-domain study until the relevant tolerance is reached.
3. Reconcile element power, export power, bin integration, physical support and shell measures.
4. Compare coil-only / one-way / Picard directly at fixed I and matched sigma, including the production range.
5. Report weighted spatial Q/J errors and corrected complex analytic checks.
6. Only then make the final cost/accuracy statement for `--aind=off`.

If coil-only has unacceptable Q-shape error for the temperature observable, first consider a validated reduced EM carrier or controlled external-reference Q input as a diagnostic. Do not enable O(N²) induction on the full fluid lattice as the immediate response. Label any external-Q thermal study clearly; it validates a different part of the chain.

### Stage 3 — First quantitative stirring comparison

Proceed only after Stages 1 and 2 resolve the blockers relevant to the claim.

**First paper-observable family:** temperature distribution and temperature uniformity in the mechanically stirred case, with compatible power, time/revolution window, geometry and normalization. This directly exercises the French heat-transfer objective and avoids waiting for natural circulation or paper Np/Nu readiness. Use deposited-Q profiles as a prerequisite, not as a substitute for thermal reproduction.

Minimum outputs:

- Time/revolution traces of mean T, T_std, Tmax−Tmin and cumulative energy residual.
- Identically defined radial/axial temperature cuts or r–z maps; archive raw data before rendering.
- Per-revolution U_rms, signed torque/power, and selected mean/phase-conditioned velocity sections as secondary diagnostics.
- Side/bottom/free loss histories and EM/fluid power histories.
- All material/control modes and uncertainty sources next to the comparison.

Use a staged run: a short multi-refresh gate, then an observation window sufficient to assess the selected quantity. Existing 10-rev trajectories can be historical controls, not proof of the corrected implementation. Run longer only when the remaining obstacle is physical transient evolution and the energy/source/boundary evidence is sound.

Sensitivity order:

1. A bounded dt and EM-refresh-interval check for the selected outputs.
2. A practical particle/carrier-resolution comparison to estimate numerical uncertainty.
3. Skull/crust sensitivity only after area quadrature is corrected. Freeze the baseline and vary one uncertainty at a time; use physically justified ranges. Do not tune to the same figure used for validation.
4. Variable-property/local-viscosity extension only if the chosen comparison and temperature range require it.

Deliver `44_FIRST_STIRRING_LITERATURE_COMPARISON.md`, figure data, commands and a clear list of matched/mismatched assumptions. If the paper only provides a stationary field and SPH remains transient, label the plot preliminary and continue only under the stationarity/energy conditions below.

### Small separate diagnostic — Natural force test

Implement R10 on the existing natural mesh or a cheaper controlled geometry. Time-box it as a force/response validation task. It need not delay the stirring thermal track once shared gates are corrected. No automatic finer-mesh natural campaign follows from a pass; reassess signal/noise and literature parameters first.

## 6. What may be compared now

| Observable/claim | Present status |
|---|---|
| Corrected real-feedback response | Valid implementation progress, supported by code and reported reduced tests |
| Simplified FEM/SPH total power and shell tendencies | Preliminary numerical comparison with known support/reference uncertainties |
| Coil-only absolute error “about 5%” | Not established by the current one-way/Picard evidence |
| Existing fixed-current/fixed-power window statistics | Useful historical comparison for those windows; reweight before precision claims |
| Temperature contour vs paper | Exploratory only — Stage-3 r0 reports SPH T family; paper digitization OPEN |
| Stationary thermal uniformity | Not established; 1-rev T_std=4.3 K vs historical 10-rev ~21 K (still evolving) |
| Paper Np/Nu | Definitions/provenance audit first; retain `_sph` labels |
| Natural-convection cell | Not reproduced; diagnostic remains inconclusive |
| Dynamic skull shape / segmented metal / 1000 s state | Outside this cycle and not validated |

There is no defensible universal “within X% of a paper contour is model error” rule yet. Separate measured numerical uncertainty, source/geometry simplifications, boundary uncertainty, material uncertainty, transient mismatch and figure-digitization uncertainty. Treat an unexplained discrepancy as unresolved, not automatically as either a solver bug or permitted modeling error.

## 7. Stop conditions

Do not start or extend a literature campaign if any relevant condition holds:

1. Actual cumulative fluid heating and H balance cannot be reconciled within the frozen tolerance.
2. Side/bottom quadrature remains biased at the present 18–19% level without a quantified acceptable impact on the selected observable.
3. A source/export normalization, phase, support or reference-domain issue remains large enough to obscure the EM error being claimed.
4. The accepted run skips requested physical time, EM refreshes, solver failures, out-of-grid events or substantial clamp energy.
5. The selected observable changes materially under the bounded numerical sensitivity checks.
6. Paper definitions or operating conditions are unknown in a way that prevents an apples-to-apples comparison.
7. A proposed longer run is intended to hide an unresolved numerical discrepancy rather than resolve a credible physical transient.

If only stationarity is missing and the above gates pass, inspect consecutive revolution windows: means, slopes, spatial T_std, face losses and energy storage. Bulk mean T alone is insufficient. Define acceptable drift for the selected observable before extending the horizon.

## 8. Instructions to Cursor

Do not restart all internal EM development, and do not declare Batches A–E completely closed. Preserve the working implementation and address the specific review findings.

Start with a disposition table: confirmed/fixed/not applicable/needs evidence, each linked to code or a reproducible result. Do not mark an item fixed solely because its diagnostic or documentation changed. Implement Stage 1 first while completing the literature definition table. Then requalify the reduced EM evidence and produce the first bounded stirring temperature comparison.

For every batch, return:

1. Changed files and the concrete behavior changed.
2. Exact commands and run identity.
3. Raw or compact sufficient evidence, not only a prose success statement.
4. Every acceptance metric and threshold, including failures and not-exercised gates.
5. Remaining uncertainty and the next bounded action.

Keep full logs/CSV per run rather than overwriting shared output names. Retain historical reports as historical evidence. No silent source rescaling, skull retuning, beta inflation, viscosity reduction or artificial lid to manufacture paper agreement.

# File: `41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION_2026-09-21.md`

# Literature contract v0 and Doc-40 review disposition

Date: 2026-09-21  
Predecessor: [`40_POST_DOC23_CODE_REVIEW_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09-21.md`](40_POST_DOC23_CODE_REVIEW_AND_NEXT_PLAN_2026-09-21.md)  
Machine-readable freeze: [`reference/literature_contract_v0.json`](reference/literature_contract_v0.json)

This document does two things: (1) records where Cursor’s post-pack assessment agrees with or differs from Doc 40; (2) freezes the operational literature contract for the next implementation cycle. It does **not** close Batches A–E as physical acceptance.

Historical numbers in Docs 24–39 remain historical evidence. Do not relabel them as results of the corrected energy/gate path.

---

## 1. Working agreement (do this cycle)

Shared with Doc 40:

- Do not restart all internal EM development.
- Keep production `--aind=off` as a computational/modeling choice, not a validated universal 5% coil-only error.
- No 1000 s campaign, no finer natural production mesh, no segmented-metal EM, no dynamic skull.
- First scientific target, after gates: **stirred temperature distribution / uniformity**, not Np/Nu or a natural cell.
- Stage order: freeze contract → source/thermal/gates (R01–R04) → requalify reduced EM (R05–R09) → first stirring T comparison.
- Keep `_sph` on Np/Nu until a written paper definition exists.

This code drop implements Stage 0 (this file) and the first Stage 1 slice: **R01 applied-heat ledger**, **R04 shared gates**, **R02 variable-cp cannot validate**, and the R10 freeze-T diagnostic (disable Robin/radiation while T is prescribed).

---

## 2. Disposition vs Doc 40

Status key:

| Status | Meaning |
|---|---|
| **AGREE** | Finding accepted; implement or keep open as stated |
| **AGREE, down-rank** | Finding accepted; do not treat it as a reason to discard an existing reduced result |
| **PARTIAL** | Direction accepted; current wording over-claims or under-claims |
| **DEFER** | Valid, not in this slice |

| ID | Cursor vs Doc 40 | Status now | Action this slice |
|---|---|---|---|
| R01 energy uses `p_joule` not fluid heat; `P_heat_fluid` stale between EM refreshes | **AGREE** | Open → code | Ledger + residual on applied fluid heat; sample every advection |
| R02 variable-cp heating vs constant-cp diffusion/BC | **AGREE** | Open | Block `validation_passed` unless constant cp/k/μ; do not claim variable-cp physics |
| R03 18% discrete area | **AGREE** | Open | Not geometrically fixed this slice. Energy now integrates the **same discrete flux the update uses**, so the 18% is a temperature-accuracy issue, not a hidden residual trick |
| R04 smoke/validation; natural old gates; 0 refreshes can “validate” | **AGREE** | Open → code | Shared `evaluateFrenchCouplingRunGates`; validation needs ≥3 periodic refreshes and an energy residual |
| R05 one-way ≠ coil-only | **AGREE** | **Closed (numerical)** | Three-mode azi-avg @ dp=0.02, σ=16: coil-only P ratio **1.009**, one-way 1.041, Picard 0.979. Doc 43. No Picard-on-production |
| R06 GetDP outer domain not proven at infinity | **AGREE, down-rank** | **Closed for working ref** | Further enlarge <2% vs `fine_large`; residual mainly axial (~1.6%). Keep published ref; carry ≲2% abs-P. Doc 43 |
| R07 shell match overstated (probe vs element integral; corr≠amplitude) | **AGREE, down-rank** | **Closed (metrics)** | Midplane corr Q≈0.90, shape L2≈0.20; full-glass L2≈0.9; element ΔP_outer≈0.027. Doc 43 |
| R08 23% coil-field / 5 mm section | **AGREE** as an audit item | **Closed (audit)** | Fresh coil-only \|Bz\| ~20% low; 5 mm ~0.001%; enlarge +1.2%. Do not retune js0. Doc 43 |
| R09 analytic phase conjugate | **AGREE** as reference bug | **Closed (code)** | α=(1−i)/δ under exp(+iωt); weak-limit E phase gate; see Doc 43 |
| R10 prescribed-T twin | **AGREE** | Inconclusive | Retract “no-go because controlled test failed”. Freeze-T now also disables BC. **Doc77 (2026-09-30):** natural mesh refine no longer blanket-forbidden — gated by foundation A/B/C |
| R11 windowed U_rms | **AGREE** | Historical | Endpoint 55% stays rejected. Do not claim stationarity; T_std still evolving |
| R12 Picard joint norm, k-scaling MMS, GPU μ swap | **AGREE** | Defer | After Stage 1 thermal path |
| P0-03 real feedback | **AGREE** | Keep | Do not redesign |
| P1-02 Q path is 3D EM→Euler | **AGREE** | Keep | No axi rotate-back |

### 2.1 Explicit disagreements / scope limits

1. **Batches A–E “closed.”** Doc 40 is right that this was too broad. Cursor’s earlier summary meant “instrumentation and reduced EM workflow delivered,” not “physical acceptance passed.” Corrected here.
2. **Independent EM is not discarded.** Domain, support, and mode-label issues require **requalification**, not a new architecture. Shape-gate *tendency* (outer-shell power, mid-plane correlation) remains preliminary evidence with declared uncertainty.
3. **R03 vs R01 order.** Closing the cumulative **applied** energy identity does not need the 18% area to be gone first. Quantitative paper-T comparison **does**. Do not tune `h` or skull δ to hide the area gap.
4. **Natural “no-go.”** Replace with: quantitative Jacoutot cell remains **open**; the previous prescribed-T pair is **inconclusive**. **Doc77:** natural is client-priority mainline; foundation hydrostatic / conduction / de Vahl Davis before French cylinder refine.

---

## 3. Literature contract v0 (operational freeze)

Sources in-tree: `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_literature_parameters.h`, stirring/natural geometry headers, Docs 09/20/23. Journal/thesis PDFs were **not** re-read for this file. Missing page/equation provenance is `OPEN_PARAMETER`, not a guessed number.

### 3.1 Two regimes (do not mix reloads)

| Item | Natural (CEP-like) | Stirred (Jacoutot CES 2008 track) |
|---|---|---|
| Absorbed-power **target** | 50 kW | 60 kW |
| Frequency | **282 kHz** (`OPEN` vs 300 kHz) | 300 kHz |
| Production dp | 15 mm | 5 mm |
| Axis | +z | +z |
| Stirrer | none | 10 rpm |
| Melt R / H (code) | reduced cylinder; H **0.185 m** (`OPEN` vs 0.21) | CAD glass-z: R=0.25 m, z∈[0, 0.21] m |
| Coil stack | reduced seven-loop | 0.230 m (`OPEN` vs melt 0.21 m; EREBUS 230 mm) |
| T0 | 1473 K | 1473 K |
| σ(T) law | CEP 2008 table-1 family; σ(1473) **16 S/m** | Thesis IV.10 family; σ(1473) **~24.75 S/m** (`OPEN` vs 16) |
| ρ0 | case physics (`OPEN` 2750 vs 2800) | stirring physics (`OPEN` vs Table-1 2750) |
| EM control (baseline) | fixed-current | **fixed-current** |
| A_ind (production) | off | off |
| cp/k/μ (baseline) | **constant at T0** | **constant at T0** |
| Q deposit | 3D EM → Euler CIC | 3D EM → Euler CIC |
| Skull (production) | Level-1 resistance optional | Level-1 `--skull-resistance` + `--free-crust-resistance`, δ=5 mm |
| Lid | **none** | **none** |
| Heat-loss rescale | not paper physics | `--balance-heat-loss` forbidden for comparison |
| Boundary area (SPH) | continuum geometric faces | **`--boundary-area-normalize` on** (default): scale shell weights to tagging-cylinder A; not an h tune. Opt out: `--no-boundary-area-normalize` |

Generator 400 kW is supply-side, never a glass Joule target.

### 3.2 Observables

| Name | Status | Rule |
|---|---|---|
| `P_em_recon` / `P_em_particles` / `P_grid_at_em` | EM diagnostics | Not the fluid energy source |
| `P_heat_fluid` | Sampled fluid Q·V | Used for live heating power |
| `E_Q_applied` | ∫ P_heat_fluid dt | Energy identity |
| `E_boundary_out` | ∫ discrete face flux dt | Same weights as the T update |
| `Np_sph`, `Nu_sph` | SPH candidates | Keep suffix. Paper L, ΔT, which walls: **OPEN** |
| Windowed U_rms, signed T_z | Stirring diagnostics | Means over a stated rev window; not stationarity |
| T_mean, T_std, Tmax−Tmin | **First paper family (Stage 3)** | After R01–R04 and EM requalification |
| Natural cell / Ra–Nu | Deferred | Inconclusive diagnostic |

### 3.3 Error budget (not a paper-contour allowance)

Separate, when quoting a mismatch: numerical uncertainty, coil+glass simplification, `--aind=off`, discrete-area bias, σ(T) table conflict, transient vs paper stationary field, figure digitization. An unexplained gap is **unresolved**, not automatically “model error” or “solver bug.”

---

## 4. Stage 1 acceptance (provisional engineering tolerances)

Written **before** the next production comparison. Not literature-derived.

| Check | Provisional gate |
|---|---|
| Q-only applied heat vs ΔH | \|R_E\| / max(\|E_Q\|, \|ΔH\|) < **2%** under refinement |
| Insulated diffusion | ΔH consistent with k; no false pass on “negative energy-cap” |
| Coupled residual using applied Q and discrete BC | same 2% on a short multi-refresh run |
| Validation | ≥ **3** completed periodic EM refreshes, requested time completed, transfer history, finite fields |
| Variable-cp / local μ | cannot `validation_passed` |
| Insulated / `--zero-q` | must **not** require positive heat loss |

R03 area 1–2% remains the target **before** claiming paper T; it is not required to land the applied-energy identity.

---

## 5. What this slice implemented (2026-09-21)

Code (constant-property path; no 10-rev rerun):

- `FrenchAppliedEnergyLedger`: `R_E = ΔH − E_Q_applied + E_boundary_out`. Instantaneous residual uses `P_heat_fluid`, not `P_em`.
- Fluid Q is (re)sampled every advection step; out-of-grid is tracked on those samples (stirring).
- Shared `evaluateFrenchCouplingRunGates`: smoke ≠ validation; validation needs ≥3 periodic refreshes, completed time, and a ledger residual. Variable-cp cannot `validation_passed`. Positive heat loss is not required.
- `--freeze-prescribed-t` now also skips Robin/radiation and skull-shell thermal updates.
- Both cases compile (`ninja` stirring + natural). Startup host identity `applied_energy_check` runs before the time loop.

Not in the first code drop: production area 1–2%, coupled reload energy identity.

Follow-up evidence: [`42_THERMAL_SOURCE_BOUNDARY_VERIFICATION.md`](42_THERMAL_SOURCE_BOUNDARY_VERIFICATION.md) (host double identities, GPU insulated MMS, multi-face reload ratios, **ledger H = double Σ ρ cp (T−T0) V**). After the ledger fix, stirring 0.5 s has `energy_residual_H_rel≈0.5%`; 2 s with ≥3 refreshes reaches **`validation_passed=1`** (`energy_residual_H_rel≈0.013%`). The earlier `≈1` residual was a float Σ ρ cp T V diagnostic, not a physics failure.

## 6. Next bounded action

1. Stage 3 r0 + sensitivity (Doc 44): 1-rev baseline and interval/normalize diagnostics done. EM interval robust; normalize-off moves loss ~−8%.
2. Next optional: 5-rev observation window (T_std / loss/P drift). No 1000 s.
3. (Deferred) Dedicated Q-only / insulated / cooling helper on the reload.


<!-- ===== END 41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION_2026-09-21.md ===== -->

<a id="42-thermal-source-boundary-verificationmd"></a>

# File: `42_THERMAL_SOURCE_BOUNDARY_VERIFICATION.md`

# Thermal source and boundary verification (Stage 1 / Doc 40 R01–R04)

Date: 2026-09-21  
Predecessor: [`41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION_2026-09-21.md`](41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION_2026-09-21.md)  
Code: `electromagnetic_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_one_way.h`, `test_3d_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_mms.cpp`, stirring/natural cases

This is the Stage 1 evidence pack requested by Doc 40. It does **not** close a literature temperature comparison, a 1–2% production area gate, or a coupled multi-refresh energy identity on the stirring reload.

---

## 1. Gates written before the GPU MMS

Provisional engineering tolerances (Doc 41 §4), not literature-derived:

| Check | Gate | Role here |
|---|---|---|
| Host Q-only / Robin identities | relative < **1e-8** in **double** | Algebra of `T += Q dt/(ρ cp)` and `q·(V/δ)` |
| Insulated Q+diffusion (GPU) | \|E_thermal − E_Q\| / E_Q < **2%** | MMS box, one SPHSystem |
| Dirichlet wall dump | compliance > 0.95 and energy leaves the body | MMS box |
| Production area | 1–2% vs geometry | **not** claimed; reported only |
| Coupled ledger | \|R_E\| / scale < **2%** | **open** (see §6) |
| Validation | ≥3 periodic EM refreshes | 0.5 s smoke has 1 refresh → not exercised |

Production `Real` is `float`. A naïve `T += ΔT` at T0=1473 K with French-scale explicit `ΔT` can be smaller than `ulp(T)`. The kernels reconstruct `T = T0 + ΔT` from a stored increment, so this is a **host-identity** issue, not a proof that production temperature is frozen.

---

## 2. Host identities (`verifyFrenchThermalStage1HostAlgebra`)

Single-particle algebra in **double** (ρ=2800, cp=1200, dt=10⁻⁴, Q=10⁶ W/m³, V=10⁻⁶ m³, T0=1473 K; Robin h=300, δ=0.0075 m, T_cool=300 K):

| Quantity | Observed |
|---|---:|
| `q_rel` = \|ΔH − Q V dt\| / \|Q V dt\| | **2.02272e-09** |
| `cool_rel` = \|ΔH + P_loss dt\| / \|P_loss dt\| | **1.01764e-10** |
| `float_ulp_swallows_dT` | **1** (1473.f + ΔT_float == 1473.f) |
| Synthetic cylinder corners | n_corner=4 |
| A_side exclusive / multi-face over geom | 1.22413 / 1.34095 |
| A_bottom exclusive / multi-face over geom | 1.13067 / 1.44213 |

The synthetic cylinder **over-counts** relative to the geometric surface (coarse dp=0.02 packing). It only shows that non-exclusive weights recover corner area that exclusive tagging drops. It is **not** a 2% production packing gate.

A first MMS attempt used `Real` (float) and `1e-6` relative: it failed immediately with `q_only_identity_failed` (238 ms, GPU path never reached). That is expected: ΔT ≈ 3×10⁻⁵ K < ulp(1473 K) ≈ 1.8×10⁻⁴ K.

---

## 3. GPU MMS — one SPHSystem

Do not construct a second `SPHSystem` in the same process: tearing one down and creating another has hung the SYCL runtime (~900 s then disconnect). The case now runs host identities, insulated Q+diffusion, then `ResetOphelieThermalStateToInitialCK` and Dirichlet k=4 in **one** system.

Executable: `build/tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_mms/bin/test_3d_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_mms`

Box Lattice, dp=0.04 m, n=512, ρ=2500, cp=1200, k=4, T0=300 K, Q=5×10⁴ W/m³, dt=0.1 s, 5 steps.

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Insulated `energy_rel` | **−7.45058e-08** |
| Insulated max T | 300.008 K (matches 5 × Q dt/(ρ cp) = 0.00833 K) |
| E_joule = E_thermal | 819.2 J |
| Dirichlet n_boundary | 387 |
| Boundary compliance | **1** |
| Dirichlet `energy_rel` | **−0.756** (energy leaves; not a 2% conservation claim) |
| `passed` | **1**, exit 0, wall ~3 s |

This closes the **insulated Q+diffusion** GPU identity on a small solid lattice. It is not moving-particle CIC, not Robin production BC, and not T=1473 K.

---

## 4. Multi-face boundary weights (R03, code + one reload)

Kernel and host audit share `frenchCombinedBoundaryHeatFluxWm2`. Face weights `w_side`, `w_bottom`, `w_free` are **non-exclusive** (a corner particle in two shells contributes to both fluxes). Exclusive `face` remains a diagnostic tag.

Stirring production reload, `--aind=off`, `--constant-thermal-rheology`, `--end-time=0.5`, no skull, shell = 1.5×dp = 0.01 m, n_glass=322458:

| Audit | A_disc/A_geom side / bottom / free | n_corner / n_multi_face |
|---|---|---|
| vs CAD cylinder | **0.862 / 0.846 / 1.043** | 855 / 1994 |
| vs melt extent | **0.874 / 0.846 / 1.043** | 855 / 1994 |

Discrete areas: 0.284 / 0.166 / 0.205 m². CAD geometry: 0.330 / 0.196 / 0.196 m².

Doc 26 exclusive-tag ratios were ~0.82 / 0.81. Multi-face recovered some corner area; **side/bottom remain 13–15% short** of the 1–2% target. Do not tune `h` or skull δ to hide this. Free-surface ratio >1 is packing in the top shell, not a paper match.

Uniform-T0 loss (no skull): side 100.1 kW, bottom 6.82 kW, free conv+rad 48.4 kW, total 155 kW vs P_target 60 kW. Geometric 0-D CAD estimate at the same h is ~171 kW. The particle/geometry gap tracks the area ratios.

No nonuniform-flux manufactured test yet.

---

## 5. Coupled stirring smoke (before ledger H fix)

Same 0.5 s command **before** switching H to relative double energy. Host algebra and applied-energy **algebra** identities passed at startup. Fluid Q is sampled every advection (`fluid_samples=166`). `out_of_grid_max=0`.

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| `smoke_passed` | **1** |
| `validation_passed` | **0** (`fail_reasons=periodic_refresh,energy_residual`) |
| periodic refreshes | 1 (need ≥3 for validation) |
| `P_em_recon` | 5.996e4 W |
| `P_heat_fluid` (end) | 5.924e4 W |
| `E_Q_applied` | 2.964e4 J |
| `E_boundary_out` | 7.844e4 J |
| `energy_residual_H_rel` | **1.02** (fails 2%) |
| T_mean | 1473 → 1472.61 K |

`french_energy_budget.csv` `thermal_energy` jumped by O(10⁶) J between rows while physical cooling from ΔT≈−0.4 K is only O(10⁴) J. Cause: case-local `hostThermalEnergy` accumulated **`float` Σ ρ cp T V**. WCSPH `VolumetricMeasure` is not a conserved mass; at T≈1473 K that product is ~1.88×10⁸ J, so a ~1% volume wiggle swamps ΔH.

Gates behaved as specified: a 0.5 s run with one refresh is smoke, not validation.

---

## 5b. Ledger H fix + coupled evidence (2026-09-21 follow-up)

**Code:** `hostThermalEnergyConstantCp` in `electromagnetic_ophelie_french_material_laws.h`:

`H = Σ ρ cp (T − T0) V` accumulated in **double**. Stirring and natural cases call it with `t_ref = T0`. Variable-cp path already used `hostThermalEnthalpyEnergy` (relative to `t_ref`). Ledger `residualHRel` keeps the scale arithmetic in double.

Global `Real` remains `float` on the SYCL path. Field solves are unchanged.

### 0.5 s smoke (same CLI as §5)

| Item | Before | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| `energy_residual_H` | 2.80e6 J | **−385 J** |
| `energy_residual_H_rel` | 1.02 | **0.00491** |
| `thermal_energy_ok` | 0 | **1** |
| CSV `thermal_energy` | ~1.88e8 | ~−4.8e4 (sensible ΔH) |
| `fail_reasons` | `periodic_refresh,energy_residual` | `periodic_refresh` only |

### 2.0 s validation (`--end-time=2`, interval 0.5 s)

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| physical time | 2.000 s |
| periodic refreshes | **3** (expected 4; gate needs ≥3) |
| `E_Q_applied` | 1.183e5 J |
| `E_boundary_out` | 3.136e5 J |
| `energy_residual_H` | **−41 J** |
| `energy_residual_H_rel` | **0.000131** |
| `smoke_passed` | **1** |
| `validation_exercised` | **1** |
| `validation_passed` | **1** |
| `fail_reasons` | none |

This closes the **coupled applied-energy identity** on the production stirring reload for constant-cp / `--aind=off`, under the provisional 2% gate. It does **not** close the 1–2% geometric area target or a paper temperature comparison.

Boundary loss is still integrated with a **stale** `p_loss_live` between monitor samples; residual at 0.01% suggests that error is small on this window, but a per-step flux accumulate remains a follow-up hardening item.

---

## 5c. Explicit geometric area renormalization (R03, 2026-09-21)

### Relation to French literature

| Item | Jacoutot / continuum FEM | This SPH path |
|---|---|---|
| Physics | Robin side/bottom + free conv/rad (same h, ε family) | **Same** coefficients (`h_side=300`, …); skull Level-1 optional |
| Surface measure | Geometric CAD / mesh faces | Shell proxy `A_i = V_i/δ` on tagged particles |
| What we change | — | Correct the **SPH measure** toward geometry; **do not** retune h or skull δ |
| Forbidden legacy | — | `--balance-heat-loss` (scales h/ε); mutually exclusive with normalize |

So this is **not** a new heat-loss *model*. It is an explicit quadrature correction so that at uniform T the particle loss matches the 0-D geometric budget that a continuum code would use with the same h.

### CLI

`--boundary-area-normalize` → `thermal_bc.renormalize_boundary_area_to_geometry`. After multi-face tagging (and every retag), each face weight is scaled by `A_geom/A_disc` of the **tagging** cylinder (melt extent). Scales are logged; update and audit use the same weights.

### Stirring 0.5 s evidence (`--aind=off`, constant-cp, normalize on)

| Quantity | Before normalize (Doc 42 §4) | After |
|---|---:|---:|
| scale side/bottom/free (startup) | 1/1/1 | **1.144 / 1.182 / 0.959** |
| A_disc/A_geom vs melt | 0.874 / 0.846 / 1.043 | **1.000 / 1.000 / 1.000** |
| A_disc/A_geom vs CAD side | 0.862 | 0.987 (height CAD≠melt) |
| T0 side loss | 100.1 kW | **114.5 kW** (0-D melt 114.5 kW) |
| T0 bottom loss | 6.82 kW | **8.06 kW** (0-D 8.06 kW) |
| `h_side_used` | 300 | **300** (unchanged) |
| `energy_residual_H_rel` | 0.49% (ledger fix) | **0.068%** |
| `smoke_passed` | 1 | 1 |

Default is now **on** for the literature baseline (contract v0 + stirring/natural CLI). Opt out with `--no-boundary-area-normalize`. Keep skull/crust as separate physics.

### Nonuniform-T flux (2026-09-21 freeze)

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Host synthetic cylinder + normalize, `T=T0+az z+ar r` | `nonuniform_P_rel` side/bot = **6.6e-4 / 1.6e-3** |
| Production packing (stirring reload), same T law | `rel_side=6.4e-5`, `rel_bot=4.2e-4` (`nonuniform_flux_particles_ok`) |
| Default-normalize 0.5 s smoke | `A` melt ratios 1.000; `energy_residual_H_rel=1.0e-4`; `smoke_passed=1` |

Still open under R03: whether paddle-wetted area should replace the full tagging cylinder in `A_geom` (cavity still counted in the geometric side).

---

## 6. What is closed vs open

**Closed in this slice**

- Host Q-only and Robin identities in double.
- GPU insulated Q+diffusion on one SPHSystem.
- Dirichlet dump + compliance on that box.
- Shared multi-face weights in the T update and the loss audit.
- Smoke ≠ validation; variable-cp still cannot `validation_passed`.
- Production `--aind=off` stirring still starts and smokes after the host-identity fix.
- **Ledger H = double Σ ρ cp (T−T0) V**; coupled residual ≪ 2% on 0.5 s and 2 s stirring.
- **2 s stirring `validation_passed=1`** (constant-cp, `--aind=off`, ≥3 periodic refreshes).
- **Optional `--boundary-area-normalize`**: melt-extent face areas to 1.000; T0 loss matches 0-D with **unchanged h**.
- **`--boundary-area-normalize` default on** (literature contract); host + production nonuniform-T flux checks passed.

**Open (do next, still Stage 1)**

1. Optional: paddle-cavity-aware geometric A_side; per-step boundary-flux accumulate.
2. Stage 2 EM requalification (coil-only dump, outer-domain +1, common-support Q errors, analytic phase).
3. Only then first stirring T comparison (Doc 40 Stage 3).
4. Dedicated Q-only / insulated / cooling helper on the reload (optional).

**Not this cycle:** Stage 2 EM requalification, 1000 s, finer natural mesh, segmented metal, dynamic skull, Picard on production, paper T contours.

---

<a id="43-em-reference-requalificationmd"></a>

# File: `43_EM_REFERENCE_REQUALIFICATION.md`

# EM reference requalification (Stage 2 / Doc 40 R05–R09)

Date: 2026-09-21  
Predecessor: [`41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION_2026-09-21.md`](41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION_2026-09-21.md), [`42_THERMAL_SOURCE_BOUNDARY_VERIFICATION.md`](42_THERMAL_SOURCE_BOUNDARY_VERIFICATION.md)  
GetDP tree: [`reference/em_rz_getdp/`](reference/em_rz_getdp/)  
Analytic cylinder: [`reference/em_cylinder_analytic/`](reference/em_cylinder_analytic/)

Stage 1 thermal/source/boundary gates are closed enough for the literature baseline. This document starts **Stage 2**: requalify independent EM evidence without changing the production `--aind=off` architecture.

---

## 1. Scope this slice

| Doc 40 ID | Action this slice | Status |
|---|---|---|
| **R09** analytic phase conjugate | Fix α → `(1−i)/δ` under `exp(+iωt)`; phase gates on weak-limit E | **Closed** |
| **R05** one-way ≠ coil-only | Dedicated coil-only rz dump + azi-avg vs GetDP; three-mode table | **Closed (numerical)** |
| **R06** outer domain | Further enlarge beyond `fine_large` (R / z / both); successive ΔP gate | **Closed for working ref** |
| **R07** shell / support | Common-support L2 + probe vs element shells; midplane vs full-glass | **Closed (metrics)** |
| **R08** source / axis-B | Fresh Flag_coil_only OnPoint; remove hard-coded 23%/5 mm blame | **Closed (audit)** |
| Production `--aind` | Still **off** | Unchanged (now with honest coil-only evidence) |

Provisional engineering notes (not literature tolerances): magnitude/Q gates remain valid after the phase fix; Re/Im must also pass.

---

## 2. R09 — Analytic phase convention fix

### Convention (SPH / GetDP metadata)

```text
time factor: exp(+i ω t)
E = −i ω A
α² = −i ω μ σ  ⇒  α = (1 − i)/δ ,  δ = √(2/(ω μ σ))
weak limit: E_θ = −i ω B₀ r / 2
```

Previous code/docs used `α = (1+i)/δ` and weak `E_θ = +i ω B₀ r / 2`, i.e. the **complex conjugate** of this convention (Doc 40 R09). Magnitudes and Q were unchanged, so magnitude-only tests could not catch it.

### Code / reference updates

- C++: `electromagnetic_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_self_induction.h` — `ophelieCylinderAlpha` returns `(1/δ, −1/δ)`; samples expose `Etheta_re/im`; weak-limit gate checks α phase and `E ≈ −i ω B₀ r/2`.
- Python: `reference/em_cylinder_analytic/generate_reference.py` — same α; metadata records `time_convention` / `alpha_convention`.
- Regenerated `meta_*.json` / `profile_*.csv` under `reference/em_cylinder_analytic/`.

### Evidence

| Check | Result |
|---|---|
| Host weak-limit identity | `cylinder_analytic_weak_limit_ok phase=exp(+iwt) E=-i*omega*B0*r/2` **passed=1** |
| Regenerated α (weak 50 Hz) | `{re: +0.0562, im: −0.0562}` |
| Regenerated α (French-like 300 kHz, σ=16) | `{re: +4.353, im: −4.353}` |
| `test_3d_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_skin` (Picard vs analytic) | **passed=1**: corr_J=0.991, corr_Q=0.988, rel_P=0.031, A_ind/A_coil≈0.054 |

This closes the **reference/phase-validation defect**. It does **not** by itself prove the production SPH induction operator sign; production still runs `--aind=off`.

---

## 3. R05 — Three EM modes at matched geometry (dp=0.02, σ=16, I=1 A peak)

Matched French reduced geometry vs published GetDP `reference_rz.csv` (`fine_large`, working reference — **not** domain-at-infinity). Compare: `compare_sph_getdp.py --mode azi-avg --dp 0.02`.

| Mode | SPH dump | Feedback | `P_sph/P_getdp(J,2πr)` | SPH P_outer | GetDP P_outer | \|ΔP_outer\| | JSON |
|---|---|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| **Coil-only** | `work/sph_rz_coil_only_dp02.csv` | off | **1.009** | 0.695 | 0.700 | **0.0048** | `compare_aziavg_coil_only_stage2_r05.json` |
| One-way | `work/sph_rz_dp02.csv` | on (once) | **1.041** | 0.689 | 0.700 | 0.011 | `compare_aziavg_oneway_stage2_r09.json` |
| Picard | `work/sph_rz_picard_dp02.csv` | iterated | **0.979** | 0.696 | 0.700 | 0.004 | `compare_aziavg_picard_stage2_r09.json` |

### Coil-only run (this cut)

```text
P_complex_coil_only = 1.478 W
feedback_resolve = 0
coil_only_fields = 1   # Br/Bz total columns = coil Biot; J/Q from A_coil recon
A_ind/A_coil ≈ 0.178   # diagnostic Biot of coil-only J; NOT fed into φ
n = 4916 lattice @ dp=0.02
```

CLI: `test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic` with `--ophelie-aind-one-way-feedback=0` and the Doc 30 matched geometry flags (`--target-power=0`, σ=16, I=1 A, edge-flux complex). Dump helper now forces coil Biot into total-B columns when feedback did not run, so the CSV is a consistent **mode-1** artifact.

### Interpretation (honest)

1. Docs 30–35 previously quoted one-way / Picard ratios as if they were coil-only. That labeling error is corrected by the three-mode table.
2. Against this GetDP working reference, **coil-only absolute power agrees to ~0.9%**, closer than one-way (~4.1% high). One-way overshoots; Picard undershoots slightly.
3. This does **not** promote a universal “~5% coil-only error” claim. Outer-domain residual and spatial L2 are quantified in §4–§5. Production `--aind=off` remains a cost/modeling choice, now backed by a dedicated mode-1 dump rather than a mislabeled one-way number.
4. Stirring production σ near ~24.75 S/m is still a separate case; this table is the σ=16 GetDP-matched gate only.

---

## 4. R06 — Outer-domain enlarge beyond `fine_large`

Published working reference remains **`fine_large`** (R_ext=1.5 m, z_pad=0.6 m, P=1.466 W). New cases keep the same glass mesh (lc_g=0.01) and enlarge only the far field (`./run_domain_enlarge.sh`).

| Case | R_ext | z_pad | P (W) | signed ΔP vs fine_large | \|ΔP_outer\| |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| fine_large (published) | 1.5 | 0.6 | 1.4655 | — | — |
| **fine_r2** (radial) | 2.0 | 0.6 | 1.4674 | **+0.13%** | 3e-5 |
| **fine_ztall** (axial) | 1.5 | 0.9 | 1.4889 | **+1.59%** | 4.5e-4 |
| **fine_r2z** (both) | 2.0 | 0.9 | 1.4944 | **+1.97%** | 5.4e-4 |

`summarize_convergence.py --vs fine_large` → `domain_enlarge_gate.pass=true` (all three < 2% on P and shell).

### Interpretation

1. Further **radial** enlargement is essentially negligible (~0.1%). Truncation residual is mainly **axial** (~1.6%).
2. Combined enlarge reaches ~2% — within the predeclared engineering gate, **not** a proof of domain-at-infinity.
3. Do **not** rename metadata to “converged_domain.” Keep `fine_large` as the working SPH compare target; carry **≲2% absolute-P** outer-domain uncertainty (axial-dominated) into any temperature claim that needs absolute watts at fixed I.
4. Optional later: promote `fine_ztall` / `fine_r2z` only if a tighter abs-P budget is required; would need re-compare of SPH dumps.

JSON: `work/convergence_summary.json` (`vs_reference`).

---

## 5. R07 — Common-support L2 (retract “near-perfect shape”)

`compare_sph_getdp.py` now reports, on the GetDP probe grid with SPH azi-avg samples:

- `vol_sph_over_nominal`
- `shell_GetDP_probe` (2πr probe weights) vs `shell_GetDP_element_J` (triangle J integral)
- volume-weighted relative L2 for absolute Q, shape-normalized Q, and |J_θ|
- mid-plane subset (z∈[0.09,0.12]) separately from full-glass masked probes
- correlation as supplementary only

### Three-mode results @ dp=0.02 (same dumps as §3)

| Mode | vol_SPH/nom | \|ΔP_outer\| probe | \|ΔP_outer\| element | L2(Q) mid | L2(Q shape) mid | corr Q mid | L2(Q) full masked |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| Coil-only | 0.954 | 0.0048 | **0.028** | 0.38 | 0.20 | **0.90** | 0.91 |
| One-way | 0.954 | 0.011 | **0.034** | 0.42 | 0.20 | 0.90 | 0.91 |
| Picard | 0.954 | 0.0036 | **0.027** | 0.35 | 0.20 | 0.90 | 0.90 |

### Interpretation

1. Probe-shell agreement (~0.005) was **overstated** as shape validation: element-integral FEM P_outer differs by ~0.027 (Doc 40 R07 recomputation confirmed).
2. SPH represents ~4.6% less than the nominal cylinder volume at dp=0.02.
3. **Mid-plane** radial Q/J agreement is usable (corr≈0.90, shape L2≈0.20) but not “near perfect.”
4. **Full-glass** masked Q L2≈0.9 with near-zero/negative correlation — axial deposition and end regions dominate the spatial mismatch. Shell fractions alone cannot certify Q(r,z).
5. No equal-power rescale in these numbers. Correlation is not used as a pass gate.

JSON fields: `common_support_L2` in the three `compare_aziavg_*_stage2_*.json` files.

---

## 6. R08 — Coil-only axis B / source normalization audit

Legacy `metadata.json` hard-coded `getdp_coil_only_T=1.105817e-5` (rel_err=0.228) and blamed “5 mm coil section + truncation.” Doc 40 already showed free-space finite-section shift is ~0.001%. This cut recomputes the comparison.

### Tools

- `./run_coil_only_audit.sh` — GetDP `Flag_coil_only=1` at `fine_large` and `fine_r2z`
- `audit_coil_axis_b.py` → `work/coil_axis_b_audit_r08.json`
- `export_reference_csv.py` — no longer copies the hard-coded 23% block; writes fresh OnPoint metadata when `b_axis.txt` exists

### Results

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---:|
| Analytic filament |Bz| (z=0.105) | 1.4329×10⁻⁵ T |
| Analytic 5 mm×5 mm mean |Bz| | 1.4329×10⁻⁵ T (**rel ≈ 1.1×10⁻⁵**) |
| GetDP coil-only `fine_large` OnPoint |Bz| | 1.1450×10⁻⁵ T (**rel_err ≈ 0.201**) |
| GetDP coil-only `fine_r2z` OnPoint |Bz| | 1.1586×10⁻⁵ T (**rel_err ≈ 0.191**) |
| Domain enlarge Δ|Bz| | **+1.19%** only |
| SPH near-axis mean |Bz_coil| (dp=0.02 dump) | 1.219×10⁻⁵ T (lattice inset from r=0) |

### Interpretation

1. **5 mm section is not the cause** of the historical ~23% claim (confirmed numerically here).
2. **Far-field truncation is not the dominant cause** either: enlarging beyond the published domain recovers only ~1% of axis |Bz|.
3. A **~20% coil-only axis-|Bz| deficit vs filament remains** under the present GetDP axi setup (Dirichlet on axis/outer, VolAxiSqu `js0=2π I/A`). Treat it as an open formulation/extraction residual — **do not retune `js0` to erase it**. Absorbed-power / shell gates (R05–R07) already close without that knob.
4. Legacy hard-coded metadata and the “~23% / 5 mm” limitation string are **removed** from the export path.

js0 convention (kept): `js0_φ = 2π I_peak / Area` for Flag_Axi + VolAxiSqu S0 (without 2π, axis B historically collapses by ~2π).

---

## 7. What remains / Stage 3 hand-off

1. ~~R05–R09 numerical/audit items~~ → **done** here (R08 residual axis-B noted, not “fixed” by retuning).
2. Optional later: deeper GetDP axi B-extraction / axis-BC study for the remaining ~20% coil-only |Bz| — **not** required before Stage 3 if temperature claims use fixed-power / shape-dominated observables and carry the declared abs-P / midplane-shape uncertainties.
3. Written cost/accuracy statement for `--aind=off` tied to the **temperature** observable (Stage 3).

Do **not** enable O(N²) induction on the full fluid lattice as the next step.

---

## 8. Commands

```bash
source /opt/intel/oneapi/setvars.sh
python3 docs/ophelie/reference/em_cylinder_analytic/generate_reference.py

cd build && ninja test_3d_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_skin test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic -j$(nproc)
./tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_skin/bin/test_3d_ophelie_analytic_cylinder_skin

./tests/extra_source_and_tests/3d_examples/test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic/bin/test_3d_ophelie_french_aind_diagnostic \
  --dp=0.02 \
  --glass-radius=0.25 --glass-height=0.21 --glass-center=0,0,0.105 \
  --coil-radius=0.285 --coil-num-loops=7 --coil-z-min=-0.01 --coil-z-max=0.22 \
  --coil-current=1.0 --frequency=300000 --sigma=16 --target-power=0 \
  --ophelie-current-form=edge-flux --ophelie-edge-flux-complex=1 \

source ~/opt/onelab/env.sh
cd docs/ophelie/reference/em_rz_getdp
./run_domain_enlarge.sh
./run_coil_only_audit.sh
python3 compare_sph_getdp.py --sph work/sph_rz_coil_only_dp02.csv --getdp reference_rz.csv \
  --mode azi-avg --dp 0.02 --out-json work/compare_aziavg_coil_only_stage2_r05.json
python3 compare_sph_getdp.py --sph work/sph_rz_dp02.csv --getdp reference_rz.csv \
  --mode azi-avg --dp 0.02 --out-json work/compare_aziavg_oneway_stage2_r09.json
python3 compare_sph_getdp.py --sph work/sph_rz_picard_dp02.csv --getdp reference_rz.csv \
  --mode azi-avg --dp 0.02 --out-json work/compare_aziavg_picard_stage2_r09.json
python3 summarize_convergence.py --root work --vs fine_large
```
