<a id="44-first-stirring-literature-comparisonmd"></a>

# File: `44_FIRST_STIRRING_LITERATURE_COMPARISON.md`

# First stirring literature comparison (Stage 3 / Doc 40)

Date: 2026-09-21  
> **Disposition (docs 45–46):** this report is a **historical development archive**. Do **not** read `validation_passed=1` or E_rel≈1–2% as closed proof of per-step energy identity, refresh schedule, or Q-shape qualification. See Doc 45 defects F01–F07 and Doc 46 Batch A/B repairs in progress. Paper contours remain OPEN.

Contract: [`reference/literature_contract_v0.json`](reference/literature_contract_v0.json)  
Predecessors: Docs [`41`](41_LITERATURE_CONTRACT_AND_REVIEW_DISPOSITION_2026-09-21.md), [`42`](42_THERMAL_SOURCE_BOUNDARY_VERIFICATION.md), [`43`](43_EM_REFERENCE_REQUALIFICATION.md)  
Case: `test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em`  
Evidence dir: [`reference/stirring_stage3_r0/`](reference/stirring_stage3_r0/)

This is the **first bounded Stage-3 slice**: corrected Stage-1 thermal ledger + area normalize + Stage-2 EM uncertainty carry, on the stirred literature baseline. It is **not** a Jacoutot figure reproduction and does **not** claim stationarity.

---

## 1. Claim level (frozen)

| May claim | Must not claim |
|---|---|
| Auditable SPH T_mean / T_std / ΔT / loss book under the contract | Bit-identical paper contours |
| `validation_passed` with applied-energy residual & ≥3 EM refreshes | Stationary thermal uniformity (T_std still evolves historically) |
| Comparison to **historical** 10-rev Doc 20 controls (different code stack) | Paper Np/Nu (keep `_sph` labels; L OPEN) |
| Uncertainty budget next to every mismatch | “Within X% of paper ⇒ model error” |

Journal/thesis PDFs and digitized figure targets are **not** in-tree → paper-contour match status = **OPEN**.

---

## 2. Frozen run identity (Stage-3 r0)

| Item | Value |
|---|---|
| Regime | mechanically stirred (Jacoutot CES track) |
| Power target | 60 kW absorbed (fixed-current calibration) |
| f / σ(T0) / T0 | 300 kHz / **24.75 S/m** / 1473 K |
| Properties | **constant** cp/k/μ at T0 |
| `--aind` | **off** |
| Skull / free crust | Level-1 δ=5 mm both |
| Boundary area | `--boundary-area-normalize` **on** |
| Coupling | periodic, `em-update-interval=0.5` s |
| Horizon (r0 slice) | **1 revolution** (`--end-time=6`) |
| Horizon (obs window) | **5 revolutions** (`--end-time=30`) — same flags |
| Gate | `--require-validation` |

Historical Doc 20 10-rev logs remain **controls**, not proof of this corrected stack.

---

## 3. Uncertainty carry (from Stage 1–2)

| Source | Carry into T claim |
|---|---|
| R06 GetDP outer domain | Absolute EM watts ≲**2%** (axial-dominated) at σ=16 working ref |
| R07 Q shape | Midplane usable (corr≈0.90); full-glass L2 large — do not claim full r–z Q match |
| R08 coil-only axis \|Bz\| | ~**20%** vs filament remains; **not** a power-gate blocker; do not retune js0 |
| σ table | Production σ(1473)≈24.75 vs GetDP EM gate σ=16 (`OPEN`) |
| `--aind=off` | Cost/modeling choice; coil-only vs GetDP P≈1.009 at σ=16 |
| Area normalize | Geometric face A; not an h tune; paddle-cavity A_side still approximate |

---

## 4. Results — Stage-3 r0 (1 revolution)

Log: `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/run.log`  
Summary: `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/summary.json`  
Wall ≈ 0.036 h (~2.2 min Phase B after EM startup).

| Metric | Stage-3 r0 (1 rev) | Doc 20 historical (10 rev end) |
|---|---:|---:|
| validation_passed | **1** | n/a (old gates) |
| energy_residual_H_rel | **0.012** (< 2%) | n/a |
| periodic_refreshes | 11 | 120 updates |
| P_joule / kW | 59.85 | 60.0 |
| P_heat_fluid / kW | 59.12 | — |
| total_heat_loss / kW | 67.35 | 57.87 |
| loss/P | **1.13** | ~0.96 |
| T_mean / K | 1472.6 | 1473.4 |
| T_std / K | **4.27** | 21.1 |
| ΔT = Tmax−Tmin / K | **41.3** | ~147 |
| T_out / T_ctr / K | 1471.7 / 1472.0 | 1470 / 1466 |
| U_rms / m/s | 0.089 | 0.073 |
| Nu_sph (label) | 126 | ~30 |
| out_of_grid_max | 0 | 0 |
| h_side_used (skull) | 120 | 120 |

### Read (honest)

1. **Gates pass** on the corrected stack: applied-energy residual ~1.2%, ≥3 refreshes, `--require-validation`, skull+crust, area-normalize, `--aind=off`.
2. **1 rev is not stationary.** T_std=4.3 K here vs ~21 K at Doc 20’s 10-rev endpoint — consistent with Doc 40 R11 (T_std still evolving). Do not claim uniformity.
3. **loss/P≈1.13** (net cool) vs historical ~0.96: this run enables **area-normalize** (larger discrete losses) and is early-time; Doc 20 was a longer equilibrating window without the Stage-1 normalize default. Not a contradiction until a matched longer window is re-run.
4. Mid-plane T(r): cooler near the wall (`r≈0.25 → 1467 K`) than center (`r≈0.01 → 1473 K`) — archived in `summary.json`. **No paper radial target** attached.
5. Np_sph / Nu_sph remain **SPH labels** (paper L OPEN).

### Mid-plane T(r) snapshot (dr=0.02 bins)

See `summary.json` → `midplane_T_profile_dr0p02`. First/last bins: T(0.01)≈1472.8 K, T(0.25)≈1467.1 K.

---

## 5. Observation window — 5 revolutions (same flags as r0)

Evidence: [`reference/stirring_stage3_r0/obs_5rev/`](reference/stirring_stage3_r0/obs_5rev/)  
Compare: [`obs_5rev/compare_1rev_vs_5rev.json`](reference/stirring_stage3_r0/obs_5rev/compare_1rev_vs_5rev.json)  
Snapshots: [`obs_5rev/rev_snapshots.json`](reference/stirring_stage3_r0/obs_5rev/rev_snapshots.json)  
Wall ≈ 0.186 h (~11 min Phase B).

### End-of-run vs r0

| Metric | r0 (1 rev) | obs (5 rev) |
|---|---:|---:|
| validation_passed | **1** | **1** |
| energy_residual_H_rel | 0.012 | **0.017** |
| periodic_refreshes | 11 | 59 |
| P_joule / kW | 59.85 | 59.18 |
| total_heat_loss / kW | 67.35 | 65.97 |
| loss/P | **1.13** | **1.11** |
| T_mean / K | 1472.6 | 1470.8 |
| T_std / K | **4.27** | **16.3** |
| ΔT / K | **41.3** | **157** |
| U_rms / m/s | 0.089 | 0.083 |
| Nu_sph (label) | 126 | 33 |

### Per-rev drift (nearest monitor + energy join)

| rev≈ | T_mean | T_std | ΔT | loss/P |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| 1 | 1472.57 | 4.26 | 41.4 | 1.141 |
| 2 | 1472.13 | 7.98 | 77.3 | 1.135 |
| 3 | 1471.68 | 11.18 | 107 | 1.134 |
| 4 | 1471.26 | 13.89 | 133 | 1.130 |
| 5 | 1470.83 | 16.30 | 157 | 1.129 |

rev5−rev1: ΔT_mean≈**−1.7 K**, ΔT_std≈**+12.0 K**, Δ(ΔT)≈**+115 K**, Δ(loss/P)≈**−0.011**.

### Read

1. **Gates still pass** at 5 rev: E_rel 1.7% < 2%, refreshes OK, out-of-grid 0.
2. **T family is still evolving** — T_std/ΔT rise roughly linearly on this window; do **not** claim thermal stationarity. Trend is toward Doc 20’s 10-rev T_std≈21 K (different stack; control only).
3. **loss/P stays ~1.11–1.14** under normalize-on; early-time net cool is stable, not a runaway. Still do not retune to Doc 20’s ~0.96.
4. Mid-plane wall cooling deepens: T(0.01)≈1471.6 K, T(0.25)≈1450.4 K at end of 5 rev.
5. This closes Doc 40’s optional **5-rev observation** item; skull-δ sensitivity is in §6; longer campaigns remain out of scope this cycle.

---

## 6. Bounded sensitivity (1-rev, same skull / aind / constant-cp)

JSON: [`reference/stirring_stage3_r0/sensitivity_compare.json`](reference/stirring_stage3_r0/sensitivity_compare.json)

| Run | interval | area-normalize | skull/crust δ | E_rel | T_mean | T_std | ΔT | loss/P | h_side | validation |
|---|---:|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---|
| **r0 baseline** | 0.5 s | on | **5 mm** | 0.012 | 1472.56 | 4.27 | 41.3 | 1.125 | 120 | 1 |
| em-interval=1.0 | **1.0 s** | on | 5 mm | 0.013 | 1472.56 | 4.26 | 41.6 | 1.127 | 120 | 1 |
| no area-normalize | 0.5 s | **off** | 5 mm | 0.018 | 1472.89 | 3.85 | 39.0 | 1.039 | 120 | 1 |
| skull/crust δ=10 mm | 0.5 s | on | **10 mm** | 0.004 | 1473.71 | 2.79 | 27.4 | **0.740** | **75** | 1 |

### vs baseline

| Perturbation | ΔT_mean | ΔT_std | rel Δ(ΔT) | rel Δ(loss/P) |
|---|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| interval 0.5→1.0 | **−0.001 K** | −0.004 K | +0.6% | +0.2% |
| normalize off | +0.33 K | **−0.42 K** | **−5.7%** | **−7.7%** (−7.6% total loss) |
| δ 5→10 mm | +1.14 K | **−1.48 K** | **−34%** | **−34%** (−34% total loss) |

### Read

1. **EM refresh interval** is robust on this 1-rev horizon: T family and loss/P barely move; keep **0.5 s** as baseline.
2. **Area-normalize** is a moderate lever: turning it off cuts discrete loss ~8% and shrinks ΔT/T_std modestly. h and skull are unchanged — this is the R03 measure effect, not an h tune.
3. **Skull/crust δ** is the **strong** lever on this horizon: 5→10 mm drops h_side 120→75, cuts total loss ~34%, and pulls loss/P from 1.13 to 0.74 (toward Doc 20’s historical ~0.96, but for the wrong reason). **Do not retune δ** to chase paper or Doc 20 balances; keep Level-1 freeze at **5 mm**.
4. Keep literature defaults **normalize on** and **δ=5 mm**. Normalize-off and δ≠5 mm are diagnostic only.
5. Stop-condition §8 item 5 is **exercised** for interval + area measure + skull-δ.

---

## 7. Matched / mismatched assumptions


**Matched (operational contract)**

- Stirred geometry R=0.25 m, H=0.21 m, 10 rpm, 60 kW target, 300 kHz, T0=1473 K  
- Fixed-current, `--aind=off`, constant properties, Level-1 skull+crust, no lid, no `--balance-heat-loss`  
- Q path: 3D EM → Euler CIC → fluid sample  

**Mismatched / OPEN**

- Paper figure digitization absent  
- Paper Np/Nu characteristic L and ΔT definition OPEN  
- σ(T) table family vs CEP Table-1 16 S/m OPEN  
- Coil stack 0.230 m vs melt 0.21 m (EREBUS) OPEN  
- SPH still transient through 5 rev (T_std rising); Doc 20 shows T_std still rising through rev 10  
- GetDP EM requalification at σ=16, not production 24.75  

---

## 8. Stop-condition check (Doc 40 §7)

| Condition | r0 + sensitivity + 5-rev status |
|---|---|
| 1 Energy residual within frozen 2% | **Pass** (1.2–1.8% on 1-rev suite; **1.7%** at 5 rev) |
| 2 Area bias without quantified impact | Normalize **on**; off-diagnostic Δ(loss)≈−8% quantified |
| 3 EM source/support obscures claim | Stage 2 working gates OK; axis-B residual noted |
| 4 Skipped time / refreshes / out-of-grid | **Pass** (1-rev×3 and 5-rev) |
| 5 Numerical sensitivity | **Done** for interval + area-normalize + skull/crust δ (this cut) |
| 6 Unknown paper defs block apples-to-apples | Contours **OPEN** — SPH family only |
| 7 Longer run to hide discrepancy | 5-rev is observation only; not used to hide mismatch |

---

2. ~~Bounded sensitivity (interval + normalize-off + skull-δ)~~ → **done**.
3. ~~5-rev observation (T_std / loss/P drift)~~ → **done** (`obs_5rev/`).


Compare: `reference/stirring_stage3_r0/sensitivity_compare.json`, `obs_5rev/compare_1rev_vs_5rev.json`, `sens_skull_delta_10mm/compare_vs_r0.json`.

<a id="45-post-doc40-review-and-next-actions-2026-09-22md"></a>

# File: `45_POST_DOC40_REVIEW_AND_NEXT_ACTIONS_2026-09-22.md`

# Post-Doc-40 review: correctness, bottlenecks and next actions

Date: 2026-09-22  
Reviewed package: `chatgpt_post_doc40_review_2026-09-22.tar.gz`  
Predecessors: Doc 40 and implementation reports 41–44  
Audience: project owner and Cursor

## 1. Decision

Preserve the new implementation and archived runs, but do not close this cycle as a numerically validated stirring thermal baseline yet. It is a reproducible development baseline with substantially improved instrumentation and several remaining correctness defects.

Two immediate priorities precede a quantitative literature comparison:

1. Make the actual production thermal update and energy ledger consistent, including single-precision behavior and gate failure paths.
2. Explain the large spatial EM discrepancy under matched source conditions. Total absorbed power agreement does not qualify a heat-source distribution for a temperature-field comparison.

The next scientific question should be: **under the same explicitly verified coil excitation, where does the SPH–GetDP Q(r,z) disagreement originate: source geometry/field, data export, spatial comparison, or the conducting-domain solve?**

Paper provenance and figure extraction can proceed as documentation work. Do not substitute digitization, a conductivity-table switch or a natural-convection campaign for the two priorities above. Keep production A_ind off while testing the reduced EM cases. Do not retune skull thickness or coil current normalization to force agreement.

## 2. What was checked

Read reports 41–44, package README, relevant diffs and actual thermal/EM/production sources, reference scripts, selected JSON and the supplied production logs/CSV. Used the preceding snapshot only for unchanged supporting source.

Independent checks performed:

- Reproduced float32 thermal arithmetic in the production update order.
- Extracted the actual shared gate structs/functions into a minimal C++17 harness, compiled them with g++, and ran failure counterexamples.
- Recomputed the 5-revolution energy ratios from archived output.
- Checked GetDP Table field offsets against its official format documentation and the archived raw tokens.
- Numerically integrated a seven-loop Biot–Savart field at the exact SPH probe coordinate recorded in the audit JSON.

No full SPHinXsys GPU executable was rebuilt or rerun. The package omits the framework, reloads and raw FEM/SPH EM fields required for a full reproduction. Reported production results are archival evidence, not a new solver execution. No production source was modified in this review.

## 3. Progress that should be retained

| Change since Doc 40 | Assessment |
|---|---|
| Separate fluid Q power and cumulative bookkeeping | Useful implementation progress; remaining problems listed below |
| Constant-cp sensible energy reduced in double | Improves numerical conditioning; mass/volume convention still needs verification |
| Variable-property validation blocked | Correct containment of the known variable-cp inconsistency; not a physical repair |
| Non-exclusive side/bottom/free weights | Correct direction for corner flux contributions |
| Explicit area normalization | Legitimate geometric correction in principle; not equivalent to validation of local surface flux |
| Shared smoke/validation gates | Better structure, but acceptance logic still contains false-pass paths |
| Actual coil-only dump | Fixes one-way versus coil-only labeling; sigma=16 result cannot automatically transfer to production sigma(T) |
| Analytic alpha changed to (1-i)/delta | Corrects the previously identified phasor reference sign; preserve this fix |
| Additional FEM domain sizes | Useful truncation sensitivity, but not a rigorous residual-error bound |
| Separate 1-rev/5-rev and sensitivity archives | Good evidence organization; temperature field remains transient |
| Freeze-T skips boundary and shell thermal updates | Repairs an important part of the earlier diagnostic; the complete natural-force experiment remains unvalidated |

## 4. Confirmed defects and acceptance gaps

### F01 — Small thermal increments are discarded by diffusion and inactive clamping [P0]

Files/functions:

- `code/shared/electromagnetic_ophelie/diagnostics/electromagnetic_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_one_way.h`: `ApplyLaplaceDiffusionDtCK::UpdateKernel::update`.
- `code/cases/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em.cpp`: `ClampTemperatureCK::UpdateKernel::update` and the acoustic-step thermal sequence.

The diffusion update still performs:

```cpp
temperature[i] += dt * diff_factor * laplace[i];
delta_t[i] = temperature[i] - t_initial;
```

The clamp always copies `temperature - t_initial` back to delta_t, even when the temperature lies inside the permitted interval. Thus both paths can overwrite a more accurately accumulated small delta with the rounded absolute temperature. This defeats the protection intended by storing a separate ThermalDeltaT field. Diffusion itself also adds small increments directly to the large absolute temperature.

Minimal reproduction, float32, no cooling and zero Laplacian:

```text
T0 = 1473 K; rho = 2800; cp = 1150
Q = 50000 W/m^3; dt = 0.00038 s; steps = 10000
float32 spacing at 1473 K = 0.0001220703125 K
per-step heating = 0.00000590062109 K
expected accumulated rise = 0.05900621 K
production-order accumulated rise = 0 K
```

This reproduces the arithmetic defect, not the full production-field error. It does not mean all production heating disappears: larger local increments survive or round differently. The lost/biased amount depends on local Q, diffusion, boundary flux and dt.

Required repair:

- Choose a single authoritative thermal state: a sufficiently accurate temperature increment or specific enthalpy.
- Apply Q, diffusion and boundary changes to that state; derive absolute T for material laws/output.
- An inactive clamp must preserve the authoritative state. When a clamp acts, update it consistently and accumulate the associated energy intervention.
- Audit both cases and all thermal helper paths for absolute-T round trips.
- Use the same authoritative thermal state in energy diagnostics where appropriate.

Acceptance: run the actual production operator sequence at T0=1473 K with per-step increments below and above one ULP; compare against a double scalar reference and repeat with dt halved. Test Q-only, cooling-only, nonzero diffusion and combined operation. A reduction in dt must not suppress a real thermal response. It is not necessary to convert all hydrodynamics to double to repair the thermal-state representation.

### F02 — Boundary energy is integrated from stale log-time power [P0]

Both production cases call `ledger.accumulateBoundary(ledger.p_loss_live, dt)` on acoustic steps, but `noteLiveLoss(...)` is primarily updated in the screen/CSV output block. It does not record the flux actually used by every boundary update.

Consequences:

- Changing screen/CSV intervals changes the estimated cumulative boundary energy.
- Temperature evolution and boundary retagging can change flux while the ledger continues using old power.
- Boundary flux is applied after other thermal substeps, whereas the recorded power may refer to a different state.
- With output disabled, the initial power can remain the ledger's integration value.

Doc 42 explicitly lists per-step accumulation as optional. It is required for the claimed actual-applied-energy identity.

Required repair: accumulate boundary energy from the actual operator at the same state, weights and dt used for the update. Per-particle increments can be reduced in batches; avoid unnecessary full host synchronization at every acoustic step. Integrate source, boundary, clamp/imposed-T and relevant contact terms independently of reporting cadence. Carry invalid/nonfinite events as sticky failures instead of silently ignoring them in `accumulateQ`/`accumulateBoundary`.

Acceptance: the same run with different screen/CSV intervals produces the same physical solution and cumulative ledger to reduction precision. Perform a per-step reference comparison on a small run. For a melt-only Level-2 ledger, count contact loss at the melt; for a melt+shell ledger, include shell storage/external loss and cancel internal contact exchanges.

### F03 — The shared acceptance gate regressed in schedule and transfer checks [P0]

File: `code/shared/electromagnetic_ophelie/electromagnetic_ophelie_thermo_em_coupling.h`, `evaluateFrenchCouplingRunGates`.

Two counterexamples were compiled against the actual extracted gate implementation:

```text
Requested end_time=30 s; interval=0.5 s; expected refreshes=60
Actual periodic_refresh_count=3, update_count=4
Other inputs valid, residual=0
Result: validation_passed=1

Same horizon; periodic_refresh_count=59
P_em=60000 W; P_heat_fluid=0 W; fluid_power_recorded=true
Other inputs valid, residual=0
Result: validation_passed=1
```

The new code requires a minimum of three refreshes but no longer checks completion of the expected schedule. It also removed the prior relative fluid/EM transfer check without replacing it with an explicit transfer/domain acceptance criterion. An internally balanced zero-source fluid can pass despite nonzero EM power.

The supplied 5-rev archive actually has 59 refreshes, so the first counterexample is a gate defect, not evidence that this run stopped refreshing after three updates.

Additional gaps:

- Only the latest phi residual is checked; earlier failures can be overwritten.
- `q_sample_rel_l2` remains a startup measure or is bypassed.
- Natural passes out-of-grid inputs as hard-coded zero and q L2 as -1: unmeasured is treated as valid.
- Invalid events can be discarded by the ledger without a failed-run flag.
- Completion tolerance is 1% of the requested horizon; define an endpoint tolerance tied to stepping rather than silently tolerating an arbitrarily long missed tail.

Required repair: test minimum exercise AND expected scheduling with an explicit endpoint policy; retain update timestamps/max gaps and sticky solver status. Validate transfer using a documented common-domain/source mapping criterion, accounting separately for legitimate cavity/domain exclusions. A zero delivered source with nonzero expected deposition must fail. Distinguish passed/failed/not-exercised. For natural, measure out-of-grid state or mark mapping validation unexercised.

### F04 — GetDP Table parsers skip the wrong number of columns [P0 for phase evidence]

Files:

- `code/reference/em_rz_getdp/audit_coil_axis_b.py`, `parse_getdp_b_axis_table`.
- `code/reference/em_rz_getdp/export_reference_csv.py`, `parse_getdp_table_point_b`.

Both start reading field components immediately after xyz. Official GetDP `Format Table` includes THREE additional context-dependent real columns before the values. Source: [GetDP manual, Types for PostOperation](https://getdp.info/doc/texinfo/getdp.html#Types-for-PostOperation), Table/SimpleTable definitions.

The archived `fine_large` raw row has 14 numbers:

```text
15 415 | 0.0001 0.105 0 | 0 0 0 | 0 -1.144987081284425e-5 0 | 0 2.437884017992692e-19 0
type/id| coordinates   | extra | real vector               | imaginary vector
```

Current JSON reports Bz_real=0 and Bz_imag=-1.144987e-5. Correct column positions give Bz_real=-1.144987e-5 and Bz_imag≈2.44e-19, under the row's real/imaginary vector layout.

The magnitude happens to stay unchanged in this case. Therefore this bug corrupts the phase evidence but does NOT explain away the approximately 20% magnitude deficit. Do not generalize this Table bug to the separate `.pos` parser without checking that format.

Required repair: use an explicit validated schema, preferably SimpleTable or separately named scalar component exports. Remove heuristics that swap Br/Bz or substitute magnitudes merely because one seems closer to an expected field. Add fixtures with six distinct nonzero components, the archived row, and a nearly real coil-only field. Share one parser rather than maintaining two copies.

### F05 — Existing thermal tests do not verify production diffusion [P0 validation gap]

The insulated MMS uses uniform initial T and uniform Q. Its Laplacian is zero in the intended state. It exercises heating and an insulated identity, but cannot verify a nonzero conduction rate, k scaling or diffusion's rounding behavior.

The helper uses the combined thermal-step path. Production stirring uses separate Q, diffusion, BC and clamp kernels. Therefore a helper's pass does not validate the complete production sequence implicated in F01.

The Dirichlet test requires heating, wall compliance and energy loss. Directly resetting wall temperatures can satisfy those conditions even if diffusion contributes incorrectly. Calling the test MMS does not provide a manufactured solution.

Required test: a nonuniform initial-temperature diffusion decay or manufactured solution with known rate, k=1 versus k=4, an insulated conservation check, and the actual production sequence at 1473 K. Prefer a small test over another long production run. Add one genuinely evolving nonuniform field; do not duplicate more uniform-heating identity tests.

### F06 — The reported 1.7% energy error is significant for net cooling [P0 interpretation]

Recomputed from the 5-rev final record:

| Quantity | Value |
|---|---:|
| Integrated fluid source | 1,763,445.046 J |
| Integrated boundary loss | 2,004,901.766 J |
| Predicted net energy change | -241,456.720 J |
| Residual | -34,765.188 J |
| Residual / boundary throughput | 1.734% |
| Residual / magnitude of predicted net change | **14.398%** |
| Average residual power over 30 s | **-1,158.83 W** |

The 2% throughput gate is not mathematically wrong; it was a provisional criterion. It is insufficient by itself to justify the cooling trajectory. Do not silently change the historical gate: report both throughput error and net-storage/temperature impact, with a floor when net exchange is near zero. Investigate the residual using F01/F02 and mass/volume consistency; this review has not apportioned its cause.

Also, the 5-rev energy CSV contains an early cumulative residual of 2.887%, although the endpoint passes. An instantaneous startup exceedance is not automatically a fatal error, but endpoint-only acceptance must not be described as a checked residual history. Define any startup exclusion explicitly and add window-local balances to prevent cumulative cancellation from hiding later errors.

### F07 — Sensible-energy reference improves conditioning but does not establish mass consistency [P1]

`hostThermalEnergyConstantCp` accumulates `rho0*cp*(T-Tref)*VolumetricMeasure` in double, then returns Real. The energy path still uses rho0 times current volume rather than demonstrably conserved particle mass. The documentation itself mentions WCSPH volume noise.

If sum(V) changes, changing Tref changes the measured energy difference by a term proportional to that volume change. Subtracting T0 can hide a large baseline contribution without repairing the underlying mass convention. The absent framework prevents concluding here whether VolumetricMeasure is constant or updated along this path.

Required evidence: report sum(Mass), sum(VolumetricMeasure), sum(rho0*V) and the relevant rho definition over motion, density updates and sorting. Reconcile source energy with the mass-based thermal state. If using H=sum(m*h), use the corresponding source conversion rather than changing only the diagnostic. Retain double accumulation and appropriate precision through the ledger API.

## 5. EM validation remains the main scientific bottleneck

The new full-domain metrics are useful precisely because they expose an unresolved discrepancy. For the supplied coil-only sigma=16 comparison:

```text
Full masked relative L2(Q)       = 0.90999
Full masked relative L2(Qshape)  = 0.86347
Full masked correlation(Q)      = -0.08317
Midplane relative L2(Q)         = 0.38275
Midplane shape relative L2      = 0.19892
Midplane correlation(Q)         = 0.90290
```

These do not qualify full Q(r,z) for a temperature-distribution claim. They do not yet prove the SPH solver is physically wrong either: export, coordinate conventions, source geometry, FEM formulation and unlike spatial filters remain possible contributors.

The comparison still samples an SPH disk average against a FEM nearest-centroid point value. New L2 reporting does not fix the mismatch in spatial observation operators. Use common cell averages/controlled quadrature and retain complex components for phase checks. Shape normalization should use a stated physical weighting, not an unexplained unweighted mean. Inspect radial AND axial profiles and end regions; volume integrals can cancel spatial errors.

### A second source-field discrepancy deserves attention

The coil audit says the SPH near-axis deficit can be attributed to the lattice probe not lying on the axis. At the exact closest point in the JSON, this explanation is insufficient:

```text
r = 0.00707101868 m; z = 0.110000007 m
Declared source: R=0.285 m, seven cell-centered loops, z stack [-0.01,0.22], I=1 A
Independent unsmoothed loop quadrature Bz = 1.43288690198e-5 T
Archived SPH Bz_coil at that point        = 1.25297011e-5 T
Relative difference                     = -12.556%
```

This is a discrepancy between the declared source and the archived probe, not proof of a particular Biot–Savart implementation bug. Check actual runtime loop centers/current/segment count/softening, field identity and dump identity. A changed runtime source or field would itself invalidate the present matched-source comparison.

### Domain study: do not convert sensitivity into an error bound

The additional radial/axial cases are useful. A combined enlargement changes P by about 1.97%. This supports the statement “observed change under this enlargement is 1.97%.” It does not prove the remaining outer-domain error is at most 2%. Preserve the larger-domain result as evidence; use successive convergence or a justified open-boundary reference if a strict absolute-power bound is needed. No further expensive domain sweep is required before fixing parsing and verifying the source.

### Minimal EM isolation sequence

1. Correct Table parsing and verify raw export component conventions.
2. Run a nonconducting source-only comparison: identical exact observation points, declared loop geometry/current, analytic loop quadrature, SPH Biot–Savart, FEM source-only. Export A and B with units and phasor sign.
3. Compare exact geometry/field parameters at runtime, not only command-line arguments. Preserve raw point rows.
4. Once source-only is consistent, compare prescribed-field/coil-only induced J and Q under a weak-feedback setting.
5. Reintroduce conductor reaction, use matched spatial averages and inspect Q(r,z) at several radii/heights.
6. Only after this works at sigma=16, repeat at the production sigma≈24.75 and relevant sigma(T) range. Do not switch sigma to make a discrepant reference agree.

If the discrepancy is an export problem, fix the export and regenerate evidence. If it is a discretization/source problem, fix that component. If it is genuine induction/model error, quantify its thermal effect using a reduced carrier. Do not redesign the full production solver before these cheaper isolating tests.

## 6. Boundary area and skull sensitivity: distinguish different questions

The explicit area normalization can be retained. It enforces the chosen cylinder face area by construction. It does not prove that local surface placement, transient retagging or heat-flux distribution is accurate.

The nonuniform test uses T=T0+80z+40r while wall temperature difference is about 1173 K. The varying part is only tens of kelvin, so the large uniform component dominates total flux. A small total relative error does not strongly test the spatial quadrature of the nonuniform part. Add a test of the perturbation flux itself, surface moments or stronger controlled gradients, including nonlinear free-surface heat loss. Check weights between retags, curved/moving surface assumptions, actual wetted geometry, and sorting. A face with no supporting particles should fail rather than silently keep scale=1.

The flux kernel discards particles if sum(weights)<0.5. Now weights are continuous normalized areas rather than binary tags, that cutoff is not generally valid; use a physically appropriate zero/positive-weight test. No evidence here establishes that production weights currently cross that threshold, so treat it as a robustness defect, not the established cause of the reported residual.

### Answer to the 5 mm versus 10 mm question

Changing skull AND crust thickness simultaneously is a physical-model sensitivity, not a mesh/time-step convergence test. A 34% heat-loss change is not, by itself, evidence of a numerical failure. Nor does it validate 5 mm as the correct literature value.

- Keep 5 mm as the operational baseline if clearly labeled provisional.
- Use the result to identify boundary-parameter uncertainty as important.
- Resolve journal/thesis provenance or use a justified uncertainty range.
- Separate skull-only and crust-only perturbations if attribution is needed; the existing combined run cannot identify which dominates.
- Conditional baseline runs can be meaningful once solver/source checks pass. A tight paper-level prediction cannot be claimed while the important boundary parameters remain unconstrained.

Doc 40's numerical-sensitivity stop condition should refer to dt, dp, EM carrier resolution and solver/update tolerances. Skull thickness is a different category. In this package, long literature campaigns remain blocked by F01–F05 and the spatial EM mismatch regardless of the 34% physical sensitivity.

## 7. Natural-convection status

Keep natural production refinement deferred. Credit the new skip of boundary and shell updates during freeze mode, but the prescribed profile remains a once-assigned particle temperature, not automatically a fixed Eulerian T(r,z). Define which diagnostic is intended.

Additional code issues:

- Ledger H0 is set before hydro settling and before the prescribed temperature is applied. A nonzero imposed temperature profile changes H without being counted as source; reset the diagnostic origin after initialization or account for imposed energy explicitly.
- Out-of-grid gate inputs are hard-coded to zero instead of measured.
- The zero-Q/freeze flags need a consistent contract: the print says Q is disabled whenever freeze is set, whereas zero_q is forced in parsing only when a positive prescribed hot-bottom delta is supplied. Reject unsupported flag combinations or implement the stated behavior.
- The total-force accumulation/consumption audit requested by Doc 40 has not been supplied in this delta.

A later small prescribed spatial-temperature force test with a nonzero rotational buoyancy component is appropriate. It should not displace current thermal and EM-source repairs.

## 8. Revised claim level

| Claim | Current disposition |
|---|---|
| Reproducible 1-/5-rev development trajectories under stated flags | Supported by supplied logs/CSV |
| Improved energy bookkeeping exists | Supported |
| Actual per-step source/boundary energy identity closed | Not yet; F01/F02/F07 |
| `validation_passed` proves schedule, transfer and thermal correctness | No; counterexamples F03 and tests F05 |
| Area normalization enforces chosen global areas | Yes, by construction |
| Full nonuniform/time-dependent surface flux validated | Not yet |
| Coil-only is distinct from one-way in the new comparison | Yes |
| Full EM Q shape independently validated | No; full-domain error remains large |
| Coil-only error is universally a few percent | No |
| Analytic reference phasor sign corrected | Yes in reviewed code; preserve |
| Stationary thermal uniformity or paper contour reproduction | Not claimed and not established |

## 9. Bounded next development plan

### Batch A — Repair the production thermal state and ledger

Scope: F01, F02, F07; retain constant cp/k/mu validation baseline.

Deliver:

1. One authoritative thermal-state update contract and a documented mass/source convention.
2. Production-order high-T small-increment test, nonuniform diffusion test, active/inactive clamp test.
3. Actual source/boundary/intervention energy accumulation independent of output cadence.
4. A short coupled run with per-operator and window-local energy balances.

Acceptance: dt refinement preserves physical heating/diffusion; energy discrepancy is attributable and within a predeclared tolerance; reporting cadence does not affect ledger physics. Do not simply relax the 2% threshold.

### Batch B — Fix acceptance and parser failures

Scope: F03 and F04.

Deliver actual failure tests for missing refresh schedule, zero/invalid transfer, intermediate solver failure, missing measurements, nonfinite integration inputs and incomplete requested time. Add GetDP fixtures with real/imaginary component identity checked. These are necessary correctness tests, not tests that merely repeat implementation.

A source-free or insulated physics test should have its own appropriate acceptance contract; do not force every diagnostic to pass a production periodic-heating gate.

### Batch C — Resolve the reduced EM spatial discrepancy

Use the source-only isolation sequence in Section 5. This is the primary scientific next step, ahead of conductivity-table alignment as a numerical experiment or a natural-flow campaign. Write a short root-cause report with raw point/profile data and one consistent coordinate/frame definition.

Do not label the unexplained 20% source-field discrepancy and approximately 0.9 full-Q L2 as harmless uncertainty. They may originate in reference or comparison code, but they must be localized before using temperature agreement as solver evidence.

### Batch D — Return to the stirring temperature family

After A–C pass, rerun a short matched baseline, then repeat the 1-/5-rev observation only as needed to assess the corrected trajectory. Keep old results as historical evidence.

Perform a genuine bounded dt sensitivity and practical spatial-resolution/carrier check. The previous EM-interval test is useful but cannot replace them. Inspect local energy balance, Tmin/Tmax, T_std and temperature profiles as well as mean T.

Freeze paper parameter provenance, then align sigma(T) and skull/crust assumptions. Digitize a specific temperature figure only when its geometry, time/state and normalization are identified. Paper definition/provenance extraction can begin now without running a new case.

Natural quantitative circulation, full local viscosity, dynamic skull, segmented metal and full-fluid Picard remain deferred unless the corrected baseline reveals a concrete requirement.

## 10. Required evidence in the next package

- Commit and dirty diff, build precision, execution device and exact command manifest; a fixed contract filename alone is insufficient run identity.
- Runtime coil coordinates/current/softening and exact probe coordinates.
- Raw GetDP point rows and a small raw SPH/FEM spatial sample sufficient to regenerate comparison metrics.
- Thermal test initial state, per-operator increments and independent scalar/analytic reference.
- Conservation quantities using documented mass/volume conventions.
- Gate negative-test output, refresh times and sticky failure history.
- Raw CSV per run and scripts that regenerate all reported metrics; do not overwrite or manually patch summary-only results.

The next acceptance report should separate “implemented,” “test exercised,” “test passed,” and “physical claim supported.” This prevents another cycle in which improved instrumentation is mistaken for completed validation.

# File: `45b_DISAGREEMENTS_WITH_DOC45_FOR_NEXT_GPT_2026-09-22.md`

# Disagreements with Doc 45 (for next ChatGPT round)

Date: 2026-09-22  
Context: Cursor began Doc 45 Batches A–B (F01–F04) after the owner's go-ahead.  
Purpose: record where Cursor agrees, where it partially agrees, and where it still differs so the next GPT pass can adjudicate without replaying the whole history.

**Disposition (2026-09-22):** Soft points below were adjudicated in [`47_BATCH_AB_REVIEW_AND_BATCH_C_ENTRY_PLAN_2026-09-22.md`](47_BATCH_AB_REVIEW_AND_BATCH_C_ENTRY_PLAN_2026-09-22.md) §9. Historical text retained; do not treat open questions as still unanswered.

This is **not** a rejection of Doc 45. The implementation work treats F01–F04 as real P0 defects.

---

## 1. Agreed (Cursor accepts Doc 45)

| Item | Position |
|---|---|
| Do **not** claim a numerically validated stirring thermal baseline yet | Agree. Doc 44's “Stage-3 slice closed” meant “bounded evidence archived under frozen flags,” not “energy/gate/EM identity proven.” Wording in Doc 44 over-claimed relative to Doc 45 §8. |
| F01 float32 absolute-T round-trip in production Q/diffusion/clamp | Agree after code read. Helper combined-step already used ΔT-first; production stirring/natural kernels did not. |
| F02 boundary ledger tied to screen/CSV cadence | Agree. `accumulateBoundary(p_loss_live)` used stale `noteLiveLoss`. |
| F03 min-3 refresh without expected schedule; zero fluid / nonzero EM can pass | Agree. Counterexamples match the shipped gate logic. |
| F04 GetDP `Format Table` skips three context columns after xyz | Agree vs [GetDP manual](https://getdp.info/doc/texinfo/getdp.html#Types-for-PostOperation). Magnitude accident; phase corrupted. |
| F05 MMS does not exercise production diffusion | Agree; deferred to a dedicated nonuniform diffusion test after F01. |
| Full-domain Q L2 / correlation do not qualify T-distribution claims | Agree. Midplane corr≈0.90 is not a full-field pass. |
| Keep `--aind=off`; do not retune skull δ or js0 | Agree. |
| Batch order A → B → C → D | Agree; this session starts A+B. |

---

## 2. Partial agreement (nuance Cursor wants preserved)

### 2.1 Skull δ 5→10 mm and Doc 40 §7 item 5

**Doc 45:** 34% loss change is physical-model sensitivity, not mesh/dt numerical failure; §7.5 should mean dt/dp/carrier/solver tolerances. Long campaigns blocked by F01–F05 and EM mismatch anyway.

**Cursor:** Agree that δ is not a numerical-convergence lever and that 5 mm stays the operational freeze.  
**Still want GPT to confirm:** whether Doc 40's original wording (“selected observable changes materially under bounded numerical sensitivity”) was meant to include **frozen physical parameters with large lever arms**. Cursor's previous answer to the owner treated the 34% lever as a stop on literature extension; Doc 45 reclassifies it. For the next pack, Cursor will label δ uncertainty as **model-parameter**, not as §7.5 numerical failure, unless GPT reverts.

### 2.2 The 1.7% energy number (F06)

**Doc 45:** Residual / \|net ΔE\| ≈ 14%; throughput 2% gate alone does not justify the cooling trajectory.

**Cursor:** Agree both ratios should be reported.  
**Disagreement on tone only:** Cursor does **not** treat “14% of net” as proof the 5-rev archive is physically wrong until F01/F02 are fixed and the residual is re-measured. It may shrink, grow, or move. Do not rewrite historical Doc 44 numbers; add dual reporting going forward.

### 2.3 Axis-B / source-field ~12–20% (Doc 45 §5)

**Doc 45:** Independent Biot–Savart at the archived probe is ~12.6% above SPH; lattice-off-axis explanation insufficient; may be runtime source identity, not necessarily Biot bug.

**Cursor:** Agree this is a **matched-source** discrepancy and Batch C priority.  
**Partial:** The previous ~20% vs filament and ~12% vs loop quadrature are different references; do not merge them into one “20% bug.” Keep filament, GetDP OnPoint (after F04 fix), and SPH probe as three numbers.

### 2.4 Domain-enlarge “≲2%”

**Doc 45:** Observed ΔP under that enlargement ≈1.97%; not a residual-error bound.

**Cursor:** Agree. Doc 43/44 language “≲2%” was too strong. Prefer “ΔP under radial+axial enlarge ≈2% at σ=16 working mesh.”

---

## 3. Soft disagreements / open questions for GPT

1. **Claim vocabulary.** Should Doc 44 be amended in-place with a Doc-45 disposition banner (like docs 32/35), or left historical with Doc 46 as the superseding claim table? Cursor prefers **banner + Doc 46**, no silent rewrite of archived numbers.

2. **F02 performance contract.** Strict per-acoustic-step host sync is correct but expensive. Cursor intends a **device reduce of the same flux formula** (or BC-local power field) so cadence independence does not require host sync every step. Please confirm that meets “actual operator at same state.”

3. **Gate schedule tolerance.** Exact `periodic_refresh_count == expected` races the last interval vs `end_time`. Cursor plans: require `count >= expected - 1` **and** `count >= min(3, expected)` **and** last refresh within one interval of end (or document exclusive endpoint). Please confirm preferred endpoint policy.

4. **Transfer gate threshold.** For nonzero EM, fail if `P_heat_fluid` not recorded or `P_heat_fluid <= 0`. Relative `|P_heat−P_em|/P_em` — keep as warning or hard fail? Cursor: hard fail if fluid power is zero/non-finite; relative mismatch soft until common-domain criterion exists (Batch C).

5. **F07 mass vs volume.** Cursor will add sum(Mass)/sum(V)/sum(ρ0 V) monitors in the next short run, but will **not** switch production H to mass-based enthalpy in Batch A without a dedicated convention doc. Is reporting-first acceptable?

6. **Weight cutoff 0.5.** Doc 45 flags it under continuous area weights. Cursor will change BC/power “inactive” test to `sum(weights) <= TinyReal` while keeping flux `∝ w_i`. Agree?

---

## 4. What this session implements regardless of the soft points

- F01: ΔT-authoritative updates in production Q, diffusion, clamp (stirring + natural); BC uses T from `T0+ΔT`.
- F02: accumulate boundary from operator-consistent power each acoustic step (device reduce preferred).
- F03: expected refresh schedule + zero-transfer fail + sticky ledger invalid flag; negative tests in-header.
- F04: shared GetDP Table parser (skip 3 context cols); fixture; prefer SimpleTable for new OnPoint dumps.
- Docs: this file + Doc 46 progress report. No 1000 s / Picard-on / skull retune / paper fitting.

---

## 5. One-paragraph paste for ChatGPT

```text
Cursor accepted Doc 45 F01–F04 as P0 and started Batches A–B. Soft disagreements to adjudicate: (1) Doc 44 claim wording — banner+Doc46 vs rewrite; (2) skull 34% is model-parameter not §7.5 numerical — confirm; (3) report both energy residual/throughput and residual/|net| without declaring 5-rev archive false until F01/F02 rerun; (4) F02 via device flux reduce vs host-per-step; (5) refresh schedule endpoint tolerance expected-1; (6) transfer hard-fail only on zero/nonfinite P_heat_fluid until Batch C domain criterion; (7) F07 report mass/volume first, delay mass-based H switch; (8) replace weight sum<0.5 skip with <=TinyReal under continuous area weights. Please answer only these adjudication points, then review the Batch A/B patch evidence in Doc 46.
```

<a id="47-batch-ab-review-and-batch-c-entry-plan-2026-09-22md"></a>

# File: `47_BATCH_AB_REVIEW_AND_BATCH_C_ENTRY_PLAN_2026-09-22.md`

# Doc 47 — Batch A/B review, disagreement disposition, and Batch C entry

Date: 2026-09-22  
Reviewed archive: `chatgpt_batch_ab_closeout_2026-09-22.tar(1).gz`  
Predecessors: Docs 40, 45, 45b, 46.  
Purpose: a concrete handoff to Cursor. Preserve historical results; amend claims explicitly.

## 1. Decision

**Proceed to Batch C source-only EM isolation. Accept the implemented thermal fixes as meaningful progress. Do not label all F01–F05 fully validated or close all Batch A/B evidence.**

The remaining thermal tests and mass convention work do not block a static source-only magnetic-field measurement. The parser issues in §4 must be repaired before treating the next GetDP comparison as evidence. Gate and thermal follow-ups must be completed before declaring a validated coupled/literature baseline.

This is a refinement of the stopping conditions, not a requirement to repeat every previous development stage. The key distinction is between permission to investigate a different subsystem and evidence supporting a final physical claim.

Answers to the archive's four questions:

1. **Batch A/B closeout:** accept the specific implementations and the tests actually exercised, with qualifications in §2. Do not accept a blanket “F01–F05 passed” closure.
2. **Disagreements:** device reduction before BC is acceptable; the existing cadence comparison does not vary the effective diagnostic cadence; global refresh slack=1 is insufficient as a schedule contract; the TinyReal weight cutoff change is appropriate for nonnegative continuous weights.
3. **Mass versus rho0·V:** investigate alongside Batch C and finish before thermal baseline validation. Do not blindly replace the energy formula. The approximately 2% gap alone is not proof of mass loss.
4. **First Batch C measurement:** compare signed complex coil-only B at one explicitly identical physical point, with actual runtime source settings and I=1 A, using SPH's actual coil routine, an independent Biot–Savart reference, and a correctly parsed GetDP OnPoint result. Detailed contract in §8.

## 2. What is accepted, and what is not yet established

All code paths below are relative to the extracted archive root.

| Item | Accepted evidence | Remaining qualification |
|---|---|---|
| F01 production small-increment repair | Constant-cp Q and diffusion update DeltaT first; inactive clamp preserves DeltaT; BC reads T0+DeltaT. Host float32 demonstration passes. Archived stirring GPU smoke exercises the changed path. | No isolated production-kernel sub-ULP accuracy test or dt/dt2 comparison in this pack. Energy diagnostics and diffusion gradients still read rounded absolute T. Active clamp energy is not separately accounted. |
| F02 boundary ledger | Device reduction immediately before BC evaluates the same weighted heat-loss law at the same pre-BC thermal state. The old stale screen-time power is no longer the integration input. | The purported cadence twin does not vary effective diagnostic cadence; §3. Device reduction is not evidence of zero host synchronization overhead. |
| F03 gates | Severe under-refresh and zero periodic fluid-power examples are now rejected. Sticky invalid-accumulation state reaches final validation. Existing in-header negative tests are real. | Missing timestamp policy, unmeasured transfer treated as acceptable, and other remaining false-pass cases; §5. |
| F04 GetDP | Archived single-point Table phase-column defect is corrected on its fixture; new SimpleTable fixture passes. | Parser remains heuristic for coordinates/row layout; upstream audit still substitutes components/observables. Re-export has not occurred; §4. |
| F05 diffusion | Nonuniform perturbation decays; changing k from 1 to 4 increases decay. This improves substantially on uniform-temperature testing. | This is a diffusion regression, not a manufactured-solution accuracy/convergence test. It uses the combined-step helper, not the separate production Q/diffusion/clamp sequence; §6. |
| F06 residual reporting | Both throughput and predicted-net-storage residual ratios are printed and stored. | Short-run improvement is not a corrected 30 s result. Maintain window/history evidence. |
| F07 mass/volume | Mass and volume monitors expose a real convention issue for further investigation. | The energy measure has not been reconciled with evolving particle volume and particle Mass; §7. |

Natural-convection source edits are not equivalent to a rebuilt/exercised natural-convection validation. Doc 46 explicitly leaves that rebuild optional; retain that status.

### Short-run results, interpreted correctly

| Quantity | screen=20 | screen=100 |
|---|---:|---:|
| Physical time [s] | 2.000070 | 2.000155 |
| Applied Joule energy [J] | 118449.461504 | 118454.425895 |
| Boundary-out energy [J] | 135422.024507 | 135417.867746 |
| Energy residual [J] | +122.855972 | +145.807083 |
| Residual / throughput | 0.0907% | 0.1077% |
| Residual / predicted net storage | 0.7239% | 0.8595% |
| Mass [kg] | 112.860296 | 112.860296 |
| rho0 × volume [kg] | 110.682592 | 110.610655 |
| Periodic refreshes excluding initialization | 3 | 3 |

These are encouraging 2 s smoke results. Do not compare them directly with the previous 30 s residual as a measured improvement factor: run lengths and transients differ. Do not alter the archived 30 s numbers.

## 3. New finding: the cadence twin does not change the effective cadence

**Evidence:** `code/cases/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em/test_3d_ophelie_french_stirring_em.cpp`:

- `LocalCli::screen_every = 20` and `LocalCli::csv_every = 20`.
- The shared diagnostic block executes under `if (write_csv || write_screen)`.
- The archived command descriptions change only screen-every to 100.
- Both archived energy CSVs contain 30 data rows.

With CSV still every 20 steps, changing screen output from every 20 to every 100 does not change when the shared energy/host-diagnostic block executes: every multiple of 100 is already a multiple of 20.

**Consequence:** the observed boundary-energy difference of approximately 3.07e-5 is a small difference between two runs, but it does not establish sensitivity to different effective diagnostic cadences. This control would also fail to distinguish the old stale-ledger defect if CSV kept refreshing its diagnostic power every 20 steps in both runs.

The initial EM RHS fingerprints already differ between the archived runs, before the time-loop screen-cadence decision. This supports caution about assuming identical numerical executions. It does not prove the later difference is caused by GPU nondeterminism or by chaotic flow. Different endpoint times are another confounder.

### Required correction, without blocking source-only Batch C

- Amend Doc 46 §3 and `batch_ab_cadence_compare.json`: call the result a two-run smoke comparison with unchanged effective diagnostic cadence.
- Do not rename `f02_cadence_ok=false` to true by simply loosening its threshold.
- For a real cadence test, vary both CSV and screen together, e.g. 20/20 versus 100/100, or disable CSV in both and vary screen. Record the effective diagnostic-block invocation count.
- Prefer one small frozen-particle test, with identical starting state and fixed timestep sequence, using the actual BC/reduction implementation. Accumulate a shadow per-step ledger and demonstrate that diagnostic reads cannot change it.
- Compare at identical step counts/time horizons. If the execution backend is nondeterministic, document repeatability or use deterministic replay for this narrow test; machine-epsilon identity between full turbulent GPU runs is not required.

The source-level F02 repair is accepted independently of this test weakness. It is valid to compute power immediately before an explicit BC step rather than infer it from a rounded absolute-temperature subtraction afterward.

Performance note: `const Real p_boundary_now = boundary_loss_power.exec()` is consumed by a host ledger each acoustic step. Although particle reduction is on-device, this does not establish that the implementation avoids host synchronization. Profile only if that synchronization becomes material; do not rewrite the implementation speculatively. A later on-device accumulator/fused BC diagnostic is acceptable if it preserves the same state and discrete increment contract.

## 4. F04 needs a small but important repair before Batch C measurement

### 4.1 Coordinate fallback can silently return an incorrect Table row

File: `code/reference/em_rz_getdp/parse_getdp_table.py`, function `parse_getdp_table_point_vector`.

After failing to locate the default expected point `(1e-4, 0.105, 0)`, the fallback effectively chooses token zero. For a Table row, tokens zero and one are the type/index, not coordinates. Even `format_hint="table"` does not enforce the coordinate offset.

Reproduced against the actual uploaded Python function:

```text
Input Table row:
[15, 415, .01, .11, 0, 10, 20, 30, 1, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6]

Expected:
xyz=(.01,.11,0), real=[1,2,3], imag=[4,5,6]

Actual with format_hint="table" and default expected_xyz:
xyz=(15,415,.01), real=[20,30,1], imag=[2,3,4]
```

The current archived single-point fixture still passes. This new finding concerns other points, absent/mismatched expected points, and generic reuse. If expected_xyz is an assertion, mismatch must fail; it must not trigger a fallback guess.

### 4.2 Whole-file token flattening loses row identity

`parse_getdp_table_point_b` tokenizes the entire file as one flat list. Two concatenated SimpleTable rows cause auto-detection to classify the data as Table and combine values from different rows.

Reproduced:

```text
row 1: [.0001,.105,0, 1,2,3,4,5,6]
row 2: [.0001,.105,0, 7,8,9,10,11,12]

Actual auto result after flattening:
format=Table, real=[4,5,6], imag=[.0001,.105,0]
```

A NaN component is also accepted by the current function. The high-level export wrapper catches all exceptions and returns None, which further weakens audit visibility if a required source measurement fails.

### 4.3 The caller still substitutes Br or a different spatial observable

File: `code/reference/em_rz_getdp/audit_coil_axis_b.py`, in `main`, following `getdp_bz = axis["Bz_abs_T"]`:

```python
if axis["Bz_abs_T"] < 0.2 * filament and axis["Br_abs_T"] > 0.5 * filament:
    getdp_bz = axis["Br_abs_T"]
elif axis["Bz_abs_T"] < 0.5 * pos["mean_Bz_abs_T"] and pos["mean_Bz_abs_T"] > 0:
    getdp_bz = pos["mean_Bz_abs_T"]
```

Therefore the shared parser's “Do NOT swap components by heuristic closeness” contract is not enforced end to end. A suspicious axial field can still be replaced with radial field or a near-axis spatial mean. This is a confirmed code path, not a claim that it was triggered in the archived fixture.

### Required patch

1. Parse one data row at a time. Explicitly support only known OnPoint schemas, with fixed offsets and validated counts.
2. For current complex three-component fixtures: Table is 2 header + 3 coordinate + 3 context + 6 field tokens; SimpleTable is 3 coordinate + 6 field tokens. Other layouts/time series must have their own explicit supported contract or fail.
3. Select format from known export metadata/CLI. If auto remains, allow only unambiguous exact layouts; do not infer coordinates by searching arbitrary field values.
4. Validate requested coordinates after structural parsing. Reject mismatches, nonfinite values, truncation and unexpected extra data. Reject or explicitly select multiple rows/solutions; never flatten them.
5. Remove the Br-to-Bz and centroid-mean fallbacks from the audit. Store different observables separately. Do not select the quantity closest to a reference.
6. Required audit fields must fail visibly if missing or malformed; optional export metadata may be absent only with explicit status/error.
7. Extend fixtures to include a non-default point, six distinct Table components with nonzero context columns, multiple rows, nonfinite data, and mismatched coordinates.

This is a bounded patch. Complete it and then run Batch C; it is not a reason to launch another large thermal campaign.

## 5. F03: accept the specific repairs, retain gate hardening as OPEN

I extracted the actual gate structures/functions from the uploaded header and compiled a standalone C++17 harness using float Real. No gate logic was reimplemented in the harness. Results:

| Input variation from a valid baseline | Actual validation_passed | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---|
| Baseline | 1 | Positive control |
| `out_of_grid_measured=false`, q_sample_rel_l2 left unmeasured | 1 | An unmeasured transfer condition can still become overall validation |
| end=30.25, interval=.5, count=59 | 1 | Slack accepts a missing non-endpoint refresh; 60 are due before 30.25 |
| end=30, physical_time=29.7 | 1 | The inherited 99% horizon rule accepts incomplete requested time |
| P_em=60000 W, P_heat_fluid=1 W | 1 | Positivity is not a transfer-accuracy gate |
| Nonperiodic mode, fluid heat=0 | 1 | Transfer-power condition is bypassed outside periodic mode |
| Sticky ledger-invalid=true | 0 | New invalid flag correctly rejects validation |

These are constructed gate inputs, not claims that the smoke runs suffered those failures.

### Refresh policy

A one-tick boundary allowance can be legitimate only when tied to a specified endpoint policy. It must not be a blanket credit for a missed interior refresh.

Suggested policy:

- Initialization at t=0 is separate.
- Required nominal refreshes are k*interval strictly before requested end time; the refresh exactly at the endpoint is optional because no subsequent integration may use it.
- Use an explicit numerical time tolerance, not 1% of the run duration.
- Record actual refresh times, success states, maximum field age, and maximum nominal-tick lateness. Allow delays bounded by the scheduling/advection interval as documented.
- Separate schedule satisfaction from minimum exercise count. A deliberately short run may satisfy its schedule while remaining insufficiently exercised for validation.

The current gate inputs have no last-refresh timestamp, so the freshness condition proposed in Doc 45b is not implemented. Moreover, the scheduler uses `last_update_time + interval`, allowing timing drift to accumulate. Retain a nominal next-due tick or document and test a maximum-age policy; do not conceal arbitrary missed intervals with slack.

For the actual 2 s smoke, the three refreshes near .5, 1.0, and 1.5 s are consistent with an exclusive endpoint policy. Do not reject these archived runs merely because the current expected counter prints four.

### Transfer and validation scope

- Positivity/nonfinite checks are appropriate hard failures now.
- Do not force total EM-domain and fluid-domain powers to match by artificial renormalization when supports differ.
- Until a common-domain transfer criterion exists, retain finite positive transfer as an engineering check and mark accuracy validation unmeasured/unqualified.
- `out_of_grid_measured=false` must not silently certify the missing criterion. In particular, the natural path's new flag currently suppresses the test rather than preventing a full validation claim.
- Add sticky per-refresh solver failure/history checks before long coupled validation. The final good residual must not erase a failed earlier solve.
- Negative tests should include missing measurement, missing interior refresh, stale field, invalid ledger after a prior valid sample, incomplete end time and invalid/unrecorded transfer, alongside the existing positive control.

Do not block a source-only B measurement on these coupled-run gates. Correct the qualification before using `validation_passed=1` as more than a development-stage marker.

## 6. F01/F05: remaining thermal verification is small and specific

### Accepted changes

The production stirring constant-cp source, diffusion update and inactive clamp now preserve DeltaT. The actual host demo rerun produced:

```text
expected_rise_K                  = 0.059006210904044565
buggy_production_order_rise_K    = 0.0
fixed_delta_authoritative_rise_K = 0.05900813266634941
f01_demo_pass                   = 1
```

Do not re-open the original absolute-T update defect as though these fixes were absent.

### What the new diffusion test establishes

In `test_3d_ophelie_thermal_diffusion_mms.cpp`, initial temperature is `T0 + 40*sin(0.15*i)`, where i is particle index. It is not a prescribed spatial analytic eigenfunction. The acceptance condition is essentially:

```text
var0 > 1; var_k1 < var0; var_k4 < var_k1; energy_rel_k1 < .05
```

The printed values show a sensible trend. They do not establish the correct decay rate, spatial consistency or convergence order. The test checks energy only for k=1, and the printed variance is an unnormalized sum of squared deviations.

The execution helper uses `ApplyOphelieJouleHeatDiffusionCombinedStepCK`. Production stirring uses separate source update, `ApplyLaplaceDiffusionDtCK`, BC and clamp. Thus this is a useful shared-Laplace/combined-helper regression, not direct verification of the newly changed production update sequence.

### Follow-up before thermal baseline qualification

1. One frozen high-temperature test using the actual separate production operators. Cover Q-only, nonuniform diffusion, BC and an inactive clamp with sub-ULP increments; compare dt and dt/2 against the expected evolution.
2. A small nonuniform spatial analytic/MMS diffusion problem, with a compatible boundary condition and error measured on at least two discretizations. Keep the current index-sine regression but name its scope accurately.
3. Test energy conservation for both k branches, using a documented weighted thermal measure. Do not count a bare algebraic k-ratio test as numerical diffusion verification.
4. Track active-clamp particle count and energy correction, or make any activation disqualify an unclamped physical energy claim. Current smoke extrema do not suggest active clamp, but no count is archived.
5. Derive authoritative-state energy diagnostics from DeltaT directly. The current `hostThermalEnergyConstantCp` still subtracts T_ref from rounded absolute Temperature. Production Laplace also reads Temperature, so very small temperature differences can be lost even though increments are now retained. Evaluate that resolution issue in the targeted test; it is distinct from the repaired state round-trip bug.

No blanket switch of all hydrodynamic calculations to double precision is requested.

## 7. F07: mass/volume is an energy-convention issue, not evidence of lost mass

On the two final summaries, `(sum_mass - sum_rho0_vol)/sum_mass` is approximately 1.93% and 1.99%. Mass remains constant to the archived precision.

The time-series CSVs also show evolving volume: for example, se20 ranges from approximately 0.0393389 to 0.039747 m³ over the recorded samples, a range of about 1% of the volume. The source comments already acknowledge hydrostatic compression/packing concerns around c0=8. This is relevant context, not a proven explanation of the exact residual.

**Do not infer mass loss from Mass != rho0·V.** If the framework uses V=m/rho and rho differs from rho0, this inequality may be expected. The framework implementation needed to confirm that convention is omitted from this pack.

However, the reported energy is currently

```text
H_V = sum_i rho0 * cp * V_i * DeltaT_i
```

while constant-cp heating uses `dDeltaT/dt = Q/(rho0*cp)` and sampled power is an integral of Q*V. When V evolves, the discrete change in H_V also contains a volume-change contribution. A small overall residual does not independently prove that this represents the desired material enthalpy.

### Required investigation alongside C, completed before D validation

- Inspect actual framework definitions/updates of Mass, Density and VolumetricMeasure.
- Record sum(Mass), sum(rho*V), sum(rho0*V), density extrema and relevant weighted density statistics from the same state.
- Check particlewise `Mass - rho*V`, not just global sums.
- Measure energy changes around hydro/density regularization, Q, diffusion, BC and clamp in one short diagnostic run. Distinguish remapping/volume-accounting changes from thermal increments.
- Verify whether the cached `last_power.p_heat_fluid` integrated each acoustic step uses the volume measure at the actual heating update. It is sampled at advection events; this is a remaining audit question, not a confirmed new error without the framework volume-update contract.
- Define the physical storage convention before changing production equations. If using mass-based enthalpy, make source and BC increments consistent, e.g. Q_i*V_i/(m_i*cp), and ensure diffusion uses the same conserved measure. Do not change only the diagnostic H.

Reporting-first was a reasonable Batch A choice. Reporting alone is not enough for final thermal-energy validation. This does not obstruct a fixed-geometry coil-field measurement.

## 8. One Batch C first measurement

**Measure signed complex coil-only B at physical point `(x,y,z)=(0.0001,0,0.105) m` in the SPH z-up frame.** In the current axisymmetric GetDP plane this corresponds to `(r,z,third)=(0.0001,0.105,0)`. Confirm the transform explicitly from the models.

This point uses the current OnPoint location and avoids conflating an exact probe with the nearest particle or a spatial average.

### Freeze and archive

- No conducting-current feedback; extract the coil-only field.
- I=1 A per loop under the explicitly stated peak/RMS and complex phase convention. No target-power calibration.
- Dump actual runtime coil radius, all seven center heights, winding/current sign, coil current, segment count, softening, coordinate transform and source-code/build identity. Do not rely only on hard-coded audit constants.
- The expected declared geometry is R=.285 m, seven cell-centered turns across z=[-.01,.22]. Verify that runtime values really match it.
- GetDP finite 5 mm square source and SPH filament source are different source descriptions. Retain an independent finite-section reference for GetDP and a filament reference for SPH; report their difference explicitly. Do not silently treat them as identical.

### Produce one small comparison artifact

At this identical point, report real and imaginary radial/axial components from:

1. Independent high-resolution Biot–Savart integration using the dumped source parameters.
2. SPH's actual production coil-field evaluator called directly at the point, before interpolation/particle averaging or conducting reconstruction.
3. GetDP source-only OnPoint, parsed after §4 repairs, with the raw row and output-format metadata attached.

Also include the finite-section reference where needed to distinguish physical source representation from solver error. Preserve signed components; any coordinate/winding sign transform must be declared, never selected because it improves agreement. At near-zero components use absolute error scales rather than unstable relative errors.

### Interpret the result

- If SPH direct evaluation disagrees with its own independently integrated declared source: investigate runtime geometry/current, quadrature, softening and exported-field identity first.
- If SPH direct evaluation agrees but archived particle probes do not: investigate probe position, interpolation, stale/wrong field or archive/build identity.
- If GetDP alone disagrees with its finite-section reference: inspect source density/integrated ampere-turns, boundary/domain effects, coordinates and extraction before changing thermal parameters.
- If source-only fields agree but conductor J/Q remain different: proceed to weak-feedback/conducting-field formulation and common-support reconstruction comparisons.

A single point is diagnostic, not full spatial validation. Once its discrepancy is explained, expand to a small radial/axial probe set and then domain-weighted J/Q comparisons. Do not jump directly from one point to a literature temperature-contour pass.

The earlier approximately 12.6% SPH discrepancy and approximately 20% GetDP discrepancy used different probes/references. Preserve them as separate historical observations; do not combine them into one universal “20% solver bug.” Likewise, the approximately 2% power change under one domain enlargement is an observed sensitivity, not an infinite-domain error bound.

## 9. Adjudication of Doc 45b

| Question | Decision |
|---|---|
| Amend historical Doc 44? | Banner plus explicit superseding claim table. Preserve numerical results and original run identity; no silent rewrite. |
| Skull/crust delta sensitivity of ~34% | Physical model-parameter sensitivity, not dt/dp convergence failure. Keep 5 mm operational freeze; resolve provenance/sensitivity before final literature claims. |
| Old 1.7% throughput versus ~14% net residual | Report both. The latter signals a material storage-accuracy uncertainty; it does not by itself prove every archived field physically wrong. Reassess after a matched repaired run. |
| Device reduction immediately before BC | Accept for operator-consistent power under the current explicit constant-cp BC. No mandatory host scan of all particles per step. Do not claim asynchronous performance without evidence. |
| Is 3e-5 cadence difference acceptable? | No universal 1e-6 full-GPU identity requirement. But this particular test did not change effective diagnostic cadence, so the causal conclusion must be withdrawn and the narrow test corrected. |
| Refresh slack=1 | Accept endpoint exclusion as policy; reject blanket slack as proof of schedule compliance. Add timing/freshness checks. |
| Positive transfer, relative mismatch soft? | Accept as temporary engineering check. Unmeasured common-domain accuracy cannot support full validation. No forced power matching. |
| Mass/volume reporting first | Accept for Batch A; investigate alongside source-only Batch C and resolve before thermal baseline qualification. |
| Weight cutoff <=TinyReal | Accept for nonnegative continuous weights. Validate weights are finite/nonnegative and nonzero target faces have support; cancellation of invalid weights must not count as inactivity. |

## 10. Execution order and stopping rules

### Next bounded change set

1. Correct the cadence claim and narrow the F05 label in Docs 46/45b.
2. Repair strict point parsing and remove audit substitutions; run focused fixtures.
3. Execute the one source-only measurement in §8. This is the next scientific priority.
4. Alongside that work, harden gate schedule/measurement semantics and perform the small frozen thermal/ledger tests. Investigate mass/volume conventions without changing them blindly.
5. After those contracts are adequate, rerun the corrected 1-revolution case. Extend to 5 revolutions only when it resolves an identified remaining transient/energy question. Compare like-for-like conditions.

The main scientific bottleneck remains the EM source/reference and full-domain heat-source shape comparison. Thermal bookkeeping has improved enough that it should no longer prevent static EM diagnosis. The remaining thermodynamic convention and verification questions determine when coupled temperature predictions can be trusted.

Continue to defer 1000 s campaigns, production full-N² Picard, skull retuning to fit curves, and expensive natural-convection refinement. Natural force-consumption audit, local variable-property consistency, dynamic skull/metal detail and literature provenance remain separate later workstreams, not requirements for the first Batch C probe.

### Next review pack

Include only the evidence needed to resolve these questions:

- Commit/build identity, dirty patch, scalar precision/device, complete commands and hashes/identities of reused inputs.
- Strict parser tests, raw new OnPoint row, explicit coordinate/phase mapping, runtime coil dump, and one-point comparison CSV/JSON.
- Corrected claim table identifying implemented / exercised / passed test / qualified physical claim separately.
- If performed: a genuine cadence test with invocation counts and equal timestep horizon; targeted production thermal tests; gate negative-test output and actual refresh timestamps; mass/rho/volume convention note.
- No large binary/mesh archives unless needed to reproduce a discrepancy; supply the minimal reference inputs needed to regenerate the probe.

## 11. Review scope and reproducibility

This review read the new README, Docs 45b/46, relevant prior dispositions, actual changed source and diffs, and smoke/MMS logs and CSVs. It reran the uploaded F01 demonstration and F04 self-test, exercised parser counterexamples directly, compiled and ran the extracted current gate logic, and checked the smoke ledger/volume/refresh records.

The full SPHinXsys framework, reloads, executables and GetDP meshes are omitted. Therefore this review does not claim to have rebuilt or rerun the archived GPU cases or regenerated the FEM solution. GPU results above are archived evidence; source-level and small standalone reproductions are independently checked. No production solver files were modified for this review.

# File: `49_BATCH_C_GETDP_B_DIAGNOSIS_2026-09-22.md`

# Doc 49 — Batch C GetDP coil-only B diagnosis (after Doc 48)

Date: 2026-09-22  
Parents: [`47_...md`](47_BATCH_AB_REVIEW_AND_BATCH_C_ENTRY_PLAN_2026-09-22.md), [`48_BATCH_C_ONE_POINT_COIL_B_2026-09-22.md`](48_BATCH_C_ONE_POINT_COIL_B_2026-09-22.md)  
Artifacts: `docs/ophelie/reference/em_rz_getdp/work/batch_c_*.json`

## 1. Decision

**SPH production filament Biot at the Doc 47 §8 point is trusted against an independent filament reference.**  
**GetDP coil-only B remains discrepant: ~20% low on axis, and the ratio is not a constant scale — it worsens toward the coil.**  
Do **not** retune production `js0` to erase the axis number. Do **not** change thermal parameters.

## 2. SimpleTable re-export (Doc 48 open item)

`./run_coil_only_audit.sh` re-run with current `.pro` (`Format SimpleTable`):

```text

format_used=SimpleTable
|Bz|/filament = 0.799   (same magnitude as archived Table row)
```

Strict parser accepted the new row. F04 layout fix is confirmed end-to-end; the ~20% gap is **not** a leftover Table context-column bug.

## 3. Ampere-turn / Js audit (fine_large mesh)

| Integral | Value | Interpretation |
|---|---:|---|
| `Area_plain` (∑ coil rectangles) | 1.750×10⁻⁴ m² | = 7 × 4 × (2.5 mm)² ✓ |
| `I_plain = ∫ Js dA_plain` | 43.982 | = 7 × 2π × I with production `Js=2π I/A` |
| `Area_vol` (Jacobian `Vol`=VolAxiSqu) | 4.988×10⁻⁵ | ≈ R_c × Area_plain |
| `I_vol` | 12.535 | = 2π × 7 × I × R_c |

Diagnostic **Js factor sweep** (production `.pro` unchanged):

| Js expression | \|Bz_axis\|/filament |
|---|---:|
| `2π I/A` (production) | **0.799** |
| `I/A` (drop 2π) | 0.127 (=0.799/2π) |
| `I/(A R)` | 0.446 |
| `2π I/(A R)` | 2.80 |
| `I/(2π A)` | 0.020 |

Conclusion: the historical 2π VolAxiSqu S0 factor is still required; removing it collapses B by ~2π. It does **not** explain the residual ~20%.

## 4. Mesh refinement

| coil_fine | 1.0 mm | 0.7991 |

Discretization of the published coil section is **not** the lever.

<a id="50-getdp-b-root-cause-geo-js-2026-09-23md"></a>

# File: `50_GETDP_B_ROOT_CAUSE_GEO_JS_2026-09-23.md`

# Doc 50 — GetDP coil-only B root cause: Gmsh hole list + Js factor

Date: 2026-09-23  
Parents: Docs 47–49  
Artifacts: `docs/ophelie/reference/em_rz_getdp/work/coil_axis_b_audit_r08.json`, `work/batch_c_*doc50*`, `work_single/`

## 1. Decision

**The historical ~20% GetDP coil-only axis-|B| deficit is explained and repaired.**  
Two coupled mistakes:

1. **Gmsh air surface did not punch coil holes** when written as `Plane Surface(...) = {..., coil_loops}` — the list was not expanded. Correct form: `coil_loops[]`.
2. **`Js = 2π I / Area` was a false “VolAxiSqu” compensation** for that mesh bug. With correct holes, the MagDyn S0 source that matches filament Biot is **`Js = I / Area`**.

Production files updated. Do **not** restore the 2π factor. Signed `Bz_re` may still oppose Biot (phase/convention); magnitudes now agree.

## 2. Evidence chain

### 2.1 Single-loop isolate (`single_loop_axi.*`)

| Js | \|Bz_axis\| / filament |
|---|---:|
| `I/A` | **0.992** |
| `2π I/A` | **6.23 ≈ 2π** |

Single-turn proves the 2π factor is an over-amplification once the mesh is honest.

### 2.2 Multi-turn additivity (explicit holes)

With `Plane Surface = {1, 10, 11, …}` or `{1, coil_loops[]}` and `Js=I/A`:

| n_turns | ratio to n-filament |
|---:|---:|
| 1 | 0.992 |
| 2 | 0.991 |
| 3 | 0.991 |
| 7 | 0.991 |

### 2.3 The list-expansion bug

Same `.geo` For-loop building `coil_loops`, same `I_plain=7` and `Area_plain`:

| Air holes syntax | air tris | \|B\|/fil (`Js=I/A`) |
|---|---:|---:|
| `{1, coil_loops}` (broken) | 12056 | **0.128 ≈ 1/7** |
| `{1, coil_loops[]}` or explicit list | ~25800 | **0.991** |

Post-integrated ∫J still saw all turns; the FE field behaved like ~one turn. Multiplying Js by 2π then yielded ~2π/7 ≈ 0.90 on axis vs the 7-turn filament — the Doc 43/49 “~20% low” number.

### 2.4 Production French model after fix

`french_cylinder_axi.geo`: `Plane Surface(2) = {2, coil_loops[]};`  
`french_cylinder_eddy.pro`: `Js = I_peak / AreaCoil;`

Fresh `./run_coil_only_audit.sh`:

| Case | \|Bz\|/filament | rel_err |
|---|---:|---:|
| fine_large | **0.984** | 1.64% |
| fine_r2z | **0.994** | 0.60% |
| domain enlarge Δ\|Bz\| | +1.05% | (unchanged order) |

Midplane r∈[1e-4, 0.25] ratios after fix: **0.97–0.99** (was 0.80→0.40 falling toward the coil).

SPH production probe remains ~0.03% from independent filament (unchanged).

## 3. Claim table

| Claim | Status |

<a id="50-getdp-hole-js-root-cause-2026-09-23md"></a>

# File: `50_GETDP_HOLE_JS_ROOT_CAUSE_2026-09-23.md`

# Doc 50 — GetDP coil-only B root cause: Gmsh holes + Js factor

Date: 2026-09-23  
Parents: Docs 47–49  
Artifacts: `docs/ophelie/reference/em_rz_getdp/work/coil_axis_b_audit_r08.json`, `work_single/`, `work/batch_c_*doc50*`

## 1. Decision

**The historical ~20% GetDP coil-only axis-|B| deficit is explained and fixed.**  
Root cause was **not** SPH Biot, finite coil section, or a fundamental MagDyn curl bug.

1. **Gmsh air holes:** `Plane Surface(...) = {..., coil_loops}` does **not** expand the list. Must use `coil_loops[]`. Without `[]`, coil holes are incomplete; multi-turn FE B collapses by ~`n_turns` while `∫J` over Physical COILS still counts all turns.
2. **`Js = 2π I/A` was a false compensation** for that hole bug. With correct holes, the VolAxiSqu + `BF_PerpendicularEdge` S0 source that matches filament Biot is **`Js = I/A`**. Keeping 2π then makes single- and multi-turn B high by ~2π.

Production changes (reference GetDP only; SPH unchanged):

- `french_cylinder_axi.geo`: `Plane Surface(2) = {2, coil_loops[]}`
- `french_cylinder_eddy.pro`: `Js = I_peak / AreaCoil`

Do **not** treat this as permission to retune thermal/skull parameters. Eddy P/Q common-support vs SPH still needs a fresh compare under the fixed source.

## 2. Evidence chain

### 2.1 Single-loop isolate (`single_loop_axi.*`)

| Js | \|Bz_axis\| / filament |
|---|---:|
| `I/A` | **0.992** |
| `2π I/A` | **6.23 ≈ 2π** |

### 2.2 n-turn scan (explicit holes)

With `Js=I/A`, ratio stays **≈0.991** for n=1,2,3,7.  
With legacy `Js=2π I/A`, ratio stays **≈6.23**.

### 2.3 The list-expansion bug

Same `seven_loop` For-loop geo:

| Hole syntax | air tris | \|B\|/fil (`I/A`) |
|---|---:|---:|
| `{1, coil_loops}` | 12056 | **0.128 ≈ 1/7** |
| `{1, coil_loops[]}` or explicit `{1,10..16}` | ~25800 | **0.991** |

`I_plain` and `Area_plain` were identical in both cases — post-integrals lied about FE source completeness.

### 2.4 Fixed French coil-only audit

| Case | \|Bz\|/filament | rel_err |
|---|---:|---:|
| fine_large (pre Doc 50) | 0.799 | 0.201 |
| fine_large (Doc 50) | **0.984** | **0.016** |
| fine_r2z (Doc 50) | **0.994** | **0.006** |

Midplane (fine_large, Doc 50), \|GetDP/filament\|:

| r [m] | 1e-4 | 0.05 | 0.10 | 0.15 | 0.20 | 0.25 |
|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|---:|
| ratio | 0.984 | 0.983 | 0.984 | 0.985 | 0.987 | 0.965 |

Shape error of Doc 49 is largely gone in the interior. Signed `Bz_re` remains opposite to Biot/SPH (convention); magnitudes agree.

SPH production probe at the §8 point was already within ~0.03% of filament (Doc 48) — unchanged.

## 3. Claim table

| Claim | Status |
|---|---|
| Gmsh `coil_loops[]` hole fix | **implemented** |
| GetDP `Js = I/A` | **implemented** |
| Coil-only axis \|B\| vs filament ~1% | **exercised** (fine_large/r2z) |
| Midplane \|B\| shape vs filament | **exercised**; interior ~2% |
| Sign(GetDP Bz) vs Biot | **declared** (Doc 51; do not flip for agreement) |
| Conducting eddy P/Q vs SPH under fixed source | **exercised** (Doc 51: P≈0.69; midplane corr_Q≈0.97) |
| Literature thermal baseline | **not claimed** |

## 4. Next bounded steps

1. ~~Re-run azi-avg J/Q under fixed GetDP~~ → **Doc 51**.
2. ~~Document signed-B~~ → **Doc 51 §2**.
3. ~~Apportion absolute-P / Doc 47 alongside F03/F07~~ → **Doc 51 equal-power; Doc 52–53**. Freshness timestamps + F02 GPU twin still OPEN.
4. Continue to defer 1000 s / Picard-on production / skull retune / paper fitting.

Docs 43/49 treated “do not retune js0” under the **broken** hole geometry. That freeze correctly blocked curve-fitting. Doc 50 **replaces** the source definition because the geo bug is proven; archived pre-fix `work/archive_pre_doc50_geojs/` and Doc 49 numbers remain historical.
